# metaConvert 2.0.1 — pre-CRAN audit

**85 findings survived adversarial verification; 7 refuted.**

Method: 18 subsystem reviewers, each finding then attacked by **3 independent verifiers**
(numeric reproduction / documented intent / senior-statistician), every one instructed to
default to REFUTED. A finding is listed below only if fewer than half its verifiers refuted it.
**Coverage is uniform: every finding carries all 3 lenses.**

## Prompt to start a session on this

Copy the block below into a fresh agent session in this folder.

```text
Read AUDIT-2026-08-28-findings.md in full before doing anything. It is a pre-CRAN audit of
this package (metaConvert 2.0.1): 85 findings that each survived three independent adversarial
verifiers, deduplicated to 71 work items, with a sequenced fix plan.

Then work like this:

1. ENVIRONMENT FIRST. Follow "## Environment on a fresh machine". The R path in CLAUDE.md is
   wrong, tests skip ~1585 assertions silently without NOT_CRAN=true, and checks.R points at a
   stale v1.1.0 copy of the package. Fix checks.R's PKG_ROOT for this machine, then run all
   three suites and confirm you reproduce the stated baselines before you change any code:
     tests/testthat        3915 pass, 0 fail
     tests_save/checked    7198 pass, 2 error (both missing Suggests, not defects)
     simulations/tests      957 pass, 5 fail
   If you cannot reproduce these, stop and tell me — something differs about this machine.

2. WORK FROM "## Fix plan", NOT from the raw finding list. It merges 14 duplicates, sequences
   the rest into Phases 0-4, and lists 19 conflicts where applying two fixes naively breaks
   something. Ignoring it means doing work twice and introducing regressions.

3. ASK ME THE DECISIONS BEFORE CODING. Phase 1 is 14 author-only decisions (D1-D14). Put them
   to me as a numbered list with your recommendation for each, and wait. Do not guess any of
   them — above all the NEWS.md question, where the on-disk file is a 39-line stub and the full
   1347-line version is at `git show HEAD:NEWS.md`.

4. FOR EACH FINDING, IN THIS ORDER:
   a. Reproduce it. Several are already fixed in the working tree — the audit names at least
      one. If it does not reproduce, say so and move on; do not "fix" it.
   b. Read "Verifier correction" if the entry has one. It OVERRIDES the "Fix" field. The Fix
      field is a reviewer's proposal and was measured wrong or breaking in 9 cases — in one it
      was strictly worse than the current behaviour.
   c. Make the change, add or update a test, then re-run the affected suite.
   d. Check the risk table in "## Effort and risk" first — it names, per fix, which pinned
      tests will break. Expected breakage is not a regression, but rewrite those pins
      deliberately and tell me which you changed.

5. NEVER let a pass count fall without an explanation you can state in one sentence. A suite
   that goes green while testing less is the failure mode this whole audit exists to catch.

One unresolved item you will hit in Phase 2: the two competing designs for the
.count_decimals() rounding pad (max-precision rule vs a relative 0.25*(up-lo) cap) touch the
same lines and no verifier ruled between them. It is marked with a balance-scale symbol. Bring
it to me with a recommendation rather than picking silently.

Start now with Phase 0, then give me the Phase 1 decision list.
```

## Read this before acting

- The repo has ~40 uncommitted modified files under `R/`. Findings were verified against the
  **working tree**, but re-check each before editing — three reviewers were caught reporting
  defects already fixed on disk, and one finding was withdrawn on exactly that ground.
- `NEWS.md` on disk is a 39-line stub; the full 1347-line version is at `git show HEAD:NEWS.md`.
- Where a verifier corrected a finding, the correction is printed under **Verifier correction**.
  Trust it over the original claim — several proposed fixes were shown to be wrong.


## Environment on a fresh machine — read first

The folder is self-contained (transferred whole, so the **working tree** the findings were
verified against travels with it). Three environment facts are NOT in any finding below and
will cost you time if missed:

1. **R is 4.5.0, not 4.5.1.** `CLAUDE.md` documents the wrong path. Use:
   `"/c/Program Files/R/R-4.5.0/bin/x64/Rscript.exe"` (adjust on the new laptop).
2. **Set `NOT_CRAN=true` before any test run**, or ~1585 assertions skip silently and the
   suite goes green while testing far less. This is `checks.R`'s own "piège n°1".
3. **`checks.R` is broken** — see the finding on `PKG_ROOT`. Fix line 23 to point at the
   folder you are actually working in *before* using any of its helpers, or every release
   gate will validate a stale v1.1.0 copy and report a clean pass.

### The three test suites, and their state as of this audit

`devtools::test()` runs only the first. Each is a separate suite; the archived one has
previously caught a regression the main suite missed.

| suite | how to run | result at audit time |
|---|---|---|
| `tests/testthat` | `testthat::test_dir('tests/testthat', package='metaConvert')` | **3915 pass, 0 fail, 0 error** (baseline 3859) |
| `tests_save/checked` | `pkgload::load_all()` then `test_dir('tests_save/checked', package='metaConvert')` | **7198 pass, 0 fail, 2 error** (baseline 7202) |
| `simulations/tests` | `cd simulations && Rscript tests/run_tests.R` | **957 pass, 5 fail** |

Run all three after any fix. Use them as the acceptance test: a fix that moves the pass
count *down* without an explanation you can state has broken something.

### Findings the automated reviewers could not see

These came from running the suites, not from reading code, so they appear nowhere below:

- **The 2 archived errors are missing *Suggests*, not defects.** `tests_save/checked/test-ES-COR.R:223`
  calls `MetaUtility::r_to_d` and `test-ES-NNT.R:181` calls `epiR::epi.2by2`, and **neither file
  contains a single `skip_if_not_installed()`**. On a machine without those packages the block
  errors and abandons every assertion after it while still reporting `FAIL 0` — that is the whole
  −4 against the 7202 baseline. Fix by adding the two skips, or install the packages.
  **Interaction — read this before actioning the "9 of 16 Suggests are unused" finding:** that
  finding proposes dropping `epiR` and `MetaUtility` from Suggests. Doing so makes these two
  blocks error permanently. Add the skips first, or record the two suites' needs separately.
- **The shipped `tests/testthat` is clean of this problem.** Every Suggests call there is
  properly guarded; verified line by line. No CRAN noSuggests exposure from shipped tests.
- **`simulations/` has 5 failures (not shipped to CRAN, but two are substantive):**
  - Study 11's aggregates (`11e_floor_*`) are **stale** — produced by older `es_from_ALPHA.R`,
    `es_from_OMEGA.R`, `internal_guards.R`. The provenance check is correctly reporting it.
    This matters because `CLAUDE.md` cites those exact runs as the evidence for V44's
    `floor_position` bands and the alpha/omega closed-form calibration. Re-run study 11 to
    refresh, or stop citing the numbers as current.
  - `simulations/tests/test-allocation-scope.R` asserts `metafor::conv.2x2` "fails only on
    corrected tables" (integer-table failure rate 0). The real rate is 2.3–4.2%, and the cause
    is that **every failing integer table has OR = 1 exactly** — the reconstruction degenerates
    at the null, a second failure locus the README's "corrected share times a constant" story
    does not cover. **This does not reach users:** `metafor_conv2x2` is a simulation-only
    comparator, not a shipped `or_to_rr` value (the shipped set is `metaumbrella_cases`,
    `metaumbrella_exp`, `transpose`, `grant`, `dipietrantonj`). Fix the claim, not the package.

### Honest limits of this document

- **A "Fix" field is a proposal, not a verified patch.** Where a **Verifier correction** is
  printed, it overrides the original claim — in at least one case the proposed fix was shown to
  be *strictly worse* than the current behaviour. Read the correction before editing.
- **Some items are decisions, not defects** — the truncated `NEWS.md` above all. Nobody but you
  can say whether that was deliberate.
- Severity reflects consequence for a pooled estimate, not effort. Several `minor` items are
  one-line fixes; a few `major` ones need a re-derivation and a new test.
- Every finding was reproduced against the working tree, but **re-check before editing**:
  three reviewers in this audit were caught reporting defects already fixed on disk.

## Fix plan

**Read this before touching anything.** The 85 findings below are a list, not a plan. This section sequences them. It is written against the **working tree** (~40 uncommitted files under `R/`), which is what the findings were verified against.

Three standing rules, from six independent verification passes:

1. **A `Fix:` field is a proposal, not a verified patch.** Nine filed fixes were measured to be wrong or breaking (§Conflicts). Read past `Fix:` to `Verifier correction:` before implementing.
2. **Some findings are already repaired on disk.** At least one (`measure = "rd"` / E4) is an uncommitted `+` line in the working tree. Reproduce before fixing.
3. **Where two analysts disagreed, this plan says so** rather than silently picking. Those are marked ⚖.

Fix = copy the maintainer's own shipped wording from `R/es_from_stand_MD.R:142`. Do **not** delete the argument. **Three findings are wholly or partly already on disk.** Reproduce first: the E4 `measure %in% c("nnt","rd")` line (above); and the audit preamble names three more reported-but-repaired defects. Budget 20 minutes for a reproduction pass before Phase 2.

### Conflicts table

Pairs that must not both be applied naively.

| A | B | Resolution |
|---|---|---|
| **9 of the 16 declared Suggests are never referenced…** (drop epiR/MetaUtility) | unguarded `MetaUtility::`/`epiR::` at `tests_save/checked/test-ES-COR.R:223`, `test-ES-NNT.R:181` | **Sequence, don't choose.** Add `skip_if_not_installed()` to both blocks FIRST, then prune, recording tests_save deps in `Config/Needs/check`. **Second-order trap the audit does not state:** adding skips turns 2 errors into skips and the archived count stays ~7198 *forever* — it can only reach 7202 by *installing* the two packages. Either install them, or lower `checks.R:28` `archived` to 7198 with a written reason, or the plan's own "count went down = something broke" rule fires on a correct fix. |
| **V2/V3's rounding pad…** (max-precision rule) | **Rounding-aware V3 guard…** (relative `0.25 * (up - lo)` cap) | ⚖ Same lines, two incompatible designs, **no verifier adjudicated either** — the brief's premise that a verifier rejected one is wrong; I checked all 55 `Verifier correction` blocks. **Take the triplet-max-precision variant**: scale-free, repairs V2 *and* V3, needs no new constant. The relative cap cannot repair V2 at all (`up - lo` is negative on an inverted CI). Applying both compounds two shrink factors across a pad shared by V2/V3/V4/V28/V30/V33/V36. |
| **V40 tells the user an alpha of exactly 1…** (widen skip to `raw \|\| hakstian_whalen`) | **icc = 1 under icc_to_es = "raw" is dropped with no flag at all…** (narrow the skip, remove icc) | Same three lines (`internal_flags.R:655`), pushed in **opposite directions**. A's filed fix is refuted by its own verifier (it would silence V40 for *omega* under HW, where the row genuinely drops). B's option (a) is refuted too (`se = 0` aborts `rma()`). **Resolution: replace the scalar skip with a per-(measure, scale) "does this route actually drop the value?" table** — alpha keeps 1 on raw AND hakstian_whalen (`es_from_ALPHA.R:209-211`); omega on raw only (`es_from_OMEGA.R:279-283`); icc on **no** scale (`es_from_ICC.R:245`, scale-independent). Make the "log(0) is undefined" parenthetical scale-specific. One edit closes both plus the message half of the Inf-SE finding. |
| **V40 / alpha = 1** | **alpha = 1 with a reported natural-scale SE returns alpha_se = Inf…** | The Inf branch is gated on the same `valid_es`. If the boundary convention excludes alpha = 1 on non-raw scales, the Inf finding needs **no code at all**, only a test asserting NA. Decide the convention once; do not file two patches. |
| **compute_sem(): the same-sample chi-square variance/CI is valid only for a consistency ICC(3,1)…** | **compute_sdc(): rebuilds a symmetric Wald CI from sem_se…** | If the SEM fix returns `sem_se = NA` on the default agreement path, there is no interval left to rescale and **both documented chains print NA in a rendered vignette** (`Tutorial.Rmd:241-242`, `Psychometrics.Rmd:524-538`). **Take the SEM finding's minimal option** (@details + Rd caveat mirroring V31), keep the closed form computing by default. If the author wants the NA route it must be opt-in (the `icc_agreement_se` pattern) and `compute_sdc()` must keep a Wald fallback. |
| **The +0.5 continuity correction is fed to the tetrachoric route…** | **`.se_from_or()` returns NaN rather than NA…** | Measured: `.tet_r(0,20,10,10)` on **raw** cells returns `r = -1`, `r_ci_up = NaN`, `z = -Inf`, `z_se = Inf`. So the "two-line" continuity fix *manufactures* the NaN/Inf-in-an-exported-column class on every zero-cell row. **Guard `.tet_r()` for \|r\| ≥ 1 / non-finite `vz` FIRST**, or take the finding's documentation-only branch (legitimate — 1 of 3 verifiers refuted the framing) and the collision vanishes. |
| **es_from_phi()/es_from_chisq() silently replace the tetrachoric with phi…** | its own `Fix` clause "restrict fb_cols to the correlation columns (r/z)" | The restriction is unnecessary *and* breaking: `tests_save/checked/test-phi-chisq-fallback.R:10-22` pins `d` and `d_se` on the phi-only row. **Change the GATE only** (`rebuilt <- !is.na(cont_table$ai)`); leave `fb_cols` alone. Apply identically at `:242`. |
| **baseline_risk = 0 yields rd = 0 with rd_se = 0…** | its own `Fix` option 1 (narrow `.baseline_risk_or_na()` to `x <= 0`) | Refuted by a pinned test: `tests/testthat/test-baseline-risk-guard.R:81` asserts `0` is explicitly kept, and `:89-96` pins the rare-disease limit. **Option 2 only** — `.positive_or_na()` on the derived `rd_se`. Strike option 1 before handover. Also note the same finding appears in the REFUTED section arguing *no action*; that entry means "don't touch the point estimate", not "don't fix the SE". |
| **User-input ratio measures: log-scale SE + natural CI, no point estimate → arithmetic midpoint…** | `tests_save/checked/test-ES-USER-CONVERSION.R:1144-1157` | Filed fix 1 (drop `is.na(user_se_crude)` from the gate) breaks the pin *and* silently discards every user-reported log SE that arrives with a CI. **Take fix 2 only**: recompute `es_val` on the log scale for ES-missing ratio rows regardless of SE; keep `se_val` derivation gated. Mirror into `es_from_user_adj()` at `:828`. |
| **convert_df(): per-row method columns … silently ignored on the user-input route** | its own filed fix | Filed fix passes vectors into branches that index by `nn` but recycle by `length(nn)` — a **cross-row misalignment strictly worse than the defect**. Use `cor_to_smd[nn]`, `rep(smd_to_cor, length.out = len)`, `[idx]`. Low-risk alternative: a one-off message. |
| **User-input within-subject measures: 'dw'/'gw'/'mdw' silently drop from a g/d pool** | its own filed fix (route through `.es_from_d()`) | Refuted: applies `J(n1+n2-2)` where every paired kernel uses `J(n-1)` (0.99232 vs 0.98460 at n=50 — two corrections in one `gw` pool), and emits r/z/logOR from a paired SMD where those maps are undefined. Pinned as intentional at `test-ES-USER-CONVERSION.R:955-1013`. **Doc-only**, plus optionally a narrow d↔g step with `J(n-1)`. |
| **`md_se`/`mdw_se` built from signed SDs** (verifier: "do NOT use `.positive_or_na()` inside the variance") | the mdw MINOR's `Fix` (guard the SDs with `.positive_or_na()`) | Both are right about a *different predicate*. `.positive_or_na()` rejects zero, and the mean-change wrappers legitimately pass `mean_pre_sd = 0`. **Use the `>= 0` predicate the kernel already writes out with its rationale at `internal_multiple_formulas.R:1739-1746`; promote it to `.nonneg_or_na()` in `internal_guards.R`.** Then `.positive_or_na()` on the two **POST** SE slots only, `.ci_width()` on the CI routes. The `md_se` verifier's own literal prescription ("apply `.positive_or_na()` to the four `mean_*_se_*` arguments") is **unsafe as written** — `es_from_mean_change_se()` passes `mean_pre_se_exp = rep(0, n)`, and I reproduced the resulting all-NA mean-change family. |
| **convert_df() ignores a per-row `pre_post_to_smd` column** | the verbose coercion note sharing its block (`main_convert_df.R:868-891`) | A vector is never `identical()` to the length-1 argument, so the note would fire on every verbose run — including under `morris_dz`, where `test-prepost-fixes.R:299-312` asserts silence. **Rewrite the note in the same edit**, and fix `:415`'s `.r_scales_point` too. |
| **Eight Tier-1 messages carry no quoted column name…** (harden `.v_flag_matches_scope()`) | V38 / V39 / V43, whose messages quote a **value** first ('agreement', 'total', 'first_pc') | Hardening to "only quoted tokens that are real columns" makes those three hit the `length(m) == 0` early return and **start duplicating into `flags_adjusted`** — a new regression in the checks the finding names as beneficiaries. **If hardened, the fallback for "no column found" must be CRUDE, not TRUE.** Fix V31 by moving a quoted column to the front of its string. |
| **convert_df ranks the imputed-SE OR route above the p-value route** | its own follow-on (add `or_pval` to `.or_uncertainty_reported`) | The reorder alone is safe (no auto-selection is pinned). The follow-on breaks `test-or-imputation-not-duplicated.R:55`. **Reorder `or_list_L2` only**; update three comments; separately add the `or_pval <= 0 → NA` guard, which becomes live once the pval route ranks first. |
| **Adjusted-SMD hierarchy ranks the attenuating ancova_means_se/_ci above ancova_md_sd** | **The "Hierarchy" section of ?convert_df states an ordering the code does not implement** | The doc finding's "swap items 4 and 5" is only correct if the code fix is **declined**. Settle the code question first; then write the Rd from the list actually assembled at `main_convert_df.R:1576`. The RD half of the doc finding is independent and unblocked. |
| **The NEWS.md that will ship to CRAN deletes the "results change" warning** | `cran-comments.md` + `DESCRIPTION: Version: 2.0.1` | Not filed anywhere. HEAD's NEWS has **two** sections (860-line dev + 457-line 2.0.1); the stub collapsed both into 7 bullets that describe the **dev** work, while `cran-comments.md:1-15` submits 2.0.1 as an entirely different change set and claims "described in NEWS.md with before/after values" — false against the tree. Also, a wholesale `git checkout master -- NEWS.md` **ships a newly false sentence** (HEAD:93-96, "omega has no (n,k) sampling variance", falsified by `omega_se_source = "closed_form"`). **Restore *and correct* in one commit.** |
| **D3/V27 mixes two spread scales inside one md pool** | its own documented calibration | After the scale fix the motivating pilot case (41065428) measures **4.97 — under the K = 5 gate**, i.e. the fixed check fires on nothing in the pilot. **Re-derive `sd_outlier_ratio` and both CLAUDE.md numbers** against current extractions; do not trust the documented ones. |
| **es_formulas(): .parameters_for_measure() omits conversion parameters…** | **`R CMD check --as-cran` NOTE: six examples exceed 5 s; the test suite alone takes 32 minutes** | Each added parameter costs one full `convert_df()` re-run per option: +12 runs per ratio object, +12 per cor_like. Accept the widening (the per-row differ-filter makes over-inclusion false-positive-free) but **add no `es_formulas()` example on `df.haza`**, and re-time `test-es-formulas.R`. |

### Ordered work plan

#### Phase 0 — Unblock verification (do first; nothing else can be measured until these land)

Every one of these is mechanical. None changes package behaviour.

1. **`git add` the four untracked paths** + the `simulations/data/aggregated/11*.csv` glob: `tests/testthat/test-V44-floor-effect.R`, `tests/testthat/test-reliability-se-source.R`, `simulations/studies/11_reliability_se.R`, `simulations/tests/test-study-11-reliability-se.R`. Six findings name these as their regression tests and there is **no committed baseline to diff a botched fix against**. Use explicit paths — a blanket `git add .` would stage `AUDIT-2026-08-28-findings.md` and `simulations/app/app.backup-*`.
2. **`checks.R:23`** — `PKG_ROOT` points at a v1.1.0 checkout that does not exist on the target machine, so every helper errors. Repoint to a `rprojroot`/`getwd()`-derived path with a DESCRIPTION + Version assertion; fix the stale `setwd()` at `:4`.
3. **`checks.R:28` BASELINE** — all three constants are stale: `main = 3859L` (measured **3915**), `archived = 7202L` (measured **7198 + 2 errors**), `sims = 792L` (measured **957**). Fix in the same commit as step 1, with the reason written down.
4. **`skip_if_not_installed()`** at `tests_save/checked/test-ES-COR.R:223` (`MetaUtility`) and `test-ES-NNT.R:181` (`epiR`). Neither package is installed here, so `R CMD check` aborts at "checking package dependencies" before examples, tests or vignettes run. **`test-ES-NNT.R` is the archived pin for the NNT/RD arithmetic Phase 3 edits** — until the skips land, a regression from the `rd_se` work lands inside an abandoned tail and reports FAIL 0.
5. **Reproduction pass.** Confirm the E4 `measure %in% c("nnt","rd")` line is already on disk, and re-check the three defects the audit preamble flags as already repaired.

Answer these in writing before Phase 2. Each blocks ≥ 2 findings.

#### Phase 4 — Documentation, in one roxygen pass

Batch every roxygen edit and run `devtools::document()` **once** at the end.

**Regeneration is safe — this was measured, not assumed.** DESCRIPTION declares `Config/roxygen2/version: 8.0.0` against an installed roxygen2 **7.3.3**. A scratch-tree regeneration produced **114 of 115 Rd files byte-identical, NAMESPACE identical**; the only collateral is `RoxygenNote: 7.3.3` in DESCRIPTION and one duplicated author line dropped from `man/metaConvert-package.Rd:265`.

Contents: M1 (three `@param max_asymmetry` blocks), the two `baseline_risk` parentheticals (**do not** implement the derivation — the RD SE treats it as a known constant), M12 + the omega `\value` row + the rebuilt-Wald sentence, M3's `\usage` alignment, M13's "22 measures" claims (11, not 6 — and **do not** widen `see_input_data()`; `test-see-input-data.R:41-50` pins both error substrings), README 14 → 22 (edit `README.Rmd:30` and re-knit; README.md **is** shipped — only `^README\.Rmd$` is in `.Rbuildignore`), the Flora 0.5999 → 0.6079 correction in three places, the Heywood `@details` + the `internal_flags.R:517-525` comment that cites route behaviour its own action pre-empts, the `?convert_df` Hierarchy section (after D6), the `pool_sd` `\details` block (**derivation — the filed "d_ppc2 variance" wording is forbidden by its verifier**; the correct per-branch forms live only in `@noRd` comments), the paired-F/t halving `@note`, the omega `n_sample`/`n_items` contradiction, and CLAUDE.md's D1/D3-V27/V29/V38 sentences.

Then: **Psychometrics.Rmd in one editing pass and one re-render** (`:274`, `:337`, `:379`, the 0.67 → 0.654 mis-round at `:439`, the worked example at `:447-455`, the SEM/SDC chunk at `:519-538`), and **NEWS.md last of all**, per D1.

### How to verify a fix

Three suites, three baselines. Run all three; a fix is done when every one is at or above its baseline.

```bash
R="/c/Program Files/R/R-4.5.0/bin/x64/Rscript.exe"

# 1. Live suite — tests/testthat/            baseline: 3915 pass, 0 fail
"$R" -e "pkgload::load_all('.', quiet=TRUE); testthat::test_dir('tests/testthat')"

# 2. Archived pins — tests_save/checked/     baseline: 7198 pass + 2 errors
#    (the 2 errors are the unguarded epiR / MetaUtility calls; after Phase 0
#     step 4 they become skips and the count stays 7198 — it returns to 7202
#     only by INSTALLING those two packages. See D2.)
"$R" -e "pkgload::load_all('.', quiet=TRUE); testthat::test_dir('tests_save/checked')"

# 3. Simulations — simulations/tests/        baseline: 957 pass, 5 fails
#    (the 5 are pre-existing; do not treat them as your regression)
"$R" -e "pkgload::load_all('.', quiet=TRUE); testthat::test_dir('simulations/tests')"
```

Targeted single file: `"$R" -e "devtools::test_file('tests/testthat/test-input-guards.R')"`.

**The rule: a pass count that goes DOWN without a written explanation means something broke.** Not "the fix changed behaviour" — *broke*. Three legitimate reasons to move a baseline, and each must be recorded in the commit message:

1. **You deliberately re-pinned an archived assertion** (e.g. `compute_sdc`'s CI). Say which assertion, and why the old value was wrong.
2. **You added skips** (Phase 0 step 4). Errors become skips; the pass count does not rise.
3. **You added tests.** The count goes *up*; state by how many.

Anything else — investigate before committing. Note that `checks.R:28`'s three constants are stale in the repo today (3859 / 7202 / 792 against measured 3915 / 7198 / 957), so **fix them in Phase 0 or the harness will lie in both directions**.

Also re-run `R CMD check` under CRAN's `noSuggests` condition after any change touching the tetrachoric route (`es_from_2x2`, `es_from_phi`, `es_from_chisq`, `es_from_prop`) — this class has regressed twice:

```bash
_R_CHECK_DEPENDS_ONLY_=true "/c/Program Files/R/R-4.5.0/bin/x64/R.exe" \
  CMD check --no-manual metaConvert_*.tar.gz
```

## BLOCKER (1)

### OR→RR (metaumbrella_cases/_exp) discards the reported log-OR standard error whenever all four margins are known, so a non-significant OR can be returned as a significant RR
`R/internal_multiple_formulas.R:129` — subsystem `or-conversions` — refuted by **0 of 3** verifiers

**Claim.** This release added an exact-solve rung (`.solve_2x2_from_or`, reached from `.estimate_n_from_or_and_n_cases`/`_n_exp` when a second margin pair is available). The solved table is then handed to `es_from_2x2()`, and `logrr`, `logrr_se`, `logrr_ci_lo/up` are read off that CRUDE reconstructed table. The user's reported `logor_se` is not consulted at all — not even to rescale the variance. RR is a deterministic monotone function of (OR, margins), so the correct propagation is the delta method `SE(logRR) = |d logRR / d logOR| * logor_se`. Instead the route returns the sampling SE of the reconstructed counts, which is invariant to the reported precision. On master the enumerating search matched `var_sim` to the reported variance, so `logrr_se` at least tracked it; the exact solve removed that link. Point estimate improved (good); precision silently replaced.

**Evidence.** R> suppressMessages(devtools::load_all('.')) R> for (s in c(0.05,0.2581,1.50)) { r <- es_from_or_se(or=2.0, logor_se=s, n_exp=300,n_nexp=700, + n_cases=200,n_controls=800, or_to_rr="metaumbrella_cases"); cat(s, r$logrr, r$logrr_se,"\n") } se=0.0500 -> logrr=0.545017 logrr_se=0.125293 se=0.2581 -> logrr=0.545017 logrr_se=0.125293 se=1.5000 -> logrr=0.545017 logrr_se=0.125293 <-- frozen, 30x range in reported SE Same call on master (git worktree, commit 7b8eca7): se=0.0500 -> logrr=0.549168 logrr_se=0.126395 se=0.2581 -> logrr=0.580873 logrr_se=0.225033 se=1.5000 -> logrr=0.474503 logrr_se=1.324381 <-- tracked the reported SE Independently derived correct SE (numerical derivative of the exact solve, so it uses the SAME point estimate the package returns; margins n_exp=300 n_nexp=700 n_cases=200): d logRR / d logOR = 0.76307612 reported logor_se = 0.2580662 (from adjusted OR 2.0, 95% CI 1.2-3.3) => correct SE(logRR) = 0.19692415 ; package returns 0.12529347 (-36%) => inverse-variance weight inflated (0.19692/0.12529)^2 = 2.47x End-to-end through convert_df(), DEFAULT settings, summary(flags=TRUE): R> x$or<-2; x$logor_se<-c(0.05, 1.0); x$n_exp<-300; x$n_nexp<-700; x$n_cases<-200; x$n_controls<-800 R> summary(convert_df(x, measure="rr"), flags=TRUE) es_crude se_crude es_ci_lo_crude es_ci_up_crude info_used_crude 1 1.7246377 0.12529347 1.3491116 2.2046917 or_se 2 1.7246377 0.12529347 1.3491116 2.2046917 or_se flags: <-- EMPTY for both rows Row 2 reports OR = 2.0 with logor_se = 1.0, i.e. a 95% OR CI of (0.281, 14.22) that comfortably includes 1. metaConvert returns RR = 1.72, 95% CI (1.35, 2.20), p = 1.3e-5. The delta-method RR CI for that row is exp(0.5450 +- 1.96*0.7631) = (0.386, 7.69), which includes 1. A null result is converted into a significant one, with no flag.

**Impact.** Any meta-analysis that enters an OR (with its reported SE, CI or p-value) plus all four 2x2 margins and asks for measure = "rr"/"logrr" — the default `or_to_rr = "metaumbrella_cases"` — receives an RR standard error that reflects the crude margin-implied table rather than the study's own precision. The typical case where a user HAS all four margins and a reported CI is an adjusted OR from logistic regression, whose SE is larger than the crude one; the RR is then over-weighted (2.5x in the example above) and its CI too narrow. No Tier-1 or Tier-2 flag fires. Direction of the error is systematically anti-conservative, so it does not average out on pooling.

**Fix.** In the `metaumbrella_cases`/`metaumbrella_exp` branches of `.or_to_rr()`, keep the solved table for the POINT estimate only, and build `logrr_se` by propagating the supplied `logor_se`: compute `logRR(or)` from the solve at `or*exp(+/-h)` for small h to get `g = d logRR/d logOR` (or use the closed form), then set `logrr_se <- abs(g) * logor_se` and `logrr_ci <- logrr +/- qnorm(.975)*logrr_se`. Fall back to the reconstructed-table SE only when `logor_se` is NA (the `es_from_or()` imputed-SE route). At minimum, if the current behaviour is kept, state in `@details` that the returned RR precision is the crude-table precision and is independent of the reported OR precision, and add a Tier-2 flag when `logrr_se / logor_se` departs materially from the implied derivative.


## MAJOR (31)

### es_from_chisq()/es_from_chisq_pval() still switch correlation estimand silently — the 2.1.0 disclosure was added to es_from_phi() only
`R/es_from_PHI_CHISQ.R:243` — subsystem `2x2-core` — refuted by **0 of 3** verifiers

**Claim.** Commit 2b6f141 ("es_from_phi() no longer switches estimand silently") added a .notify_once() disclosure to the es_from_phi() fallback at R/es_from_PHI_CHISQ.R:126, but es_from_chisq() carries a byte-for-byte identical fallback at :243 with NO message at all. When some rows carry n_cases/n_exp and others do not, one call returns the TETRACHORIC correlation for the first group and the phi coefficient itself for the second — two different population quantities, 1.63x apart on the same input — both labelled info_used = "chisq", with no warning, no flag and no way for the user to tell the rows apart. D and G differ by 52% between the two regimes. es_from_chisq_pval() delegates to es_from_chisq() and inherits it. The Rd makes this worse rather than better: ?es_from_chisq @details states the value is converted "exactly as in es_from_phi() (including the correlation-based R/Z/D/G fallback ...)", which a user reads as including the disclosure that es_from_phi() now emits.

**Evidence.** $ Rscript -e '...' (devtools::load_all(".")) # es_from_phi, one call mixing both regimes -- messages captured via withCallingHandlers rm(list=ls(metaConvert:::.mcv_notices), envir=metaConvert:::.mcv_notices) p <- es_from_phi(phi=c(0.2182,0.2182), n_sample=c(200,200), n_cases=c(60,NA), n_exp=c(100,NA)) #> r: 0.3548452 0.2182 d: 0.5407595 0.3549433 #> n messages: 1 <- "...returned TWO DIFFERENT correlation estimands..." # es_from_chisq, IDENTICAL structure (chisq = n*phi^2 = 200*0.2182^2 = 9.52216) rm(list=ls(metaConvert:::.mcv_notices), envir=metaConvert:::.mcv_notices) q <- es_from_chisq(chisq=c(9.52216,9.52216), n_sample=c(200,200), n_cases=c(60,NA), n_exp=c(100,NA)) #> r: 0.3548452 0.2182 d: 0.5407595 0.3549433 #> n messages: 0 <- SILENT #> ratio r_row1/r_row2 = 1.626238 #> info_used: chisq (both rows) Independent check of which value is which: row 2's r is exactly the phi sqrt(chisq/n) = sqrt(9.52216/200) = 0.2182 (the Pearson r of the two binary indicators); row 1's r is metafor::escalc(measure="RTET") on the metafor::conv.2x2 reconstruction, i.e. the latent-normal correlation. The package's own test tests/testthat/test-table-2x2-to-cor.R:171-177 asserts these two regimes differ by >1.4x and is the reason the phi message exists.

**Impact.** Any review that extracts chi-square statistics — routine for older trial reports, where the 2x2 margins are often not printed — silently pools the tetrachoric correlation for the rows whose margins happened to be recorded with the phi coefficient for the rows whose margins were not. On the demonstrated inputs that is r = 0.355 pooled with r = 0.218 (and g = 0.541 with g = 0.355) for identical underlying data. phi is additionally margin-bounded, so the mixture also manufactures heterogeneity. Nothing in the output, the flags, or the console distinguishes the two.

**Fix.** Lift the disclosure block from R/es_from_PHI_CHISQ.R:126-143 into es_from_chisq() immediately after the `es[miss, fb_cols] <- pr[, fb_cols]` assignment at :255, with the message wording changed from 'phi coefficient' to 'phi = sqrt(chisq/n)'. Cleanest is to factor the fallback + notice into one internal helper (e.g. .phi_family_fallback(es, r_vec, n_sample, reverse, miss)) called from both entry points, so the two cannot drift again. Also give the chisq rows a Tier-2/cross-row flag rather than relying on a session-scoped console message.

### The +0.5 continuity correction is fed to the tetrachoric route, contradicting the documented "OR/RR only" and producing an SE 17x-27,000x too small on zero-cell tables
`R/es_from_2x2.R:120` — subsystem `2x2-core` — refuted by **1 of 3** verifiers

**Claim.** es_from_2x2() adds 0.5 to all four cells IN PLACE at :120-123 and then builds dat2x2 from the corrected cells at :156-161, so the tetrachoric solve at :178 receives the corrected table. Two documented statements are violated. (1) man/es_from_2x2.Rd:47-48 states 'if a cell is equal to 0, we applied the typical adjustment (add 0.5) to all cells. This adjustment is used for the OR/RR only; the RD and NNT are obtained from the raw cell counts' — R/Z also receive it, and the sentence tells the reader otherwise. (2) The same @details says the correlation route 'relied on the implementation of the formulas of the metafor package', and tests_save/checked/test-2x2.R pins yi/vi parity with metafor::escalc(measure="RTET") at 1e-10 — but metafor deliberately does NOT apply add/to to RTET, so that contract silently fails on exactly the zero-cell rows the pinned dataset does not contain. The consequence is not a cosmetic difference: metafor's ML on the raw table returns the boundary estimate with an enormous variance (its way of saying the correlation is not identified), whereas metaConvert returns an interior estimate with an ordinary-looking, finite SE. The reported SE is the sampling SE of the SHRUNK table and does not account for the shrinkage, so it is anti-conservative for the value actually reported. No flag fires by default: V8 (single zero cell) is gated behind enable_informational and its text mentions only OR/RR.

**Evidence.** $ Rscript (devtools::load_all("."); library(metafor)) for (t in list(c(0,20,10,10), c(0,50,8,42), c(3,47,0,50), c(0,30,20,10), c(12,0,5,45))) { m <- es_from_2x2(n_cases_exp=t[1], n_cases_nexp=t[3], n_controls_exp=t[2], n_controls_nexp=t[4]) e <- escalc(measure="RTET", ai=t[1], bi=t[2], ci=t[3], di=t[4]) } a/b/c/d mcv_r mfor_r mcv_se mfor_se se ratio 0/20/10/10 -0.8564 -1.0000 0.11347 149.029 1313.3 0/50/8/42 -0.6805 -1.0000 0.17063 4653.163 27270.3 3/47/0/50 0.4763 1.0000 0.26489 4843.165 18283.8 0/30/20/10 -0.9381 -1.0000 0.05067 17.028 336.1 12/0/5/45 0.9642 1.0000 0.03571 0.624 17.5 # metaConvert's value is exactly metafor's on the CORRECTED table, confirming the route: > escalc(measure="RTET", ai=0.5, bi=20.5, ci=10.5, di=10.5) yi vi 1 -0.8564 0.0129 # sqrt(0.0129) = 0.1135 = mcv_se > escalc(measure="RTET", ai=0, bi=20, ci=10, di=10) # what metafor itself returns yi vi 1 -1.0000 22209.7271 > escalc(measure="RTET", ai=0, bi=20, ci=10, di=10, to="all") # correction NOT applied 1 -1.0000 22209.7271 # inverse-variance weight for that one row: 1/0.11347^2 = 77.7 vs 1/22209.7 = 4.5e-05 # no default flag on such a row: dat <- data.frame(study_id=paste0("s",1:4), n_cases_exp=c(0,20,15,30), n_controls_exp=c(20,30,25,20), n_cases_nexp=c(10,10,12,18), n_controls_nexp=c(10,40,28,32)) summary(convert_df(dat, measure="r", verbose=FALSE), flags=TRUE)$flags_crude[1] #> "[DISCORDANT] CI width inconsistent with SE ...; [UNUSUAL] Direction outlier ..." #> -- nothing about the zero cell or the correction

**Impact.** A meta-analysis of correlations from 2x2 tables that contains any zero-cell study gives that study 17x to 27,000x more inverse-variance weight than any honest treatment of the same table would (77.7 vs 4.5e-05 on the worked example), while reporting a point estimate that is a shrinkage of the boundary ML value with no disclosure. Rare-outcome binary reviews — where zero cells are the norm — are precisely the case affected. The reader of ?es_from_2x2 is told the correction touches OR/RR only, so the behaviour is not discoverable from the documentation.

**Fix.** Two-line change plus a doc line: build dat2x2 (R/es_from_2x2.R:156-161) from the RAW cells (save copies before the :120-123 in-place correction), so the tetrachoric solve sees the same table metafor would. If the authors prefer to keep the corrected table because they judge the boundary estimate unusable, then (a) correct the @details sentence at man/es_from_2x2.Rd:47-48 to say the adjustment also feeds R/Z, (b) narrow the 'relied on metafor' sentence, and (c) make V8 fire by default for measure = 'r'/'z' with text naming the SE consequence, rather than leaving it behind enable_informational with OR/RR-only wording.

### es_from_phi()/es_from_chisq() silently replace the tetrachoric with phi (and overwrite correct D/G) whenever mvtnorm is absent — CRAN's own noSuggests flavour — and the new notice then states a false cause
`R/es_from_PHI_CHISQ.R:106` — subsystem `2x2-core` — refuted by **0 of 3** verifiers

**Claim.** The fallback gate is `miss <- which(is.na(es$r) & ...)` — it keys on the RESULT being NA, not on the margins being absent. es$r is also NA when the tetrachoric route is unavailable for an unrelated reason, the documented one being a missing 'mvtnorm' (a Suggests of metafor, absent on CRAN's noSuggests flavour that this repo's own .github/workflows/rhub.yaml runs). In that case the fallback fires even though n_cases and n_exp WERE supplied, and it overwrites d/g as well as r/z — even though d/g were computed correctly from the reconstructed table's log OR and never needed mvtnorm at all. The message that 2.1.0 added then asserts a cause that is false: 'n_cases / n_exp are missing, so the 2x2 table could not be reconstructed'. es_from_chisq() has the same gate at :243 with no message at all. The guard test tests/testthat/test-mvtnorm-guard.R:66-76 exercises this exact path but asserts only that the mvtnorm notice appears — it never checks what r, d or g become.

**Evidence.** $ Rscript (devtools::load_all(".")) ns <- asNamespace("metaConvert"); unlockBinding(".has_mvtnorm", ns) # --- WITH mvtnorm --- es_from_phi(phi=c(0.3,0.5), n_sample=c(120,200), n_cases=c(20,60), n_exp=c(40,100)) d d_se g r r_se z z_se logor 1 0.8896584 0.2867525 0.8839897 0.5170670 0.13866680 0.5723281 0.1892696 1.613662 2 1.4923556 0.2426814 1.4866944 0.7645119 0.06481828 1.0069845 0.1559926 2.706834 # --- WITHOUT mvtnorm (assign(".has_mvtnorm", function() FALSE, envir=ns)) --- d d_se g r r_se z z_se logor 1 0.4962261 0.1463696 0.4930643 0.3 0.08341956 0.3095196 0.09245003 1.613662 2 0.8734392 0.1104540 0.8701259 0.5 0.05316609 0.5493061 0.07124705 2.706834 messages emitted: "...requires the 'mvtnorm' package, which is not installed..." "es_from_phi(): n_cases / n_exp are missing, so the 2x2 table could not be reconstructed..." <-- FALSE: n_cases = c(20,60), n_exp = c(40,100) were supplied d falls 0.8896584 -> 0.4962261 (-44%) and r 0.5171 -> 0.3000 (= phi itself), while logor is unchanged at 1.613662 — proving the 2x2 WAS reconstructed and the d/g overwrite was gratuitous (d = logor*sqrt(3)/pi = 1.613662*0.5513 = 0.8897 is the value the row should keep).

**Impact.** On any installation without mvtnorm — which includes CRAN's noSuggests check flavour, and an ordinary `install.packages("metafor")` because mvtnorm is metafor's Suggests, not Imports — every phi- and chi-square-derived r, z, d and g in a convert_df() run changes estimand at once, by 44% on the demonstrated row, with no error, no NA, and a console message that misdiagnoses the cause. Users comparing results across machines will see the numbers move and be told the margins are missing.

**Fix.** Gate the fallback on the CAUSE, not on the symptom: compute `rebuilt <- !is.na(cont_table$ai)` and use `miss <- which(!rebuilt & ...)`, so a row whose table was reconstructed never falls back. Restrict fb_cols to the correlation columns (r/z) — d/g on a reconstructed row already come from the log OR and must not be overwritten. When !rebuilt is FALSE but es$r is NA (mvtnorm absent, or a solver failure), leave r/z as NA and let the existing mvtnorm notice stand alone. Apply the same change at R/es_from_PHI_CHISQ.R:243 for es_from_chisq(). Add an assertion to tests/testthat/test-mvtnorm-guard.R that d is unchanged by the mock.

### `small_margin_prop` is range-checked nowhere: an out-of-range value silently flips the sign of the Bonett correlation and returns a negative standard error
`R/internal_multiple_formulas.R:1099` — subsystem `or-conversions` — refuted by **1 of 3** verifiers

**Claim.** Bonett & Price's coefficient is `c = (1 - |p1+ - p+1|/5 - (0.5 - pmin)^2)/2`, which is guaranteed to lie in [0.275, 0.5] ONLY when `pmin` is a genuine smallest marginal proportion in (0, 0.5]. `small_margin_prop` is a user-entered column (documented as "a proportion in (0, 0.5]") and is validated nowhere: it is absent from `.positive_columns()` and from `.bounded_columns()` in R/internal_flags.R (its direct sibling `baseline_risk` IS bounded [0,1] there, with the comment "must be a proportion (0-1), not a percentage"), and `es_from_or_se()` applies no guard — the derivation block at R/es_from_stand_OR.R:436 explicitly lets a user-supplied value win untouched. A value > 0.5 shrinks c; a negative value or a percentage makes c negative, which reverses the monotonicity of `r = cos(pi/(1+or^c))` and makes `r_se = logor_se*(pi*c*or^c)*sin(...)/(1+or^c)^2` negative.

**Evidence.** R> suppressMessages(devtools::load_all('.')) # reference row: or=3, logor_se=0.2, n_exp=40, n_nexp=60, n_cases=30, n_controls=70, n_sample=100 # correct pmin = min(40,60,30,70)/100 = 0.30 R> es_from_or_se(or=3, logor_se=0.2, n_exp=40,n_nexp=60,n_cases=30,n_controls=70,n_sample=100, + or_to_cor="bonett")[c("r","r_se")] r = 0.38643417 r_se = 0.063747999 <-- correct (derived pmin) R> es_from_or_se(or=3, logor_se=0.2, small_margin_prop=30, n_exp=40,n_cases=30,n_sample=100, + or_to_cor="bonett")[c("r","r_se","z","z_se")] r = -1 r_se = -1.4148309e-221 z = -Inf z_se = NaN Through convert_df() with the DEFAULT summary() (flags = FALSE): R> x$or<-3; x$logor_se<-0.2; x$n_exp<-40; x$n_nexp<-60; x$n_cases<-30; x$n_controls<-70 R> x$n_sample<-100; x$small_margin_prop <- c(NA, 0.9, 30) R> summary(convert_df(x, measure="r")) es_crude se_crude es_ci_lo_crude es_ci_up_crude 1 0.38643417 6.3747999e-02 0.25559988 0.50448421 <- correct 2 0.34092940 5.7574225e-02 0.22404454 0.44895219 <- 11.8% low, NO flag at all 3 -1.00000000 -1.4148309e-221 -1.00000000 -1.00000000 <- sign reversed, NEGATIVE SE Row 3 (`small_margin_prop` entered as a percentage, 30 instead of 0.30) reports a correlation of -1 for an odds ratio of 3, with a standard error of -1.4e-221, i.e. an inverse-variance weight of ~5e442. Row 2 (`small_margin_prop = 0.9`, the largest margin entered instead of the smallest) is the silent case: no flag fires in any tier. Hand-check of row 3: c = (1 - |0.4-0.3|/5 - (0.5-30)^2)/2 = -434.6, or^c ~ 0, r = cos(pi/1) = -1.

**Impact.** A user who mis-reads the parameter (enters the largest rather than the smallest margin, or a percentage) gets a silently wrong correlation. In the mild case (0.9) the error is ~12% in r with no diagnostic at all. In the percentage case the row carries a sign-reversed r = -1 with a near-zero NEGATIVE standard error, which in `rma()` becomes an essentially infinite weight and dominates the whole pool. `summary()` only surfaces it when the user opts into `flags = TRUE`; the default output is silent.

**Fix.** Add `list(col = "small_margin_prop", lo = 0, up = 0.5, label = "smallest margin proportion")` to `.bounded_columns()` in R/internal_flags.R (so convert_df sets it to NA under `correct_inputs = TRUE`), and add an unconditional guard in `.or_to_cor()`'s bonett branch — e.g. `if (!is.na(small_margin_prop) && (small_margin_prop <= 0 || small_margin_prop > 0.5)) small_margin_prop <- NA_real_` — so the 89 directly-callable exported routes are covered too, per the stated policy in R/internal_guards.R. Belt-and-braces: clamp `c` to (0, 0.5] before use.

### convert_df ranks the imputed-SE OR route above the p-value route, contradicting the note added in this release, and gives the study ~2.2x too little weight
`R/main_convert_df.R:1446` — subsystem `or-conversions` — refuted by **0 of 3** verifiers

**Claim.** `or_list_L2` orders `es_odds_ratio` (whose SE is IMPUTED by `.se_from_or()` from the margins) ABOVE `es_odds_ratio_pval` (whose SE is recovered exactly from the reported p-value: se = |logOR / qnorm(p/2)|). `.or_uncertainty_reported` deliberately excludes `or_pval` from the suppression on the stated ground that "on a pval-only row the marginal reconstruction is the better source". That claim is refuted by the @note this same release added to `es_from_or()`, which measures the imputed SE at ~1.4x too wide, size-dependent, and says: "prefer es_from_or_se(), es_from_or_ci() or es_from_or_pval() whenever the source reports a standard error, a confidence interval OR A P-VALUE". The pipeline does the opposite of its own documentation.

**Evidence.** R> suppressMessages(devtools::load_all('.')) # true 2x2 = (40,160,20,180) => OR = 2.25, exact SE(logOR) = 0.29462783 R> a<-40;b<-160;cc<-20;d<-180 R> or <- (a*d)/(b*cc); se <- sqrt(1/a+1/b+1/cc+1/d) R> pv <- 2*pnorm(abs(log(or)/se), lower.tail=FALSE) # 0.005907... R> x$or<-or; x$or_pval<-pv; x$n_cases<-60; x$n_controls<-340 R> summary(convert_df(x, measure="logor")) selected: or es = 0.81093022 se = 0.43902895 (ratio to true SE: 1.4901137) R> es_from_or(or=or, n_cases=60, n_controls=340)$logor_se [1] 0.43902895 ratio to truth 1.4901137 <- selected by the hierarchy R> es_from_or_pval(or=or, or_pval=pv, n_cases=60, n_controls=340)$logor_se [1] 0.29462783 ratio to truth 1.0000000 <- available, ranked below weight ratio (pval route / or route) = (0.43903/0.29463)^2 = 2.2204387 The 1.49x measured here matches the package's own @note ("median ratio ... about 1.4"), so the note and the hierarchy are describing the same quantity and disagreeing about it. The ordering is unchanged from master, so this is pre-existing, but the note asserting the opposite is new in 2.0.1.

**Impact.** Every row that reports an OR with a p-value and case/control margins — a very common reporting pattern — is pooled with a standard error ~1.4-2x too wide, i.e. roughly half its correct inverse-variance weight. Because the inflation grows with study size (per the package's own note), the distortion is not uniform across studies and therefore shifts the pooled point estimate, not just its width. `info_used` says "or", so a careful user can notice, but nothing warns.

**Fix.** Reorder `or_list_L2` to `list(es_odds_ratio_se, es_odds_ratio_ci, es_odds_ratio_pval, es_odds_ratio, es_logreg_t)`. This does NOT break tests/testthat/test-or-imputation-not-duplicated.R, which pins only that the imputation still RUNS on a p-value row (`.imputation_ran`), not that it is selected. Update the comment at R/main_convert_df.R:1441-1445 and the one at R/main_convert_df.R:679-681 accordingly. If the ordering is kept deliberately (e.g. because a p-value rounded to "<0.001" gives se = 0), guard that case explicitly (`or_pval <= 0` -> NA) rather than demoting the whole route.

### measure = "rd" silently pools incidence rate differences (per person-time, unit-dependent) with risk differences; the mixing flag exists only for measure = "nnt"
`R/internal_flags.R:3753` — subsystem `rr-rd-nnt` — refuted by **0 of 3** verifiers

**Claim.** `es_from_cases_time()` writes the incidence rate difference `IRD = baseline_rate * (1 - IRR)` into the `rd`/`rd_se`/`rd_ci_*` columns (R/es_from_stand_IRR.R:84-96), and `IRR` is an ACTIVE tier of the `measure = "rd"` hierarchy (R/main_convert_df.R:1659-1660). An IRD is an events-per-person-time rate, not a probability: its numeric value depends entirely on the time unit the extractor transcribed, and it is unbounded. The cross-row unit-mixing flag that exists for exactly this hazard (E4) is gated on `measure == "nnt"` (R/internal_flags.R:3753), so for `measure = "rd"` the pool is mixed with NO flag at all. Worse, the flag that DOES fire on a large IRD -- B6/B6b `[INVALID] RD outside [-1, 1]` (R/internal_flags.R:2523, 2529) -- is itself wrong: an IRD of 3 events/person-year is perfectly legitimate.

**Evidence.** R (Rscript 4.5.0, devtools::load_all('.')): # study A: 2x2 counts (a true risk difference); study B: person-time counts df <- data.frame(study_id=c("A","B"), n_cases_exp=c(40,50), n_controls_exp=c(60,NA), n_cases_nexp=c(55,100), n_controls_nexp=c(45,NA), time_exp=c(NA,1000), time_nexp=c(NA,1000)) d <- as.data.frame(summary(convert_df(df, measure="rd", verbose=FALSE), flags=TRUE)) OUTPUT: info_used_crude es_crude se_crude es_ci_lo_crude es_ci_up_crude 1 2x2 0.15 0.06982120 0.01315296 0.28684704 2 cases_time 0.05 0.01224745 0.02599544 0.07400456 FLAGS: [1] "" "" <-- no flag on either row # identical study B, time recorded in person-MONTHS instead of person-years (x12): df2 <- df; df2$time_exp[2] <- 12000; df2$time_nexp[2] <- 12000 info_used_crude es_crude se_crude 1 2x2 0.150000000 0.069821200 2 cases_time 0.004166667 0.001020621 The SAME study contributes rd = 0.05 or rd = 0.00417 to the same pool purely by choice of time unit, and its inverse-variance weight relative to study A is (0.0698/0.01225)^2 = 32x in the first case and (0.0698/0.001021)^2 = 4679x in the second. No flag either time. Large-IRD case (B6 false [INVALID]): n_cases_exp=30,time_exp=10, n_cases_nexp=60,time_nexp=10 info_used_crude es_crude se_crude 2 cases_time 3 0.9486833 FLAGS[2]: "[INVALID] RD outside [-1, 1]: rd = 3 (from cases_time); [INVALID] RD CI bound outside [-1, 1]: CI lower = 1.141, CI upper = 4.859 (from cases_time)" IRD = 6/py - 3/py = 3 events per person-year -- arithmetically valid, wrongly [INVALID].

**Impact.** Any `measure = "rd"` review that contains at least one person-time study gets a pooled estimate that mixes probabilities with rates. Because the IRD SE is on a different scale, the person-time row typically dominates the inverse-variance weight (32x-4700x in the demo above), so the pooled RD is driven by a quantity that is not an RD at all -- and the answer changes by whatever factor the extractor's time unit implies. Nothing warns the user. Conversely a legitimately large IRD is stamped `[INVALID]`, prompting users to delete correct data.

**Fix.** Two independent one-liners: (1) generalise E4 (R/internal_flags.R:3753) from `measure == "nnt"` to `measure %in% c("nnt", "rd")`, adapting the message to "risk difference vs incidence rate difference"; the rest of the block is already measure-agnostic. (2) Skip B6/B6b (R/internal_flags.R:2523-2545) when `info_used[i] == "cases_time"`, since [-1, 1] is not the parameter space of an IRD. Longer term, consider giving the IRD its own output columns (`ird*`) instead of reusing `rd*`, or excluding IRR from the `measure = "rd"` hierarchy the way `RD_stand` is excluded from `measure = "r"`.

### B5 "minimum possible NNT" applies the benefit-side bound 1/baseline_risk to NNT-to-harm; the package flags its own self-consistent 2x2 output as [INVALID]
`R/internal_flags.R:2504` — subsystem `rr-rd-nnt` — refuted by **0 of 3** verifiers

**Claim.** B5 computes `min_nnt <- 1 / br` and tests `abs(es[i]) < min_nnt`, i.e. it applies the same floor to both signs of the NNT. With `baseline_risk = BR` fixed and the treated risk p_t in [0,1], the risk difference RD = BR - p_t ranges over [BR - 1, BR]. The benefit side is bounded by BR, so |NNT| >= 1/BR is right there; but the HARM side is bounded by 1 - BR, so the correct floor for a negative NNT is 1/(1 - BR), which is a completely different number for small BR. The same defect is in the person-time branch (line 2513, `min_nnt_pt <- 1 / brate`): on the harm side the incidence rate ratio is unbounded above, so |IRD| is unbounded and a rate-based NNH has NO positive lower bound at all.

**Evidence.** R (Rscript 4.5.0, devtools::load_all('.')): # a harmful exposure, fully self-consistent 2x2: control 5/100, exposed 90/100 df <- data.frame(study_id="A", n_cases_exp=90, n_controls_exp=10, n_cases_nexp=5, n_controls_nexp=95, baseline_risk=0.05) d <- as.data.frame(summary(convert_df(df, measure="nnt", verbose=FALSE), flags=TRUE)) OUTPUT: info_used_crude es_crude se_crude 1 2x2 -1.176471 0.05132317 FLAG: "[INVALID] Minimum possible NNT = 20 (baseline_risk = 5%) - reported |NNT| = 1.176 (from 2x2)" Independent derivation: RD = 0.05 - 0.90 = -0.85, NNH = 1/(-0.85) = -1.176471 (exactly what the package itself computed from the counts). The largest attainable |RD| on the harm side is 1 - BR = 0.95, so the smallest attainable |NNH| is 1/0.95 = 1.052632. 1.176 > 1.053, so the value is ATTAINABLE -- and demonstrably so, since a valid 2x2 table produced it. The flag's own bound, 1/0.05 = 20, is the benefit-side bound and is irrelevant here. Person-time branch, same defect: df2 <- data.frame(study_id="B", n_cases_exp=500, n_cases_nexp=10, time_exp=100, time_nexp=100, baseline_rate=0.1) info_used_crude es_crude se_crude 1 cases_time -0.2040816 0.06650862 FLAG: "[INVALID] NNT magnitude < 1: NNT = -0.204 (from cases_time); [INVALID] Minimum possible person-time NNT = 10 (baseline_rate = 0.1) - reported |NNT| = 0.204 (from cases_time)" IRR = 50, IRD = 0.1*(1-50) = -4.9 /py, NNT = -0.204 py. IRR is unbounded above, so no floor exists on the harm side; both [INVALID] verdicts are false.

**Impact.** Every NNT-to-harm row whose |NNH| falls between 1/(1-BR) and 1/BR is stamped `[INVALID]` -- the tag CLAUDE.md reserves for "mathematically impossible". For low-baseline-risk outcomes (BR = 0.05 gives a false window of 1.05 to 20) that is essentially every appreciable harm signal, i.e. exactly the safety-outcome rows a reviewer must not discard. Users following the flag's own wording will delete correct data or chase a non-existent extraction error.

**Fix.** In R/internal_flags.R, make B5 sign-aware: min_nnt <- if (es[i] > 0) 1 / br else 1 / (1 - br) and restrict the person-time branch to the benefit side only: if (!is.na(brate) && brate > 0 && es[i] > 0) { ... 1 / brate ... } The pinned test tests_save/checked/test-ES-NNT-RD.R:375 only asserts that a flags column exists, and tests/testthat/test-flag-delimiter.R:63 uses a positive NNT (|NNT| = 2 vs floor 5), so both keep passing.

### mdw standard error / CI in the single-group pre-post route bypasses the dispersion guards: a negative reported SD produces a finite, positive, wrong SE (up to ~3x the correct meta-analytic weight)
`R/es_from_PAIRED_SINGLE_GROUP.R:146` — subsystem `prepost-single-group` — refuted by **0 of 3** verifiers

**Claim.** `es_from_means_sd_pre_post_single_group()` computes the within-group MD variance inline at lines 146-150 as `(mean_pre_sd_exp^2 + mean_sd_exp^2 - 2*r*mean_pre_sd_exp*mean_sd_exp)/n_exp`, using the *raw* argument values. Every other consumer of these SDs goes through `.single_group_pre_post_to_smd()`, which NAs a negative SD precisely because "a negative raw SD flips the sign of the -2*r*sd_pre*sd_post cross term in sd_diff, yielding a wrong-but-finite standardizer" (R/internal_multiple_formulas.R:1578-1583). The `mdw` arm of the same function never received that guard. Consequence: on a row with a negative `mean_pre_sd_exp` (or `mean_sd_exp`), `dw`/`gw` are correctly NA'd by the kernel while `mdw`, `mdw_se`, `mdw_ci_lo`, `mdw_ci_up` are all emitted, with the cross term sign-flipped. The SE is positive and plausible, so nothing downstream catches it: internal_guards' own sweep (tests/testthat/test-input-guards.R) checks only sign flips of `d`/`logor`/`logrr`/`rd`/`md` (its `prim()` helper never looks at `mdw`), negative SEs, and transposed output CIs — a wrong-but-positive `mdw_se` passes all three. With a POSITIVE r_pre_post the error is conservative (SE too large); with a NEGATIVE r_pre_post it is anti-conservative. The same route also feeds `es_from_means_se_pre_post_single_group()` (negative SE -> negative SD) and `es_from_means_ci_pre_post_single_group()`.

**Evidence.** R> suppressMessages(devtools::load_all('.', quiet=TRUE)) R> good <- es_from_means_sd_pre_post_single_group(50, 55, 10, 11, 40, -0.5) R> bad <- es_from_means_sd_pre_post_single_group(50, 55, -10, 11, 40, -0.5) R> cat("correct (sd_pre=+10): mdw =", good$mdw, " mdw_se =", good$mdw_se, " ci=[", good$mdw_ci_lo, ",", good$mdw_ci_up, "]\n") correct (sd_pre = +10): mdw = 5 mdw_se = 2.87663 ci=[ -0.8185333 , 10.81853 ] R> cat("corrupt (sd_pre=-10): mdw =", bad$mdw, " mdw_se =", bad$mdw_se, " ci=[", bad$mdw_ci_lo, ",", bad$mdw_ci_up, "] d =", bad$d, "\n") corrupt (sd_pre = -10): mdw = 5 mdw_se = 1.665833 ci=[ 1.630534 , 8.369466 ] d = NA Independent derivation of the correct value (definition of Var(post-pre)): R> sqrt((100+121-2*(-0.5)*10*11)/40) # correct, |sd_pre| = 10 [1] 2.87663 R> sqrt((100+121-2*(-0.5)*(-10)*11)/40) # what the package returns [1] 1.665833 R> (good$mdw_se/bad$mdw_se)^2 # inverse-variance weight ratio [1] 2.981982 Note the CI: the correct interval (-0.819, 10.819) covers 0; the emitted one (1.631, 8.369) does not — the row flips from non-significant to significant. Reachable through the pipeline under correct_inputs = FALSE (a documented, supported mode): R> s <- summary(convert_df(x, measure="mdw", verbose=FALSE, correct_inputs=FALSE), flags=TRUE) study_id es_crude se_crude info_used_crude flags_crude 1 bad 5 1.665833 means_sd_pre_post_single_group [INVALID] Negative input: 'mean_pre_sd_exp' = -10; ... 2 good 5 2.876630 means_sd_pre_post_single_group ... (x = two rows, mean_pre_exp=50, mean_exp=55, mean_pre_sd_exp = -10 / +10, mean_sd_exp=11, n_exp=40, r_pre_post_exp=-0.5) On a DIRECT call — the metaumbrella path, which internal_guards.R explicitly says validation never reaches — there is no flag at all. Existing guard sweep does not catch it (it passes today): R> test_file('tests/testthat/test-input-guards.R') -> 230/230 passed, 0 failed

**Impact.** Any meta-analysis of within-group mean differences (measure = "mdw") in which one study's SD was transcribed with a sign error, or an SE/CI was entered reversed, receives a study whose weight is up to ~3x too large (anti-conservative) or ~3x too small (conservative), depending on the sign of r_pre_post, plus a CI that can flip the study's apparent significance. The row looks half-healthy — dw/gw are NA, mdw is populated — which is exactly the pattern a user would read as "only the SMD is unavailable here". On direct API calls (metaumbrella, one-off use) there is no flag whatsoever.

**Fix.** Guard the two SDs before the mdw variance, mirroring the kernel: ```r .sd_pre_g <- .positive_or_na(mean_pre_sd_exp[nn_miss]) # or: allow 0 for the mean_change wrappers .sd_post_g <- .positive_or_na(mean_sd_exp[nn_miss]) mdw_var <- (.sd_pre_g^2 + .sd_post_g^2 - 2*r_pre_post_exp[nn_miss]*.sd_pre_g*.sd_post_g)/n_exp[nn_miss] ``` The mean_change wrappers legitimately pass `mean_pre_sd_exp = 0`, so use the kernel's own predicate (`>= 0`, NA otherwise) rather than `.positive_or_na()` for the pre slot. Then extend tests/testthat/test-input-guards.R's `prim()`/`check()` to compare the SE *magnitude* against the |SD| run, not only its sign, and add `mdw` to `prim()`.

### convert_df() ignores a per-row `pre_post_to_smd` column for the 13 mean-change and paired-t/F routes, silently substituting a different estimand
`R/main_convert_df.R:857` — subsystem `prepost-kernel` — refuted by **0 of 3** verifiers

**Claim.** `pre_post_to_smd` is one of the nine conversion parameters that convert_df() deliberately reads from a per-row column (the loop at R/main_convert_df.R:443-463 merges the column with the scalar argument, and errors with "should be specified in a column of the dataset" if the argument is not length 1). Every route that receives the parameter inside `with(x, ...)` therefore gets the per-row value. But `pre_post_to_smd_restricted` (line 857) is computed OUTSIDE `with()`, from the length-1 formal argument: `if (pre_post_to_smd %in% c("bonett","morris_dav")) "cooper" else pre_post_to_smd`. That scalar is then passed to all 8 mean-change routes (lines 900, 910, 922, 933, 968, 976, 985, 993) and all 5 paired-t/F routes (line 1044 onwards). So for those 13 routes the column is discarded entirely and every row gets whichever method the *scalar* implies — by default "bonett", coerced to "cooper"/morris_drm. The exported routes themselves handle the vector correctly (es_from_paired_t() delegates per row through .paired_t_to_smd()); the loss happens only at the convert_df() call site. The bug is pre-existing (master:R/main_convert_df.R:712) but sits squarely in the pre/post family this release refactored, and it is silent: the only message printed names 'bonett', a value the user never wrote.

**Evidence.** ### package output (R 4.5.0, devtools::load_all('.')) dat <- data.frame(study_id=c("A","B"), paired_t_exp=3.5, paired_t_nexp=1.2, n_exp=30, n_nexp=32, r_pre_post_exp=0.6, r_pre_post_nexp=0.6, pre_post_to_smd=c("morris_dz","morris_drm")) summary(convert_df(dat, measure="g", verbose=FALSE, split_adjusted=FALSE)) convert_df with per-row pre_post_to_smd column c('morris_dz','morris_drm'): row 1 es=0.37151329 se=0.23406050 info=paired_t row 2 es=0.37151329 se=0.23406050 info=paired_t <- both rows morris_drm ### the same rows through the exported route, which DOES honour the column es_from_paired_t(3.5, 1.2, 30, 32, 0.6, 0.6, pre_post_to_smd=c("morris_dz","morris_drm")) row 1 g=0.41536449 g_se=0.26778189 <- morris_dz, what row 1 asked for row 2 g=0.37151329 g_se=0.23406050 Scalar reference runs: morris_dz g=0.41536449 g_se=0.26778189 morris_drm g=0.37151329 g_se=0.23406050 => row 1 is wrong by 0.0439 in g (-10.6%) and by 0.0337 in g_se (-12.6%). ### same for the mean-change family dat2: mean_change_exp=5, mean_change_sd_exp=8, mean_change_nexp=2, mean_change_sd_nexp=7, n_exp=30, n_nexp=32, r=0.6, pre_post_to_smd=c("morris_dz","morris_drm") convert_df: row 1 es=0.29510348 se=0.23451268 row 2 es=0.29510348 se=0.23451268 es_from_mean_change_sd (honours the column): row 1 g=0.32993572 g_se=0.26827596 row 2 g=0.29510348 g_se=0.23451268 ### the only diagnostic emitted (verbose=TRUE), naming a value the user never set "Note: pre_post_to_smd = 'bonett' requires separate pre/post SDs, which mean-change and paired t/F data do not carry. ... Supply pre/post SDs to use 'bonett' throughout."

**Impact.** A reviewer who mixes designs in one sheet — the documented way to specify a per-study conversion formula — gets d_rm where they asked for d_z (or vice versa) on every mean-change and paired-t/F row, with no warning that names the column. d_z and d_rm are different estimands (d_rm = d_z * sqrt(2(1-r))): at r = 0.6 the point estimate is off by ~11% and the SE by ~13%, and the discrepancy grows without bound as r -> 1. Nothing downstream can detect it: info_used still reads 'paired_t', and the row is internally consistent.

**Fix.** Resolve the restriction per row instead of from the scalar. Replace line 857-861 with a vectorised form over the merged column, e.g. `pre_post_to_smd_restricted <- ifelse(x[, "pre_post_to_smd"] %in% c("bonett", "morris_dav"), "cooper", x[, "pre_post_to_smd"])`, and make the verbose note report the set of coerced values actually present rather than the scalar argument. All 13 receiving routes already accept one method per row (es_from_paired_t -> .paired_t_to_smd, es_from_mean_change_* -> mapply over the kernel), so no route change is needed. Add a regression test asserting that a two-row dataset with pre_post_to_smd = c("morris_dz","morris_drm") reproduces the two scalar runs.

### es_formulas(): .parameters_for_measure() omits conversion parameters that demonstrably move the estimate, producing false all-clears
`R/es_formulas.R:68` — subsystem `prepost-kernel` — refuted by **0 of 3** verifiers

**Claim.** `es_formulas()` documents its default as "evaluates every conversion parameter that can influence the requested effect size measure". `.parameters_for_measure()` does not do that. For the `ratio` class (logor/logrr/logirr/loghr/nnt/rd) it lists only or_to_rr, rr_to_or, or_to_cor, smd_to_cor — but a correlation or an SMD reaches an odds ratio through `cor_to_smd` and `pre_post_to_smd`, both of which change the OR. For the `cor_like` class (r/z/rp/zp) it omits `smd_denom` and `rr_to_or`, both of which change r. When the omitted parameter is the only one that matters, es_formulas() returns 0 rows and prints "No conversion formula altered any effect size in this dataset." — an explicit all-clear that is false.

**Evidence.** ### case 1: measure = "or" from a pearson_r; cor_to_smd is NOT in .parameters_for_measure("logor") dat <- data.frame(study_id="A", pearson_r=0.45, n_sample=120, n_exp=60, n_nexp=60) cor_to_smd=viechtbauer es=4.07343642244653 se=0.26194018418813 info=pearson_r cor_to_smd=cooper es=6.22117518089525 se=0.372374912726401 info=pearson_r cor_to_smd=mathur es=NA => the OR moves 4.073 -> 6.221 (+52.7%), the SE 0.262 -> 0.372 (+42%). es_formulas(convert_df(dat, measure="or", ...)) returned 0 rows. metaConvert:::.parameters_for_measure("logor") = or_to_rr, rr_to_or, or_to_cor, smd_to_cor ### case 2: measure = "or" from a paired t; pre_post_to_smd is NOT in the ratio list d1 <- data.frame(study_id="A", paired_t_exp=3.5, paired_t_nexp=1.2, n_exp=30, n_nexp=32, r_pre_post_exp=0.6, r_pre_post_nexp=0.6) pre_post_to_smd=morris_drm es=1.998763 se=0.4355899 info=paired_t pre_post_to_smd=morris_dz es=2.169009 se=0.4983450 info=paired_t => OR moves 1.999 -> 2.169 (+8.5%), SE 0.436 -> 0.498 (+14%). es_formulas(...) returned 0 row(s). # explicit "nothing altered any effect size" ### case 3: measure = "r" from means/SDs; smd_denom is NOT in .parameters_for_measure("r") d3 <- data.frame(study_id="A", mean_exp=25, mean_sd_exp=7, mean_nexp=22, mean_sd_nexp=4, n_exp=40, n_nexp=45) smd_denom=pooled es=0.3269834 se=0.1252518 smd_denom=glass es=0.4444447 se=0.1187107 smd_denom=glass_robust es=0.4444447 se=0.1711986 => r moves 0.327 -> 0.444 (+35.9%), SE 0.125 -> 0.171 (+37%). metaConvert:::.parameters_for_measure("r") = smd_to_cor, or_to_cor, cor_to_smd, smd_var, pre_post_to_smd es_formulas() reported 4 rows (smd_to_cor x2, smd_var x2) and silently left out the parameter with by far the largest influence.

**Impact.** es_formulas() is sold as the structural-uncertainty audit for a review. A user who runs it on an OR pool built from correlations or paired t-tests, or on an r pool built from means and SDs, is told either that no conversion choice matters or that only the small ones do. They then report a single point estimate as robust when the analyst's choice moves it by 8.5% (paired-t -> OR), 36% (smd_denom -> r) or 53% (cor_to_smd -> OR). The failure mode is worse than an omission because the function prints an affirmative all-clear.

**Fix.** Extend `.parameters_for_measure()`: add "cor_to_smd", "pre_post_to_smd", "smd_denom" and "smd_var" to the `ratio` branch, and "smd_denom", "or_to_rr", "rr_to_or" to the `cor_like` branch. The per-row filter already downstream (a parameter is reported only when the estimates actually differ) makes over-inclusion free of false positives, so the safe default is to iterate the full option set unless a parameter is provably unreachable. Add a test that each of the three datasets above yields a non-empty es_formulas() table.

### es_formulas() cannot see omega_to_es at all and returns an explicit all-clear for measure = "omega"
`R/es_formulas.R:37` — subsystem `prepost-kernel` — refuted by **1 of 3** verifiers

**Claim.** `omega_to_es` is a convert_df() argument with three options ("bonett", "raw", "hakstian_whalen", validated at R/es_from_OMEGA.R:254) and it is already recorded in `attr(res, "conversion_args")` (R/main_convert_df.R:1846) precisely so es_formulas() can re-run it. But it is absent from `.formula_parameters()`, from `.scale_changing_parameters()`, from `.parameters_for_measure()` (which has no `measure == "omega"` branch at all) and from the Rd option table. The consequence is that `es_formulas(obj)` on an omega object hits `length(parameters) == 0` and messages "No conversion formula can influence the 'omega' measure, so no alternative was evaluated", and `es_formulas(obj, parameters = "omega_to_es")` errors with "Unknown conversion parameter". This release added "hakstian_whalen" to `alpha_to_es` in the same table (the only edit to R/es_formulas.R in the diff) without adding omega's mirror argument.

**Evidence.** dat <- data.frame(study_id="s1", omega=0.86, omega_ci_lo=0.80, omega_ci_up=0.91, n_sample=200, n_items=10) o <- convert_df(dat, measure="omega", verbose=FALSE, split_adjusted=FALSE) es_formulas(o, verbose=TRUE) message: "No conversion formula can influence the 'omega' measure, so no alternative was evaluated." nrow(result) = 0 # but omega_to_es changes the analysed quantity completely: omega_to_es=bonett es=-1.96611285637283 se=0.203704686034105 omega_to_es=raw es= 0.86 se=0.028061740130856 omega_to_es=hakstian_whalen es= 0.48075058981489 se=0.0348636694512852 # the mirror argument IS covered, proving the omission is an oversight not a design choice: dat2 <- data.frame(study_id="s1", cronbach_alpha=0.86, n_sample=200, n_items=10) es_formulas(convert_df(dat2, measure="alpha", ...)) -> 3 rows parameter formula es se 1 alpha_to_es bonett -1.966 0.106 2 alpha_to_es hakstian_whalen 0.481 0.018 3 alpha_to_es raw 0.860 0.015 # and es_formulas(o, parameters = "omega_to_es") errors: # "Unknown conversion parameter(s): omega_to_es."

**Impact.** A reliability-generalisation review of McDonald's omega — the workstream this release is built around — gets a categorical statement that no formula choice can affect its results, when the choice moves the analysed value across three incompatible scales (-1.966 / 0.860 / 0.481) and by a factor of 7 in the SE. There is also no escape hatch: naming the parameter explicitly is rejected as unknown.

**Fix.** Add `omega_to_es = c("bonett", "raw", "hakstian_whalen")` to `.formula_parameters()`, add "omega_to_es" to `.scale_changing_parameters()` (it changes the analysis scale exactly as alpha_to_es does, so `deviation`/`spread` must stay NA), add `else if (measure == "omega") k <- "omega_to_es"` to `.parameters_for_measure()`, and add the row to the Rd \tabular at R/es_formulas.R:200-208. Mirror the existing alpha test in tests/testthat/test-es-formulas.R ("scale-changing parameters report values but no range").

### es_formulas() drops alpha_se_source / omega_se_source / icc_agreement_se, reinstating standard errors the analysis deliberately refused
`R/main_convert_df.R:1840` — subsystem `prepost-kernel` — refuted by **0 of 3** verifiers

**Claim.** `attr(res, "conversion_args")` records 20 convert_df() arguments but omits the three that govern where a reliability SE comes from: `alpha_se_source`, `omega_se_source` and `icc_agreement_se`. `.run_with_parameter()` rebuilds the call from that list (`args <- cargs[intersect(names(cargs), names(formals(convert_df)))]`), so every re-evaluation silently reverts them to their defaults ("closed_form", "reported", "compute"). A user who set `alpha_se_source = "reported"` or `icc_agreement_se = "drop"` — the documented honest options, whose whole purpose is to leave a row unpooled — gets an es_formulas() table full of the closed-form SEs they refused, and effect sizes for rows whose analysis value is NA.

**Evidence.** dat <- data.frame(study_id=c("P1","P2"), cronbach_alpha=c(0.85,0.90), n_sample=200, n_items=10) o1 <- convert_df(dat, measure="alpha", alpha_se_source="reported", verbose=FALSE, split_adjusted=FALSE) summary(o1): row 1 es=NA se=NA row 2 es=NA se=NA <- the analysis the user actually ran "alpha_se_source" %in% names(attr(o1,"conversion_args")) -> FALSE es_formulas(o1, verbose=FALSE): row_id parameter formula es se 1 1 alpha_to_es bonett -1.897 0.106 2 1 alpha_to_es hakstian_whalen 0.469 0.019 3 1 alpha_to_es raw 0.850 0.016 4 2 alpha_to_es bonett -2.303 0.106 5 2 alpha_to_es hakstian_whalen 0.536 0.016 6 2 alpha_to_es raw 0.900 0.011 Same for the ICC: d2 <- data.frame(study_id=c("I1","I2"), icc=c(0.80,0.75), n_sample=100, n_measurements=3) o2 <- convert_df(d2, measure="icc", icc_agreement_se="drop", ...) summary(o2): row 1 es=NA se=NA ; row 2 es=NA se=NA es_formulas(o2): row_id parameter formula es se 1 1 icc_to_es bonett -1.609 0.151 2 1 icc_to_es raw 0.800 0.030 3 2 icc_to_es bonett -1.386 0.145 4 2 icc_to_es raw 0.750 0.036

**Impact.** The sensitivity table contradicts the object it claims to describe: it prints six alpha estimates with SEs for a run that produced two NAs, and four ICC estimates with the anti-conservative agreement-type SE (coverage measured at 0.82 down to 0.14 in ?es_from_icc) that the user explicitly asked to drop. A reviewer reading the table would conclude those rows are poolable and could copy the SEs into a meta-analysis, giving each an inverse-variance weight the analysis had deliberately withheld.

**Fix.** Add `alpha_se_source = alpha_se_source, omega_se_source = omega_se_source, icc_agreement_se = icc_agreement_se` to the `conversion_args` list at R/main_convert_df.R:1840-1849. `.run_with_parameter()` needs no change — it already forwards every recorded name that is a convert_df() formal. Add a test asserting that es_formulas() on an object built with alpha_se_source = "reported" returns NA standard errors (or no rows) for rows with no reported SE.

### Rounding-aware V3 guard is defeated by trailing zeros, so a reported alpha CI that does not contain the reported alpha silently sets alpha_se (up to 30x weight error)
`R/internal_flags.R:254` — subsystem `alpha` — refuted by **0 of 3** verifiers

**Claim.** `.count_decimals()` infers "reported precision" from a double with `format(x, drop0trailing = TRUE)`, so a bound reported as 0.90 is read as 1 decimal and a bound reported as 0.900 as 1 decimal too. V3 ("Value outside CI") builds its tolerance as `pad_lo <- 0.5*10^-dp(val) + 0.5*10^-dp(lo)` (internal_flags.R:838), so a bound whose last printed digit is 0 inflates the pad from 0.010 to 0.055 (or 0.100 for two 1-dp bounds) and the check goes silent. Detection is therefore non-monotone in the severity of the inconsistency: alpha = 0.85 with CI [0.89, 0.95] is caught, while the *worse* [0.90, 0.95] and [0.90, 0.99] are not. This helper is shared by V2/V3/V4/V28-V30/V33, but the consequence is NEW in this release for alpha: es_from_ALPHA.R:227-232 now derives `alpha_se` from the reported CI in preference to the closed form, so an unflagged, self-contradictory interval silently determines the study's meta-analytic weight. The route itself has no point-estimate-vs-CI consistency guard at all, so direct callers (and metaumbrella) get no protection even in principle.

**Evidence.** $ Rscript (R 4.5.0, devtools::load_all('.')) # 1. the helper cannot see a trailing zero .count_decimals(0.9) = 1 ; .count_decimals(0.90) = 1 ; .count_decimals(0.95) = 2 .count_decimals(0.8) = 1 ; .count_decimals(0.85) = 2 ; .count_decimals(0.850)= 2 # 2. V3's tolerance for val=0.85, lo=0.90 pad_lo = 0.5*10^-2 + 0.5*10^-1 = 0.055 -> fires only if 0.85 < 0.845 (never) # 3. Tier-1 on a single alpha row, alpha = 0.85, varying the reported CI # (V3 detected via capture.output(..., type="message")) alpha=0.85, CI [0.9 , 0.95] : dp(lo)=1 dp(up)=2 -> V3 SILENT | alpha_se from CI = 0.17683 alpha=0.85, CI [0.91, 0.96] : dp(lo)=2 dp(up)=2 -> V3 FIRES | alpha_se from CI = 0.20687 alpha=0.85, CI [0.89, 0.95] : dp(lo)=2 dp(up)=2 -> V3 FIRES | alpha_se from CI = 0.20114 alpha=0.85, CI [0.9 , 0.99] : dp(lo)=1 dp(up)=2 -> V3 SILENT | alpha_se from CI = 0.58740 alpha=0.85, CI [0.8 , 0.9 ] : dp(lo)=1 dp(up)=1 -> V3 SILENT | alpha_se from CI = 0.17683 alpha=0.85, CI [0.92, 0.97] : dp(lo)=2 dp(up)=2 -> V3 FIRES | alpha_se from CI = 0.25022 closed-form alpha_se (n=200, k=10) = 0.10594 # 4. the route uses the contradictory CI, with no guard of its own > es_from_cronbach_alpha(0.85, 200, 10, cronbach_alpha_ci_lo=0.90, cronbach_alpha_ci_up=0.95) alpha alpha_se alpha_ci_lo alpha_ci_up bad_CI -1.89712 0.17682651 -2.2436936 -1.5505464 closed_form -1.89712 0.10594029 -2.1047591 -1.6894808 SE ratio = 1.6691 weight ratio = 0.3589 # and 0.58740/0.10594 = 5.54x SE, 0.033x weight for [0.90,0.99] # 5. end-to-end through convert_df(), correct_inputs = TRUE (default), flags = TRUE # d: s2 = alpha .85, n 200, k 10, CI [0.90, 0.95] study_id es_crude se_crude info_used_crude 2 s2 -1.89712 0.1768265 cronbach_alpha flags_crude[2] = "[INFO] Asymmetric CI for 'cronbach_alpha' (>50% asymmetry); [INFO] Same 'cronbach_alpha' ..." # -> no [INVALID] Value-outside-CI flag anywhere; the CI still sets the weight. # 6. control showing it is the helper, not the alpha wiring: identical numbers in an # md triplet on the same row are equally missed > x <- data.frame(study_id="s1", cronbach_alpha=0.85, n_sample=200, n_items=10, + cronbach_alpha_ci_lo=0.90, cronbach_alpha_ci_up=0.95, + md=0.85, md_ci_lo=0.90, md_ci_up=0.95) > .validate_input_data(x) Input data validation: Row 1: [INFO] Asymmetric CI for 'md' (>10% asymmetry); [INFO] Asymmetric CI for 'cronbach_alpha' (>10% asymmetry)

**Impact.** A reliability-generalization reviewer who mistypes or mis-pastes a CI bound (or pastes the neighbouring row's interval) gets no [INVALID] flag whenever a bound ends in 0 - roughly one interval in five when bounds are reported to 2 dp, and essentially always when a paper prints .80/.90 style bounds. The contradictory interval then overrides the closed form and fixes the study's inverse-variance weight: 1.67x the SE (0.36x weight) in the mild demo above, 5.5x the SE (0.033x weight) for [0.90, 0.99]. Because es_from_cronbach_alpha() has no internal consistency check, direct callers and metaumbrella have no protection at all. The failure mode is new in this release - before it, alpha had no CI columns and no CI-derived SE.

**Fix.** Do not let a rounding allowance swallow a meaningful share of the interval.
