Minimal, scale-free fix at internal_flags.R:836-841: cap the pad, e.g. `pad_lo <- min(pad_lo, 0.25 * (up[i] - lo[i]))` (and likewise pad_up), so the tolerance can never exceed a quarter of the reported half-width; [0.90, 0.95] then caps at 0.0125 and fires while a genuine 1-dp-vs-2-dp rounding case still passes. Better still, capture reported precision from the raw character strings in `.check_data()` (which sees them before numeric coercion) and store it, instead of re-deriving it from the double in `.count_decimals()`. Independently, add a cheap route-level guard in `es_from_cronbach_alpha()` (and the matching `es_from_omega`/`es_from_icc` routes): if the reported point estimate lies outside [ci_lo, ci_up], refuse the CI-derived SE rather than using it.

### compute_sem(): a length-1 `icc_se` (or any argument shorter than `sd`/`icc`) broadcasts row 1's SE and CI onto every row, producing CIs that do not contain their own point estimate
`R/psychometric_utils.R:207` — subsystem `psychometric-utils` — refuted by **0 of 3** verifiers

**Claim.** `var_sem` and the CI are built with nested `base::ifelse()` calls whose *test* is a short vector. `ifelse()` returns an object the length of `test`, and the inner `ifelse(!is.na(icc_se), var_sem_delta, var_sem_exact)` has `test = !is.na(icc_se)`. When `icc_se` is supplied as a scalar (the documented "external ICC estimate" regime), that inner call collapses to length 1; the outer `ifelse` then recycles it with `rep(..., length.out = n)`, so every row receives ROW 1's variance. `same_sample <- is.na(icc_se)` is likewise length 1, so `sem_ci_lo`/`sem_ci_up` are length 1 and are silently recycled by `data.frame()`. The function explicitly recycles `n_measurements` (line 153) but never `icc_se`, `n_sample` or `sd` — exactly the class of defect CLAUDE.md records as blocking-fix #1 for `es_from_cronbach_alpha()`/`es_from_icc()`, left unfixed here. The same collapse also occurs in the *same-sample* regime whenever `length(sd) < length(icc)`, because `icc_se <- rep(NA_real_, length(sd))` inherits `sd`'s length.

**Evidence.** $ Rscript -e 'devtools::load_all("."); compute_sem(sd=c(10,20,5), icc=c(0.85,0.60,0.90), n_sample=100, icc_se=0.03)' sem sem_se sem_ci_lo sem_ci_up 1 3.872983 0.4751395 2.941727 4.80424 2 12.649111 0.4751395 2.941727 4.80424 <- WRONG (and CI excludes sem) 3 1.581139 0.4751395 2.941727 4.80424 <- WRONG Hand oracle (the file's own documented delta formula, line 190-191: Var = SD^2/(4(1-ICC))*icc_se^2 + (1-ICC)*SD^2/(2(n-1))): row 1: sem=3.872983 se=0.475140 ci=[2.941727, 4.804240] (matches) row 2: sem=12.649111 se=1.016406 ci=[10.656992, 14.641229] (package gave 0.475 / [2.94,4.80]) row 3: sem=1.581139 se=0.262443 ci=[1.066760, 2.095517] (package gave 0.475 / [2.94,4.80]) Supplying the SAME scalar as a length-3 vector gives the correct answers, proving the defect is purely the recycling, not the formula: $ compute_sem(sd=c(10,20,5), icc=c(.85,.6,.9), n_sample=100, icc_se=c(0.03,0.03,0.03)) sem sem_se sem_ci_lo sem_ci_up 1 3.872983 0.4751395 2.941727 4.804240 2 12.649111 1.0164058 10.656992 14.641229 3 1.581139 0.2624429 1.066760 2.095517 Same-sample regime, sd scalar + icc vector (no icc_se at all): $ compute_sem(sd=10, icc=c(.85,.6,.9), n_sample=100) sem sem_se sem_ci_lo sem_ci_up 1 3.872983 0.2752409 3.400506 4.499149 2 6.324555 0.2752409 3.400506 4.499149 <- oracle se 0.4494666, ci [5.553003, 7.71...] 3 3.162278 0.2752409 3.400506 4.499149 <- oracle se 0.2247333, ci [2.776501, ...] Row 2's reported 95% CI [3.40, 4.50] does not contain its own SEM of 6.32. Partial-length recycling is also wrong (icc_se length 2, 4 rows): rows 3/4 receive rows 1/2's SE and CI. The equal-length vector call (the shape used in vignettes/Psychometrics.Rmd) is CORRECT, which is why no pinned test catches this — tests_save/checked/test-psychometric-utils*.R never calls compute_sem() with unequal argument lengths (73/73 assertions pass).

**Impact.** Any user who applies one externally-reported ICC standard error (e.g. from a single validation study) across a set of studies with differing SDs/ICCs gets every row's SEM standard error and 95% CI silently replaced by row 1's. In the example above row 2's SE is 2.1x too small and its CI is disjoint from its own point estimate. Because compute_sdc() consumes sem_se, the SDC SE and CI inherit the same corruption. Nothing warns; the output looks like a well-formed data.frame.

**Fix.** Recycle all length-1 arguments to length(sd) up front (the `es_from_pearson_r()` idiom already used for `n_measurements` at line 153), and error on incompatible lengths: `n <- max(lengths(list(sd, icc, n_sample, icc_se, n_measurements)))`, then for each argument `if (length(a) == 1L) a <- rep(a, n) else if (length(a) != n) stop(...)`. Alternatively replace the nested `ifelse()` chain with index assignment on a pre-allocated `var_sem <- rep(NA_real_, n)`, which cannot silently recycle. Add a regression test with `sd` length 3 and a scalar `icc_se`.

### compute_sem(): the same-sample chi-square variance/CI is valid only for a consistency ICC(3,1); under the package's default absolute-agreement ICC(2,1) the SE is up to 2.7x too small and coverage collapses to 51%, worsening as n grows
`R/psychometric_utils.R:203` — subsystem `psychometric-utils` — refuted by **0 of 3** verifiers

**Claim.** `Var(SEM) = SEM^2 / (2(n-1)(k-1))` and the exact chi-square CI are derived from `SEM = sqrt(MSE)`, i.e. they assume all of the non-subject variance is residual error with (n-1)(k-1) df. That identity holds for ICC(3,1) (consistency), where `SD^2 (1 - ICC) = MSE`. It does NOT hold for ICC(2,1) (absolute agreement) — the estimand `es_from_icc()` returns by default and the type the docs of that function name as the default. There `SD^2 (1 - ICC(2,1)) = sigma_r^2 + sigma_e^2`, and the sampling variance of that quantity is dominated by MSC, which carries only k-1 degrees of freedom. The stated df is therefore far too large, the SE far too small, and — exactly as for the documented V31 flag on `es_from_icc()` — the failure gets WORSE with n, because the reported SE shrinks like 1/sqrt(n) while the true sampling SD does not. Neither `?compute_sem` nor `man/compute_sem.Rd` mentions ICC type at all (grep for 'agreement|consistency|ICC\\(2|ICC\\(3|rater variance' returns nothing), while the @details assert the variance is 'the exact sampling variance' and the source comment claims 'Verified by simulation to within about 1%'.

**Evidence.** Monte Carlo, two-way random-effects DGP y_ij = mu + s_i + r_j + e_ij, ANOVA-estimated ICC and observed SD fed to compute_sem(); truth for the agreement estimand is sqrt(sigma_r^2 + sigma_e^2), for consistency it is sigma_e. 3000-4000 reps/cell, set.seed(99). se_ratio = mean(reported sem_se) / empirical sd(sem). n k sigma_r type coverage emp_sd reported_se se_ratio bias 1 30 2 0.000 agreement 0.9570 0.0491 0.0500 1.018 0.9825 2 30 2 0.224 agreement 0.7988 0.0784 0.0480 0.612 0.9438 3 100 2 0.224 agreement 0.5100 0.0721 0.0265 0.367 0.9611 4 30 3 0.387 agreement 0.5475 0.1143 0.0478 0.418 0.9401 5 30 2 0.224 consistency 0.9485 0.0412 0.0412 0.998 0.9913 6 100 2 0.224 consistency 0.9565 0.0222 0.0224 1.009 0.9971 Rows 5-6 confirm the formula is exactly right for the consistency case (se_ratio 0.998/1.009, coverage 0.949/0.957). Rows 2-4 show the agreement case: with only 5% of the total variance due to a systematic occasion/rater effect (sigma_r^2 = 0.05 of 1.0), the reported SE is 0.61x the truth at n=30 and 0.37x at n=100, and nominal-95% CI coverage falls to 0.799 and 0.510. Row 1 (sigma_r = 0) reproduces the documented ~1% agreement, so the claim in the source comment is true only for that special case. A separate one-way MC (set.seed(20260828)) confirms the point estimate and CI are fine when there is no systematic occasion effect: coverage 0.961/0.949/0.954/0.948 at (n,k) = (10,2),(25,2),(50,3),(100,2).

**Impact.** `es_from_icc()` defaults to `icc_type = 'agreement'` and `compute_sem()` defaults to `n_measurements = 2` (test-retest), where a systematic practice/occasion effect is the norm rather than the exception. A COSMIN reviewer following the documented chain therefore reports an SEM 95% CI with real coverage near 50-80% instead of 95%, and (via compute_sdc) an equally over-precise SDC interval. The direction is anti-conservative — measurement error is presented as better pinned down than it is — and it degrades with sample size, so larger reliability studies look most trustworthy while being most wrong.

**Fix.** Two options. Minimal and CRAN-safe: add the ICC-type caveat to @details and the Rd — state that the closed-form variance/CI is exact only for a consistency ICC(3,1) and is anti-conservative for an absolute-agreement ICC(2,1) with non-negligible systematic rater/occasion variance, quoting the measured coverage (0.80 at n=30, 0.51 at n=100 for sigma_r^2 = 5% of total), mirroring the V31 note already carried by `es_from_icc()`. Better: add an `icc_type` argument (defaulting to 'agreement' for consistency with `es_from_icc()`) and, for the agreement case, either return `sem_se = NA` with a pointer (the `icc_agreement_se = 'drop'` pattern already in the package) or widen the df. Do not leave the @details asserting 'exact' unqualified.

### compute_sdc(): rebuilds a symmetric Wald CI from sem_se, discarding the exact chi-square CI compute_sem() just computed — the exact undercoverage the SEM fix was made to remove
`R/psychometric_utils.R:276` — subsystem `psychometric-utils` — refuted by **0 of 3** verifiers

**Claim.** SDC = c * SEM with c = 1.96*sqrt(2) a known constant, so the correct SDC interval is simply c times the SEM interval — a monotone rescaling that preserves whatever coverage the SEM interval had. `compute_sdc()` instead discards the interval and reconstructs `sdc +/- 1.96 * sdc_se`. In the same-sample regime `compute_sem()` deliberately abandoned that Wald form: its own @details say 'the symmetric Wald interval undercovers at small df (89.7% instead of 95% at n = 10, k = 2)', and `tests_save/checked/test-psychometric-utils-fixes.R` pins the exact chi-square interval under fix P19. The documented chain `compute_sem() -> compute_sdc()` (in `?compute_sdc`, `vignettes/Tutorial.Rmd:241` and `vignettes/Psychometrics.Rmd:528`) reintroduces the discarded interval one step downstream.

**Evidence.** $ Rscript -e 'devtools::load_all(".") s <- compute_sem(sd=10, icc=0.85, n_sample=10, n_measurements=2); print(s) d <- compute_sdc(sem=s$sem, sem_se=s$sem_se); print(d) m <- qnorm(.975)*sqrt(2); cat(m*s$sem_ci_lo, m*s$sem_ci_up)' sem sem_se sem_ci_lo sem_ci_up 1 3.872983 0.9128709 2.663974 7.070558 <- exact chi-square, asymmetric sdc sdc_se sdc_ci_lo sdc_ci_up 1 10.73516 2.530303 5.775863 15.69447 <- package (Wald) coherent (multiplier * SEM CI): 7.384024 19.59823 The upper bound is 20% too low and the lower bound 22% too low; the two intervals barely overlap. Monte Carlo coverage of the SDC interval (one-way DGP, true SDC = 1.96*sqrt(2)*sigma_e, 4000 reps/cell, set.seed(20260828)): n k cov_SDC_wald(package) cov_SDC_exact(= multiplier * SEM CI) [1,] 10 2 0.9178 0.9610 [2,] 25 2 0.9335 0.9488 [3,] 50 3 0.9458 0.9538 [4,]100 2 0.9445 0.9482 The package's SDC interval undercovers by the same amount compute_sem()'s docs cite as the reason to abandon Wald (0.918 vs nominal 0.95 at n = 10, k = 2), while simply rescaling the SEM CI hits nominal.

**Impact.** The SDC and its CI are the headline measurement-error numbers in COSMIN/PROM reviews (they define whether an observed individual change exceeds measurement error). Following the package's own documented chain, a user reports an SDC 95% CI that is systematically too narrow at small reliability-study sample sizes — [5.78, 15.69] instead of [7.38, 19.60] at n = 10 — and that is not merely narrower but shifted, so the reported upper limit on measurement error is understated by 20%.

**Fix.** Accept the SEM interval and rescale it. Either add `sem_ci_lo`/`sem_ci_up` arguments to `compute_sdc()` and return `multiplier * sem_ci_lo` / `multiplier * sem_ci_up` when supplied, or (cleaner, and it makes the documented chain correct by default) have `compute_sdc()` accept the whole `compute_sem()` result object. Keep the Wald fallback only when no interval is available. Update `?compute_sdc`, `vignettes/Tutorial.Rmd:241-242` and `vignettes/Psychometrics.Rmd:528-538` to the new chain. Note the pinned assertion in tests_save/checked/test-psychometric-utils.R:171 and :163 encodes the current Wald CI, so those baselines need updating alongside.

### A6 raises a false [DISCORDANT] CI-width flag on package-computed `r` rows: the r CI is the back-transformed z interval, not a symmetric t interval (11% of df.haza rows)
`R/internal_flags.R:2251` — subsystem `flags-tier2` — refuted by **0 of 3** verifiers

**Claim.** A6 puts "r" in `qt_measures` and compares the reported r CI width against `2 * qt(.975, n-2) * r_se`. But under the default `smd_to_cor = "viechtbauer"` (and `lipsey_cooper`), `.smd_to_cor()` builds the r interval as the back-transform of a *symmetric interval on the variance-stabilised z scale* (R/internal_multiple_formulas.R:1201-1204), which is deliberately asymmetric about r and narrower than `2*z*se_r`. A6's expectation therefore does not describe the package's own construction, and the mismatch exceeds A6's own tolerance `max(0.05, 0.30/sqrt(n))` at small-to-moderate n. Every `r` (and `z`->`r`) row derived from an SMD, a 2x2 table, medians, plots or an F/t statistic is exposed.

**Evidence.** R code: ```r e <- es_from_means_sd(n_exp=13, n_nexp=13, mean_exp=5, mean_sd_exp=2, mean_nexp=3, mean_sd_nexp=2) # package's own construction, reproduced by hand from the z interval p<-0.5; a<-sqrt(dnorm(qnorm(p)))/(p*(1-p))^(1/4) bt <- function(zz) (1/a)*((exp(2*zz/a)-1)/(exp(2*zz/a)+1)) zlo <- e$z - qnorm(.975)*e$z_se; zup <- e$z + qnorm(.975)*e$z_se metaConvert:::.flag_numeric_integrity(e$r, e$r_se, e$r_ci_lo, e$r_ci_up, info_used="means_sd", measure="r", exp=FALSE, n_total=26, n_exp=13, n_nexp=13) ``` Actual output: ``` r = 0.5785844 se = 0.1839523 CI = [ 0.1481832 , 0.8576656 ] z = 0.5109237 z_se = 0.2 actual CI width : 0.7094825 A6 expectation 2*qt(.975,n-2)*se : 0.7593176 relative discrepancy : 0.06563148 A6 tolerance max(0.05,0.30/sqrt(26)) = 0.05883484 back-transform of the z interval: [ 0.1481832 , 0.8576656 ] <-- identical to the reported r CI A6 verdict: [DISCORDANT] CI width inconsistent with SE: expected 0.759, actual 0.709 (from means_sd) ``` The reported CI matches the package's own z back-transform to every printed digit, i.e. it is correct; the flag is the error. Blast radius on the shipped example data: ```r data(df.haza) r <- summary(convert_df(df.haza, measure="r", verbose=FALSE), flags=TRUE, guidance=FALSE) sum(grepl("CI width inconsistent", r$flags_crude)) ``` ``` d n=170 A6 fires on 0 row(s) md n=170 A6 fires on 0 row(s) r n=170 A6 fires on 19 row(s) e.g.: [DISCORDANT] CI width inconsistent with SE: expected 0.900, actual 0.823 (from means_sd) rows: 9,31,37,49,50,51,54,62,75,81,83,88,97,100,105,108,109,110,119 table of info_used among flagged rows: 2x2 cohen_d means_plot means_sd means_se med_quarts 3 2 6 4 3 1 ```

**Impact.** 19/170 (11%) of rows in the package's own `df.haza` example raise a [DISCORDANT] "CI width inconsistent with SE" flag for measure = "r" on data that is entirely correct. The flag tells a reviewer to go back to the primary source for a non-existent extraction error; at scale this is the classic cry-wolf failure that makes users disable `flags`. Concentrated at small n (the discrepancy shrinks below tolerance as n grows), i.e. exactly the studies most worth checking.

**Fix.** Do not treat `r` as a symmetric-t-interval measure in A6. Either (a) drop "r" from `qt_measures` and instead check the *z*-scale width, `(atanh-analogue of ci_up) - (...ci_lo)` vs `2*qnorm(.975)*z_se`, for rows whose r came from `.smd_to_cor()`; or (b) accept a band for r rows the way P6 does for `md`, i.e. accept any width between the z-back-transformed width and `2*qt(.975,n-2)*se`. Option (a) is exact. Keep the current behaviour only for routes that genuinely build `r +/- qt*se` (e.g. `es_from_pearson_r`).

### A6 (CI-vs-SE consistency) is skipped entirely for or/rr/irr/hr, so a user-entered SE on the wrong scale is accepted silently — the same error is caught for d
`R/internal_flags.R:2232` — subsystem `flags-tier2` — refuted by **0 of 3** verifiers

**Claim.** `skip_a6 <- identical(measure, "nnt") || exp` disables the whole A6 check for every exp-scale measure. The stated reason ("se stays on the log scale") is not a reason to skip: the CI is on the natural scale and the SE on the log scale, so the check is `log(ci_up) - log(ci_lo)` vs `2*z*se` — exactly the back-transform C5, D1, E3 and E5 already perform in the same file. The consequence is that the single most likely user-input error for these measures, documented in CLAUDE.md as a live hazard ("`user_se_crude` (when provided) must be on the **log scale**"), has no Tier-2 detector at all.

**Evidence.** R code — six OR rows whose CI implies a log-scale SE of 0.20; row 1 declares its SE on the natural scale (0.4 instead of 0.2): ```r d$user_es_crude <- c(2.0,1.9,2.1,2.0,1.95,2.05) d$user_ci_lo_crude <- d$user_es_crude*exp(-1.96*0.20) d$user_ci_up_crude <- d$user_es_crude*exp(+1.96*0.20) d$user_se_crude <- c(2.0*0.20, rep(0.20,5)) # row 1 = natural-scale SE summary(convert_df(d, measure="or", verbose=FALSE), flags=TRUE) ``` Actual output: ``` es se lo up info 1 2.00 0.4 1.351408 2.959875 user_input_crude 2 1.90 0.2 1.283838 2.811882 user_input_crude ... flags: 1 : 2 : ... 6 : ``` Nothing fires. The identical error on the additive scale IS caught: ```r d$user_es_crude <- c(0.70,0.65,0.72,0.70,0.68,0.71); se <- 0.20 d$user_ci_lo_crude <- d$user_es_crude-1.96*se; d$user_ci_up_crude <- d$user_es_crude+1.96*se d$user_se_crude <- c(2*se, rep(se,5)) summary(convert_df(d, measure="d", verbose=FALSE), flags=TRUE) ``` ``` flags: 1 : [DISCORDANT] CI width inconsistent with SE: expected 1.568, actual 0.784 (from user_input_crude) 2 : ``` What a log-scale A6 would have found on the OR row: ``` observed log CI width = 0.784 expected 2*1.96*se = 1.568 relative discrepancy = 0.5 ``` No other check catches it either: with 6 rows the D2 SE-outlier pool has IQR = 0 (five SEs of 0.2), so D2 is skipped, and 0.4/0.2 = 2x is under `se_outlier_min_ratio = 3`.

**Impact.** A study whose SE is 2x too large enters the meta-analysis with 1/4 of its correct inverse-variance weight, with no warning, for the four measures (OR, RR, IRR, HR) that dominate medical meta-analysis. Users who trust `flags` for the d-scale case are given no equivalent protection here, and the asymmetry is invisible from the output.

**Fix.** In `.flag_numeric_integrity()`, replace the blanket `|| exp` skip with a log-scale branch for exp-scale measures: when `exp && measure %in% c("logor","logrr","logirr","loghr")` and both CI bounds are > 0, set `ci_width <- log(ci_up[i]) - log(ci_lo[i])` and compare against `2*qnorm(.975)*se[i]` with the existing tolerance. Keep the skip for `nnt` only (its CI is genuinely discontinuous).

### D2's SE-outlier normalisation for measure="logrr" passes the RR into an OR-based 2x2 solver, giving normalised SEs of 0.68–14.7 instead of ~1 and a false SE-outlier on an ordinary small trial
`R/internal_flags.R:2884` — subsystem `flags-tier2` — refuted by **0 of 3** verifiers

**Claim.** In the `measure %in% c("logor", "logrr")` branch, `or_j <- if (on_exp_scale) es[j] else exp(es[j])` takes the row's effect size — which for a `logrr` pool is the **risk ratio**, not the odds ratio — and hands it to `.estimate_n_from_or_and_n_exp(or_j, var_j, ...)`, which solves for a 2x2 table whose *odds ratio* equals `or_j` and whose logOR variance equals `var_j` (also the wrong variance: `var_j` is Var(logRR)). The resulting cells are not the study's table, and the logRR SE evaluated on them (`sqrt(1/a - 1/(a+c) + 1/b - 1/(b+d))`) is not the study's expected SE. The normalisation whose entire purpose is "puts every row on the same ~1 scale" therefore fails only for RR pools; for OR pools it is exact.

**Evidence.** R code (direct probe of the normalisation on six ordinary 2x2 tables): ```r rec <- metaConvert:::.estimate_n_from_or_and_n_exp # for each table: normalised SE = se / exp_se, using the same call D2 makes ``` Actual output: ``` a b n1 OR RR logor_norm logrr_norm 1 40 20 100 2.6667 2.0000 1 1.5945 2 10 5 100 2.1111 2.0000 1 1.0856 3 80 60 100 2.6667 1.3333 1 0.6805 4 20 10 200 2.1111 2.0000 1 1.0856 5 5 2 50 2.6667 2.5000 1 14.6969 6 50 25 100 3.0000 2.0000 1 1.3598 ``` OR: exactly 1.0000 every time (as designed). RR: 0.68 to 14.70, driven purely by the event rate. End-to-end, eight correct 2x2 tables, run as "rr" and as "or": ```r tabs <- data.frame(a=c(40,10,80,20,5,50,30,12), b=c(20,5,60,10,2,25,15,6), n1=c(100,100,100,200,50,100,100,120), n2=c(100,100,100,200,50,100,100,120)) # d$n_cases_exp <- a ; d$n_cases_nexp <- b ; d$n_controls_* <- n - cases summary(convert_df(d, measure=m, verbose=FALSE), flags=TRUE) ``` ``` measure=or : SE-outlier rows = (none) measure=rr : SE-outlier rows = 5 a b n1 RR seLogRR 5 5 2 50 2.500 0.8124 5 : [UNUSUAL] SE outlier, IQR method: SE = 0.812 (from 2x2) ``` Study 5 is a=5/50 vs b=2/50 — a small trial with a low event rate, a correct table and a correct SE (`sqrt(1/5-1/50+1/2-1/50)` = 0.8124).

**Impact.** Every risk-ratio review with a spread of event rates gets an SE-outlier pool scattered over more than an order of magnitude. Two failure modes at once: correct small/low-event-rate studies are flagged [UNUSUAL] "SE outlier" (shown above), and the inflated IQR masks genuine SE errors that the same check catches without difficulty in an OR pool. The `se_outlier_min_ratio = 3` guard does not save it (14.7x clears it easily), and D2b's ratio method (threshold 10) fires on the same row.

**Fix.** Compute the row's odds ratio before the reconstruction. Either (i) reconstruct from the RR directly (solve for cells matching the RR and Var(logRR)), or (ii) if reusing the OR solver, note that a 2x2 with arm sizes n1, n2 satisfying a given RR has `OR = RR*(n2-b)/(n1-a)`, which is not identified from (RR, Var(logRR), n1, n2) alone — so option (i) is the correct route. Minimum safe fix: exclude `logrr` from the reconstruction branch and send it to the `sqrt(N)/balanced_se_const` fallback, which at least keeps the pool on one scale.

### Adjusted-SMD hierarchy ranks the attenuating ancova_means_se/_ci routes ABOVE the non-attenuating ancova_md_sd, contradicting the release's own stated ordering rule
`R/main_convert_df.R:1576` — subsystem `pipeline-wiring` — refuted by **0 of 3** verifiers

**Claim.** This release re-ordered the adjusted family by "attenuation tier": the code comment at R/main_convert_df.R:1517-1524 states that `es_ancova_means_sd_pooled_adj` was moved above `es_ancova_means_se`/`_ci` "so that summary() does not prefer the attenuated estimate when both are available", and the comment at :1531-1538 states that `es_ancova_md_sd` is the route whose "point estimate is unaffected by imbalance" and was therefore promoted (L20a) above the ANCOVA test statistics (L18). But `SMD_adjusted` is assembled as `c(es_cohen_d_adj_L17, means_adjusted_list_L19, md_adjusted_list_L20a, ancova_adjusted_list_L18, md_adjusted_list_L20b, plot_adjusted_L22)` (line 1576), so the WHOLE of L19 - including its two attenuating members `ancova_means_se` and `ancova_means_ci` - still outranks L20a. A row that reports both adjusted means+SEs and an adjusted MD with the residual SD therefore has the Lai & Kelley attenuated estimate selected while the unattenuated one is sitting in the same row. The promotion of md_sd was applied against L18 but not against the attenuating tail of L19.

**Evidence.** R script (run from the package root, data generated by the package's own simulations/studies/99_route_equivalence.R DGP at delta_x = 1, n = 60/60, rho = 0.5 - the exact cell tabulated in CLAUDE.md): suppressMessages(devtools::load_all('.', quiet=TRUE)) set.seed(20260801); n1 <- 60; n2 <- 60; delta <- 1.0; rho <- 0.5 x <- c(scale(rnorm(n1)), scale(rnorm(n2))); x[1:n1] <- x[1:n1] + 1.0 # covariate imbalance = 1 SD grp <- rep(c("exp","nexp"), c(n1,n2)) y <- delta*(grp=="exp") + rho*x + sqrt(1-rho^2)*rnorm(n1+n2) fit <- lm(y ~ grp + x); s_res <- summary(fit)$sigma co <- summary(fit)$coefficients; md <- -co["grpnexp","Estimate"] pr <- predict(fit, newdata=data.frame(grp=c("exp","nexp"), x=mean(x)), se.fit=TRUE) dat <- data.frame(study_id="s1", n_exp=n1, n_nexp=n2, n_cov_ancova=1, cov_outcome_r=rho, ancova_md=md, ancova_md_sd=s_res, ancova_mean_exp=pr$fit[1], ancova_mean_nexp=pr$fit[2], ancova_mean_se_exp=pr$se.fit[1], ancova_mean_se_nexp=pr$se.fit[2]) r <- summary(convert_df(dat, measure="d", selection_auto="adjusted", verbose=FALSE), flags=FALSE, guidance=FALSE) ACTUAL OUTPUT: all adjusted routes available : ancova_means_se + ancova_md_sd SELECTED by summary() : ancova_means_se d = 1.095963895 se = 0.1732187545 ancova_md_sd (non-attenuated reference): d = 1.163539319 se = 0.1750455149 ancova_means_se(attenuating tier) : d = 1.095963895 se = 0.1732187545 ratio d = 0.94192253 ratio se = 0.98956408 weight ratio (1/se^2) = 1.0212 The measured ratio 0.94192253 reproduces to 8 digits the closed form `1/sqrt(1 + n_j*D/4)` = 0.941923 that CLAUDE.md's own route-attenuation table gives for `ancova_means_se`, and 1.163539319 is the tabulated `md_sd`/`means_sd` reference. So summary() selects the estimate the package itself documents as attenuated when the unattenuated one is present. The ordering actually in force (printed from the metaConvert object): [1] user_input_adj [2] cohen_d_adj [3] ancova_means_sd [4] ancova_means_sd_pooled [5] ancova_means_sd_pooled_adj [6] ancova_means_se [7] ancova_means_ci [8] ancova_md_sd [9] ancova_t [10] ancova_f [11] ancova_t_pval [12] ancova_f_pval [13] etasq_adj [14] ancova_md_se [15] ancova_md_ci [16] ancova_md_pval [17] ancova_means_plot Control (the L20a-over-L18 half of the change IS in force, so this is specifically the L19/L20a boundary): d <- data.frame(study_id='s1', n_exp=60, n_nexp=60, n_cov_ancova=1, cov_outcome_r=0.5, ancova_md=1.0, ancova_md_sd=2.0, ancova_t=2.7, ancova_f=7.29) -> selected: ancova_md_sd es= 0.43023004 all= ancova_md_sd + ancova_t + ancova_f

**Impact.** Any comparison reporting adjusted marginal means with their standard errors alongside an adjusted mean difference with the residual SD (a common ANCOVA table layout) gets the attenuated d. At a 1-SD standardised covariate imbalance the magnitude is understated by 5.8% and the SE by 1.0%, so the study also enters the pool with ~2% excess inverse-variance weight. The bias is systematic in sign (always toward the null), so it does not average out on pooling. It is silent: the selected route is a legitimate one and no flag fires.

**Fix.** Split L19 the same way L20 was split. Define `means_adjusted_list_L19a = list(es_ancova_means_sd, es_ancova_means_sd_pooled, es_ancova_means_sd_pooled_adj)` and `means_adjusted_list_L19b = list(es_ancova_means_se, es_ancova_means_ci)`, then assemble `SMD_adjusted = c(es_cohen_d_adj_L17, means_adjusted_list_L19a, md_adjusted_list_L20a, means_adjusted_list_L19b, ancova_adjusted_list_L18, md_adjusted_list_L20b, plot_adjusted_L22)` at R/main_convert_df.R:1576 (means_se/means_ci carry only half the D contribution, so they belong between md_sd and the full-attenuation statistic routes). If instead the means-family precedence is deliberate, the comment at :1517-1524 must be corrected, because as written it asserts a rule the assembly does not implement.

### es_from_beta_std(): a negative sd_dv sign-flips d/g while d_se stays positive — the exact failure class internal_guards.R exists to close, but this route is not guarded, not in the guard sweep, and sd_dv is not in .positive_columns()
`R/es_from_REGRESSION.R:56` — subsystem `user-guards-ancova` — refuted by **0 of 3** verifiers

**Claim.** `unstd_beta <- beta_std * (sd_dv / sd_dummy)` divides by the reported SD of the dependent variable with no `.positive_or_na()` guard. A negative `sd_dv` negates `unstd_beta`; `es_from_beta_unstd()` then re-uses `sd_dv` only as `sd_dv^2`, so the pooled SD stays positive and the returned d/g is exactly sign-reversed with a perfectly healthy positive SE and a correctly-ordered CI. This is verbatim the defect internal_guards.R was written for ("a negative value sign-flips the effect size while md keeps its own sign ... and d_se stays positive, so the row looks healthy"), yet (a) the route calls none of the guard helpers, (b) it is absent from the 40-route sweep in tests/testthat/test-input-guards.R, and (c) `sd_dv` is absent from `.positive_columns()` in R/internal_flags.R, so Tier-1 validation neither flags nor NA-corrects it inside convert_df() either. The guard file's own header and CLAUDE.md both assert the sweep covers "every exported route taking a dispersion or an interval"; that claim is false for this route.

**Evidence.** $ Rscript -e "devtools::load_all('.'); ..." == beta_std with negative sd_dv (direct call) == case d d_se lo up 1 sd_dv=+0.98 0.7300839 0.3190612 0.08523717 1.37493067 2 sd_dv=-0.98 -0.7300839 0.3190612 -1.37493067 -0.08523717 (d flips sign; d_se identical and positive; CI correctly ordered around the wrong sign.) Sweep over beta/n: beta=0.10 n=20/22 d(+sd)=+0.196386 d(-sd)=-0.196386 se both=0.309699/0.309699 beta=0.35 n=50/50 d(+sd)=+0.739754 d(-sd)=-0.739754 se both=0.206727/0.206727 beta=0.60 n=50/50 d(+sd)=+1.484924 d(-sd)=-1.484924 se both=0.225887/0.225887 == through convert_df(), single row, flags=TRUE == d <- data.frame(study_id="a", beta_std=0.35, sd_dv=-0.98, n_exp=20, n_nexp=22) summary(convert_df(d, measure="g"), flags=TRUE) es = -0.7162942387 se = 0.3130348379 info = beta_std flags_crude = '' <-- NO flag at all correct (|sd_dv|) es = +0.7162942387 == correctness on valid input (so the route itself is right) == simulated y ~ g, n=40/55: means_sd d= 0.586558786784 beta_unstd d= 0.586558786784 (diff 1.1e-16) beta_std d= 0.586558786784 (diff 4.4e-16) == proof the sweep misses it == routes with a dispersion/interval argument not named in tests/testthat/test-input-guards.R: MISS es_from_beta_std :: sd_dv MISS es_from_beta_unstd :: sd_dv (grep of .positive_columns() in R/internal_flags.R: 'sd_dv' does not appear anywhere in that file)

**Impact.** Any study entered through the standardized-regression route with a mistyped/negatively-signed SD of the dependent variable contributes a Hedges' g of exactly the wrong sign, at full inverse-variance weight, with no flag in either flag tier and no warning. In a small pool this reverses the pooled direction; in a large pool it is a maximal-leverage outlier that only the cross-row direction-conflict check (Category G) might catch, and only if the pool happens to be split. es_from_beta_unstd() is safe (sd_dv enters squared) — the exposure is es_from_beta_std() specifically.

**Fix.** Add `sd_dv <- .positive_or_na(sd_dv)` at the top of `es_from_beta_std()` (and, for symmetry and the zero case, of `es_from_beta_unstd()`), add `"sd_dv"` to `.positive_columns()` in R/internal_flags.R so Tier-1 validation reports it, and add both routes to the sweep in tests/testthat/test-input-guards.R so the file's "every exported route taking a dispersion" claim becomes true.

### User-input ratio measures (or/rr/irr/hr): when a log-scale SE is supplied together with a natural-scale CI and no point estimate, the ES is taken as the ARITHMETIC midpoint of the natural CI instead of the geometric one — OR 2.75 where the truth is 2.449 (+12.3%), silently
`R/es_from_USER.R:537` — subsystem `user-guards-ancova` — refuted by **0 of 3** verifiers

**Claim.** In `es_from_user_crude()`/`es_from_user_adj()` the generic top block sets `es_val <- (user_ci_up + user_ci_lo)/2` whenever `user_es_*` is NA — an additive-scale midpoint. The ratio-aware correction block, `ratio_from_ci <- which(orig_type %in% c("or","rr","irr","hr") & is.na(user_se_crude) & ...)`, is gated on `is.na(user_se_crude)`. So when the user supplies the (correct, log-scale) SE *and* the natural-scale CI but leaves the point estimate blank, the row never enters that block: `es_from_or_se()`/`es_from_rr_se()` receive the arithmetic midpoint as the odds/risk ratio, and the `irr`/`hr` branches take `log()` of it. The resulting point estimate is not the geometric centre of its own reported CI, but the CI is restored verbatim on the log scale, so it stays inside its interval and the SE is exactly consistent with the CI width — no Category-A check (ES-outside-CI, CI-width-vs-SE), no CI-asymmetry check (which needs a non-NA user_es to run), and no flag of any kind fires.

**Evidence.** $ se <- (log(4)-log(1.5))/(2*qnorm(.975)) # 0.2502161419, the correct log-scale SE for CI [1.5, 4] # direct calls, all four ratio measures, ES left blank or logor = 1.01160091 (exp = 2.750000) se = 0.250216 ci=[0.4055,1.3863] rr logrr = 1.01160091 (exp = 2.750000) se = 0.250216 ci=[0.4055,1.3863] irr logirr = 1.01160091 (exp = 2.750000) se = 0.250216 ci=[0.4055,1.3863] hr loghr = 1.01160091 (exp = 2.750000) se = 0.250216 ci=[0.4055,1.3863] # same rows WITHOUT user_se_crude (CI-only path, which does hit ratio_from_ci) or logor = 0.89587973 (exp = 2.449490) se = 0.250216 <-- correct # independent derivation: geometric midpoint exp((log(1.5)+log(4))/2) = 2.449489743 arithmetic midpoint = (1.5+4)/2 = 2.75 # through convert_df(), measure="or", flags=TRUE study_id es_crude se_crude es_ci_lo_crude es_ci_up_crude 1 correct_entry 2.4494897 0.25021614 1.5 4 2 se_plus_ci_no_es 2.7500000 0.25021614 1.5 4 flags: [1] "" "" <-- both rows completely unflagged relative error of row 2 = 12.26828 %

**Impact.** A user-input OR/RR/IRR/HR row entered with a log-scale SE plus the published natural-scale CI, but with the point-estimate cell left blank, is pooled with a 12%-scale error in the point estimate (the error grows with CI width: it is exp(arithmetic mean) / geometric mean of the bounds) while carrying the correct weight. Nothing in either flag tier can see it, because the reported CI is what the estimate is checked against and the CI is preserved. Affects all four ratio measures and both the crude and the adjusted user routes (identical code at line 828).

**Fix.** Drop `is.na(user_se_crude)` from the `ratio_from_ci` gate at R/es_from_USER.R:537 and :828, or split it: always recompute `es_val` on the log scale for ratio originals whose ES is missing but whose CI is present (`es_val <- (log_lo + log_up)/2`), and keep the `is.na(user_se_crude)` condition only for the SE derivation. Add a regression test asserting `exp(logor) == sqrt(ci_lo*ci_up)` when the ES is blank, with and without a supplied SE.

### data_extraction_sheet() aborts with an internal error for four measure values listed in its own \arguments ("logor", "logrr", "logirr", "loghr")
`R/data_extraction.R:744` — subsystem `docs-vs-code` — refuted by **0 of 3** verifiers

**Claim.** man/data_extraction_sheet.Rd's \item{measure} documents "One of \"d\", ..., \"or\", \"rr\", \"irr\", \"hr\", \"logor\", \"logrr\", \"logirr\", \"loghr\", ...". The if/else-if dispatch chain in data_extraction_sheet() (R/data_extraction.R:565-772) has no `else` branch and no case for the four log-scale names, so `dat` is never assigned and the function dies at line 784 (`dup <- duplicated(colnames(dat))`) with the internal message "object 'dat' not found". This is the SAME defect class cran-comments.md claims to have fixed this release for the sibling function: "see_input_data() aborted with an internal error ... for several values of its measure argument, including one listed in the function's own default choices; unsupported values are now rejected by name." see_input_data() got the by-name guard (R/data_extraction.R:866); data_extraction_sheet() did not. The \usage default vector is also inconsistent with the same page's \arguments: \usage prints c("d",...,"irr","prop") and omits "rp","zp","hr","alpha","omega","icc","all", all of which the \arguments promises and all of which in fact work.

**Evidence.** $ Rscript -e 'devtools::load_all("."); for (m in c("or","logor","logrr","logirr","loghr","alpha","omega","icc","all")) {s <- try(data_extraction_sheet(measure=m, extension="data.frame", verbose=FALSE), silent=TRUE); cat(sprintf("%-8s : %s\n", m, if (inherits(s,"try-error")) gsub("\n"," ",as.character(s)) else paste0("ok (", ncol(s), " cols)")))}' or : ok (68 cols) logor : Error in data_extraction_sheet(measure = m, extension = "data.frame", : objet 'dat' introuvable logrr : Error in data_extraction_sheet(measure = m, extension = "data.frame", : objet 'dat' introuvable logirr : Error in data_extraction_sheet(measure = m, extension = "data.frame", : objet 'dat' introuvable loghr : Error in data_extraction_sheet(measure = m, extension = "data.frame", : objet 'dat' introuvable alpha : ok (27 cols) omega : ok (29 cols) icc : ok (25 cols) all : ok (267 cols) ("objet 'dat' introuvable" = "object 'dat' not found"; the session locale is French.) man/data_extraction_sheet.Rd:17 -> \item{measure}{Target effect size measure. One of "d", "g", "md", "dw", "gw", "mdw", "r", "z", "rp", "zp", "or", "rr", "irr", "hr", "logor", "logrr", "logirr", "loghr", "logvr", "logcvr", "nnt", "rd", "prop", "alpha", "omega", "icc", or "all" ...} man/data_extraction_sheet.Rd:7-9 -> \usage{ data_extraction_sheet(measure = c("d", "g", "md", "dw", "gw", "mdw", "or", "rr", "nnt", "rd", "r", "z", "logvr", "logcvr", "irr", "prop"), ...) }

**Impact.** "logor"/"logrr"/"logirr"/"loghr" are the canonical measure names in convert_df() (its own formals list "logor", "logrr", "logirr", "loghr" and NOT "or"/"rr"/"irr"/"hr"), so a user who starts from convert_df's vocabulary and asks for the matching extraction sheet — the documented first step of the package workflow — gets an opaque internal R error with no guidance. No wrong numbers, but it is a documented-argument-value crash on a user-facing entry point, i.e. exactly the failure this release states it repaired elsewhere.

**Fix.** In R/data_extraction.R, either (a) normalise at the top of data_extraction_sheet(): `measure <- sub("^log", "", measure)` for the four ratio measures (they take identical input columns to "or"/"rr"/"irr"/"hr"), or (b) add the same by-name guard see_input_data() received at line 866, listing the supported set. Option (a) is preferable since the columns genuinely exist. Then align the \usage default vector with the \arguments list.

### unit_type's documented default "raw_scale" is never used: omitting it silently yields NA effect sizes under cor_to_smd = "mathur" (7 help pages)
`R/es_from_stand_COR.R:110` — subsystem `docs-vs-code` — refuted by **0 of 3** verifiers

**Claim.** This release's doc pass added the sentence 'Defaults to \'"raw_scale"\'.' to the unit_type \item in 8 help pages (es_from_pearson_r, es_from_fisher_z, es_from_spearman_rho, es_from_linreg_t, es_from_linreg_b_se, es_from_linreg_b_ci, es_from_linreg_b_pval, convert_df), and the \usage blocks print `unit_type = "raw_scale"`. For the seven es_from_* routes that default is dead: each body immediately overwrites it with NA (`if (missing(unit_type)) unit_type <- rep(NA, length(...))` at R/es_from_stand_COR.R:110-112 and :251-253, R/es_from_REGRESSION.R:239, :400, :483, :564). The mathur eligibility gate then requires `!is.na(unit_type)` (R/es_from_stand_COR.R:143-144), so the row is dropped from the conversion and d/d_se/g/g_se come back NA with no warning and no message. convert_df() is unaffected (it fills the column from its own argument), so the defect is confined to the direct-call usage those seven help pages document.

**Evidence.** $ Rscript -e 'devtools::load_all("."); print(formals(es_from_pearson_r)$unit_type); a <- es_from_pearson_r(pearson_r=0.4, n_sample=100, cor_to_smd="mathur", sd_iv=2, unit_increase_iv=1); print(a[,c("d","d_se","g","g_se")]); b <- es_from_pearson_r(pearson_r=0.4, n_sample=100, cor_to_smd="mathur", sd_iv=2, unit_increase_iv=1, unit_type="raw_scale"); print(b[,c("d","d_se","g","g_se")])' formals default: [1] "raw_scale" (1) unit_type OMITTED (docs say it defaults to 'raw_scale'): d d_se g g_se 1 NA NA NA NA (2) unit_type EXPLICITLY 'raw_scale': d d_se g g_se 1 0.2182179 0.05752163 0.2165429 0.0570801 Same for es_from_fisher_z: omitted d = NA explicit d = 0.2182179 Control (convert_df is fine): convert_df(d, measure="g", cor_to_smd="mathur") -> es = 0.2165429, 0.2868358 with or without a unit_type column. Pages carrying the new sentence (scanned man/*.Rd for the literal string): convert_df.Rd, es_from_fisher_z.Rd, es_from_linreg_b_ci.Rd, es_from_linreg_b_pval.Rd, es_from_linreg_b_se.Rd, es_from_linreg_t.Rd, es_from_pearson_r.Rd, es_from_spearman_rho.Rd No test pins this: tests/testthat/test-method-defaults-consistency.R:301 pins only `.convert_df_defaults$unit_type == "raw_scale"` (the convert_df level, which works). Nothing in SOURCE-VERIFICATION.md, NEWS.md or tests_save/checked/ documents the direct-call NA as intentional.

**Impact.** A user following the newly written help page — call es_from_pearson_r()/es_from_fisher_z()/es_from_linreg_*() with cor_to_smd = "mathur", sd_iv and unit_increase_iv, and leave unit_type at its stated default — gets d = g = NA instead of the raw-scale conversion the page promises. The study then drops out of a pool silently (rma() removes NA rows without comment). It is a lost study rather than a wrong number, but it is invisible at the point of use and the documentation actively directs users into it.

**Fix.** One line per route: replace `if (missing(unit_type)) unit_type <- rep(NA, length(x))` with `if (missing(unit_type)) unit_type <- rep("raw_scale", length(x))` in R/es_from_stand_COR.R:110 and :251 and R/es_from_REGRESSION.R:239, 400, 483, 564 — matching the formal default and the eight help pages. (Alternatively drop the 'Defaults to "raw_scale"' sentence from the seven es_from_* pages and state that unit_type is required for cor_to_smd = "mathur"; but making the code match the doc is the smaller change and removes the silent NA.)

### The NEWS.md that will ship to CRAN deletes the "results change — re-run your 2.0.0 analyses" warning (457 lines → 9)
`NEWS.md:1` — subsystem `cran-readiness` — refuted by **0 of 3** verifiers

**Claim.** CRAN currently hosts metaConvert 2.0.0. This release fixes conversion formulas, so re-running a dataset under 2.0.1 returns different effect sizes, SEs and route selections. The repository's own NEWS.md (on `master`, and on `HEAD` of this branch) opens the 2.0.1 entry with a section titled "Results change: re-run analyses produced with 2.0.0", including a magnitude table (|r| from an OR up 10–50%; ANCOVA `r_se` down to −49%; etasq-derived d from −11% to +67%; inverted r/z CI on `reverse_2x2` rows). The **working tree** NEWS.md — the one `R CMD build` actually put in the tarball — deletes that entire section and replaces the 2.0.1 entry with 8 generic bullets, the strongest of which is "Corrected formulas and conversion explanations in the documentation". NEWS.md is the only upgrade channel CRAN publishes, so a user moving 2.0.0 → 2.0.1 gets silently different numbers with no notice to re-run.

**Evidence.** $ git show master:NEWS.md | sed -n '1,9p' # metaConvert 2.0.1 ## Results change: re-run analyses produced with 2.0.0 This release fixes bugs in the conversion formulas themselves, so a dataset re-run under 2.0.1 can return different effect sizes, different standard errors, or a different estimation route than it did under 2.0.0. **If you have results from 2.0.0 that are not yet published, re-run them.** Nothing here requires a change to your code or your data. $ echo "master NEWS 2.0.1 section lines: $(git show master:NEWS.md | awk '/^# metaConvert 2.0.0/{exit} {n++} END{print n}')" master NEWS 2.0.1 section lines: 457 $ echo "working NEWS 2.0.1 section lines: $(awk '/^# metaConvert 2.0.0/{exit} {n++} END{print n}' NEWS.md)" working NEWS 2.0.1 section lines: 9 $ git diff --stat HEAD -- NEWS.md NEWS.md | 1322 +--------------------------------- 1 file changed, 7 insertions(+), 1315 deletions(-) # what the built tarball actually ships: $ tar xzf metaConvert_2.0.1.tar.gz -O metaConvert/NEWS.md | head -8 # metaConvert 2.0.1 - Added the `flag_group` option to group across multiple rows the checks performed by metaDETECT - Added and refined metaDETECT quality flags for reliability pools, mixed transforms, and 2x2 table margins - Added `es_formulas()` to evaluate impact of choice of formula on effect size estimates - Improved speed performance of the convert_df() - Improved the reliability paths (alpha/ICC) - Corrected formulas and conversion explanations in the documentation # CRAN's current version, confirmed over the network: $ Rscript -e "ap <- available.packages(repos='https://cloud.r-project.org'); cat(ap['metaConvert','Version'])" 2.0.0 # and the shipped NEWS also never mentions the two NEW exported functions: $ comm -23 <exports of HEAD> <exports of master> es_from_omega reliability_backtransform

**Impact.** Every 2.0.0 user who upgrades and re-runs a conversion pipeline gets different effect sizes and different inverse-variance weights, with nothing in the shipped changelog telling them to re-check published or in-review results. This is precisely the "wrong number in a published meta-analysis" pathway: the numbers are now *right*, but the user does not learn the old ones were wrong. It also contradicts cran-comments.md, which still describes six numbered result-changing corrections for 2.0.1.

**Fix.** Restore the `## Results change: re-run analyses produced with 2.0.0` section (and its magnitude table) at the top of the 2.0.1 entry before building the submission tarball — e.g. `git checkout master -- NEWS.md` then re-apply the dev-branch entries, or reinstate `git show HEAD:NEWS.md`. Also add the two new exports (`es_from_omega`, `reliability_backtransform`) to the entry.

### `data_extraction_sheet()` aborts with `object 'dat' not found` for four `measure` values its own Rd documents as valid
`R/data_extraction.R:783` — subsystem `cran-readiness` — refuted by **0 of 3** verifiers

**Claim.** The `measure` dispatch in `data_extraction_sheet()` is an if / else-if chain (lines 565–772) with **no `else` branch**. An unmatched value leaves `dat` undefined, and line 783 (`dup <- duplicated(colnames(dat))`) then throws the internal R error `object 'dat' not found`. Four of the values enumerated in the function's own roxygen `@param measure` (R/data_extraction.R:4, rendered into man/data_extraction_sheet.Rd) hit this path: `"logor"`, `"logrr"`, `"logirr"`, `"loghr"` — all four are legitimate `convert_df()` measures. Any typo produces the same cryptic error. This is the *identical* defect that NEWS/cran-comments say was fixed for `see_input_data()` this release ("aborted with an internal error … unsupported values are now rejected by name"); the by-name guard was added at R/data_extraction.R:866 for `see_input_data()` only.

**Evidence.** $ Rscript -e "suppressMessages(pkgload::load_all('.', quiet=TRUE)) docd <- c('d','g','md','dw','gw','mdw','r','z','rp','zp','or','rr','irr','hr','logor','logrr','logirr','loghr','logvr','logcvr','nnt','rd','prop','alpha','omega','icc','all') for (m in docd) cat(sprintf('%-8s %s\n', m, tryCatch({x <- data_extraction_sheet(measure=m, verbose=FALSE); paste0('OK ncol=', ncol(x))}, error=function(e) paste('ERROR:', conditionMessage(e)))))" d OK ncol=163 ... hr OK ncol=18 logor ERROR: objet 'dat' introuvable logrr ERROR: objet 'dat' introuvable logirr ERROR: objet 'dat' introuvable loghr ERROR: objet 'dat' introuvable logvr OK ncol=48 ... all OK ncol=267 # a typo behaves the same, and the sister function shows what the message should be: --- documented value "logor" --- [1] "objet 'dat' introuvable" --- typo "oddsratio" --- [1] "objet 'dat' introuvable" --- see_input_data("logor") for contrast --- [1] "see_input_data(): unsupported 'measure' value \"logor\". Supported values are \"all\", \"d\", \"g\", ... use data_extraction_sheet() for those." # the Rd that promises these four values: $ sed -n '/item{measure}/p' man/data_extraction_sheet.Rd \item{measure}{Target effect size measure. One of "d", "g", "md", "dw", "gw", "mdw", "r", "z", "rp", "zp", "or", "rr", "irr", "hr", "logor", "logrr", "logirr", "loghr", "logvr", "logcvr", "nnt", "rd", "prop", "alpha", "omega", "icc", or "all" ...} # for contrast, all 22 convert_df() measures run end to end without error (smoke test, 1-row df): d g md dw gw mdw logor logrr logirr loghr nnt rd r z rp zp logvr logcvr prop alpha omega icc -> all OK

**Impact.** A user following the package's own help page to generate an extraction sheet for a log-scale measure (`logor` is the natural request for an OR meta-analysis) gets an uninformative internal error and no sheet. Note the *doc* half is new this release: on `master` the `@param measure` text named no specific values, so nothing invited the user to pass `"logor"`; the enumeration added in 2.0.1 now advertises four values that crash. R CMD check does not catch it because no example uses them.

**Fix.** Mirror the `see_input_data()` guard: immediately after `measure = measure[1]` in `data_extraction_sheet()`, reject unsupported values by name with the list of supported ones; and either add `logor`/`logrr`/`logirr`/`loghr` branches (aliasing to the `or`/`rr`/`irr`/`hr` blocks) or remove those four from the `@param measure` text at R/data_extraction.R:4 and re-document.

### `checks.R` — the repo's own pre-CRAN check harness — points at a stale v1.1.0 checkout, so every release gate validates the wrong package
`checks.R:23` — subsystem `cran-readiness` — refuted by **0 of 3** verifiers

**Claim.** `PKG_ROOT <- "c:/Users/coren/Documents/sideprojet/metaConvert"` is hard-coded. That directory exists but holds metaConvert **1.1.0** on branch `ready/fix` (HEAD `d9376c6 "before summary rework"`), not the 2.0.1 tree under audit. Every function in the file — `check_cran()`, `check_nosuggests()`, `check_win()`, `check_mac()`, `check_deps()`, `check_all()`, `check_revdeps()`, `check_before_cran()` — resolves paths through `PKG_ROOT`, so running the documented pre-submission sequence from this repository builds, checks and uploads the 1.1.0 tree. `check_nosuggests()` is specifically the guard CLAUDE.md says regressed *twice*; against 1.1.0 it cannot exercise the omega/ICC/reliability routes at all, and would pass while telling the maintainer the 2.1.0 work is safe. The file was added two commits ago (`cb72fd4 "Put the check commands in the repo instead of in a chat log"`) and added to `.Rbuildignore` this release, i.e. it is being adopted as the release procedure now.

**Evidence.** $ ls -d "c:/Users/coren/Documents/sideprojet/metaConvert" c:/Users/coren/Documents/sideprojet/metaConvert # exists $ cd "c:/Users/coren/Documents/sideprojet/metaConvert" && grep '^Version' DESCRIPTION && git log --oneline -1 && git branch --show-current Version: 1.1.0 d9376c6 before summary rework ready/fix $ diff -rq R "c:/.../270826/metaConvert/R" | head -4 Files R/data.R and .../R/data.R differ Only in .../R: es_formulas.R Only in .../R: es_from_OMEGA.R # run from the AUDITED tree, checks.R reports on the OTHER one: $ cd "c:/Users/coren/Documents/maison_juillet/270826/metaConvert" $ Rscript -e "source('checks.R'); check_deps()" Imports declares : compareDF, metafor, mvtnorm, estimraw, rio jamais vus en pkg::fun ni en import() : mvtnorm Suggests declares : 15 # but the audited DESCRIPTION has 4 Imports (no mvtnorm) and 16 Suggests: $ grep '^Imports' DESCRIPTION Imports: compareDF, metafor, estimraw, rio $ Rscript -e "d<-read.dcf('DESCRIPTION'); cat(length(strsplit(gsub('[[:space:]]+','',d[1,'Suggests']),',')[[1]]))" 16

**Impact.** The whole pre-submission safety net is aimed at the wrong tree. A green `check_before_cran()` is evidence about metaConvert 1.1.0, not about the tarball being submitted — including the noSuggests flavour that has already broken CRAN twice, and the reverse-dependency check for `metaumbrella`, which calls the `es_from_*` routes directly.

**Fix.** Replace the hard-coded constant with a location-independent root, e.g. `PKG_ROOT <- normalizePath(rprojroot::find_root(rprojroot::has_file("DESCRIPTION")))` or simply `PKG_ROOT <- getwd()` with a `stopifnot(file.exists(file.path(PKG_ROOT, "DESCRIPTION")))` and an assertion that `read.dcf(...)[1,"Version"]` matches the version being released. Also update the stale `setwd()` in the header comment (checks.R:4) and CLAUDE.md's R path (it says R-4.5.1; the installed R is 4.5.0).

### V2/V3's rounding pad is defeated by trailing zeros; on the new reliability CI triplets an inverted CI survives unflagged and becomes a 3.9x inflated meta-analytic weight
`R/internal_flags.R:800` — subsystem `flags-tier1` — refuted by **0 of 3** verifiers

**Claim.** `.count_decimals()` reads decimals from `format(x, drop0trailing = TRUE)`, so a bound printed as 0.90 is stored as 0.9 and reported as 1 dp. V2's pad becomes `0.5*10^-1 + 0.5*10^-1 = 0.10` instead of 0.01, and V3's `pad_lo` likewise. On the `icc` / `omega` / `cronbach_alpha` CI triplets -- which are NEW in .ci_triplets() this release (git show master:R/internal_flags.R shows no omega/icc/alpha entries) and whose SE comes from the CI read at the bounds -- an arithmetically impossible interval therefore passes validation AND is used to build the row's weight. `.ci_lower()`/`.ci_upper()` then absorb the transposition silently. The identical error one digit over (0.91/0.87) is caught and the row is removed, so the check's behaviour is decided by whether a reported digit happens to be a zero.

**Evidence.** R: d <- data.frame(study_id=c("A","B","C"), icc=.85, n_sample=120, n_measurements=2, icc_ci_lo=c(0.90,0.91,NA), icc_ci_up=c(0.86,0.87,NA)) s <- summary(convert_df(d, measure="icc", verbose=FALSE), flags=TRUE, split_adjusted=FALSE) Output: row_id es_crude se_crude 1 1 -1.89712 0.08583633 <- CI [0.90, 0.86] INVERTED, kept, SE built from it 21 2 NA NA <- CI [0.91, 0.87], same 0.04 inversion, caught + NA'd 2 3 -1.89712 0.16958922 <- no CI, closed-form (n,k) SE row1 flagged INVALID? FALSE row2? TRUE Weight consequence: (0.16958922/0.08583633)^2 = 3.9035 -> row 1 receives 3.90x the weight the closed form would give it, with an empty flag string. Direct .validate_input_data() probes (verbose=FALSE): icc=0.85, ci=[0.90,0.95] (point 0.05 outside its own CI) -> NO [INVALID], data kept icc=0.80, ci=[0.90,0.95] (point 0.10 outside its own CI) -> NO [INVALID], data kept icc=0.85, ci=[0.91,0.95] (same violation, non-round bound) -> [INVALID] Value outside CI for 'icc' set to missing icc=0.85, ci=[0.90,0.80] (transposed) -> NO flag at all icc=0.85, ci=[0.91,0.81] (transposed) -> [INVALID] Inverted CI for 'icc' (lo > up) set to missing metaConvert:::.count_decimals(0.90) -> 1 (true precision 2) metaConvert:::.count_decimals(0.85) -> 2

**Impact.** On a [0,1]-bounded column a +/-0.05 rounding allowance is larger than most real CI half-widths, so V2 (documented in CLAUDE.md as an unconditional logical-impossibility check) and V3 are effectively off whenever a bound ends in 0 -- which on reliability scales is the modal reporting pattern (.70, .80, .90, .95). The failure is silent and changes the pooled estimate, because the surviving interval, not the closed form, sets the inverse-variance weight.

**Fix.** Two options, both cheap. (a) Do not derive the pad from a single value's own decimals on bounded coefficient columns: cap it, e.g. `pad <- min(pad, 0.02)` for triplets whose val lies in [-1, 1]. (b) Better: take the precision of the *most* precisely printed member of the triplet as the row's reporting precision (`dp <- max(.count_decimals(val), .count_decimals(lo), .count_decimals(up))`) and build all three pads from it -- a study printing 0.85 and 0.86 is printing to 2 dp, so 0.90 is 0.90, not 0.9. Add a regression test pinning that icc = 0.85 with ci = c(0.90, 0.86) is flagged.

### V26 fires on correctly-computed paired CIs whenever r_pre_post is below ~0.15 (large n) to ~0.35 (small n), refuting its documented "needs no assumed r" robustness
`R/internal_flags.R:1251` — subsystem `flags-tier1` — refuted by **0 of 3** verifiers

**Claim.** V26 compares the reported CI half-width against the independent-groups SE and fires when the implied critical value matches z or t within `paired_as_indep_tol`. For a *correct* paired CI the implied critical value is `qt(.975, n-1) * sqrt(1 - r)`, not something safely below z: at small n the t multiplier inflates it back into the z band for any moderate r, and even as n -> Inf it stays inside the 7% band for r < ~0.135. CLAUDE.md states "A genuinely paired interval is narrower, so its implied critical value falls below z and does not match -- the check therefore needs no assumed r_pre_post, making it robust to the correlation". That is true only above the band; the check has an unstated r-dependence in exactly the region where paired analysis is least redundant.

**Evidence.** R (CI built the CORRECT paired way: half = qt(.975, n-1) * sqrt(sd_pre^2+sd_post^2-2*r*sd_pre*sd_post)/sqrt(n), sd_pre=sd_post=10): tryr <- function(n, r) { se <- sqrt(200-200*r)/sqrt(n); half <- qt(.975,n-1)*se d <- data.frame(n_exp=n,n_nexp=n,mean_sd_exp=10,mean_sd_nexp=10, user_ci_lo_crude=5-half,user_ci_up_crude=5+half, user_ci_lo_adj=NA_real_,user_ci_up_adj=NA_real_) grepl("Within-subject design", metaConvert:::.validate_input_data(d,verbose=FALSE,measure="mdw")$issues[1]) } Fire map (FIRE = false positive on a correct paired CI): n\r 0.00 0.05 0.10 0.15 0.20 0.25 0.30 0.35 0.40 0.45 0.50 10 . FIRE FIRE FIRE FIRE FIRE FIRE FIRE . . . 15 FIRE FIRE FIRE FIRE FIRE FIRE . . . . . 20 FIRE FIRE FIRE FIRE FIRE . . . . . . 30 FIRE FIRE FIRE FIRE FIRE . . . . . . 50 FIRE FIRE FIRE FIRE . . . . . . . 100 FIRE FIRE FIRE FIRE . . . . . . . 300 FIRE FIRE FIRE . . . . . . . . 1000 FIRE FIRE FIRE . . . . . . . . (Control: a CI genuinely built with the independent-groups SE fires at n=11 and n=100, i.e. the true-positive path is intact.) Closed form: fires iff qt(.975,n-1)*sqrt(1-r) is within 7% of qnorm(.975) or of qt(.975,2n-2). At n=11 that is r in [0, 0.331]; as n -> Inf it is r in [0, 0.135].

**Impact.** Any within-subject pool whose outcome has a low-to-moderate test-retest correlation (biomarkers, single-item VAS, symptom counts) gets every row flagged "[UNUSUAL] Within-subject design ... but the reported CI matches an independent-groups SE ... verify the SE formula" -- i.e. reviewers are told to go re-check a CI that is correct. Warn-only, so no number changes, but the flag is being marketed (CLAUDE.md, the errors_framework validation set) as deterministic and assumption-free, and its specificity is not.

**Fix.** Either (a) state the r-band in the message and in CLAUDE.md -- "this check cannot distinguish an independent-groups CI from a paired CI when the pre-post correlation is below ~0.15" -- or (b) suppress the flag when `r_pre_post` is supplied and the paired-SE reconstruction also matches within tolerance (i.e. only fire when the independent-groups SE matches and the paired one does not), or (c) require the implied critical value to match z/t *and* exceed the largest paired critical value achievable at any r >= 0.2.

### flag_group silently does not scope seven Tier-1 cross-row checks, while the documentation exempts only V23
`R/internal_flags.R:434` — subsystem `flags-tier1` — refuted by **0 of 3** verifiers

**Claim.** `.validate_input_data()` has no `flag_group` parameter and `convert_df()` does not pass one, so V23, V35, V36, V37, V38, V39, V42 and V43 are always computed over the whole dataset. `?summary.metaConvert` (R/functions_summary.R:160, man/summary.metaConvert.Rd:42) says flag_group scopes "the cross-row checks" and names exactly one exemption: "the byte-identical templated-data check (V23)". `.default_flag_options()`'s own comment repeats only V23. Six of the seven unlisted checks emit messages that assert a cross-pool fact ("differs from the rest of the pool", "the pool also contains", "in a pool of %d studies"), and the Tier-2 checks in the same run do get a " (within group 'X')" suffix, so the output actively misleads about which comparison set was used.

**Evidence.** R: d2 <- data.frame(study_id=paste0("T",1:6), design=c(rep("test-retest",4), rep("inter-rater",2)), icc=c(.80,.82,.84,.86,.75,.77), n_measurements=c(2,2,2,2,4,4), n_sample=c(50,60,70,80,90,100)) for (fg in list(NULL,"design")) summary(convert_df(d2, measure="icc", verbose=FALSE, flag_options=if(is.null(fg)) list() else list(flag_group=fg)), flags=TRUE, split_adjusted=FALSE) Output -- byte-identical with and without flag_group: ### ICC flag_group = NULL row 5 :: [UNUSUAL] Measurement count differs from the rest of the pool: 'n_measurements' = 4 where 4 of 6 studies report 2... row 6 :: [UNUSUAL] Measurement count differs from the rest of the pool: 'n_measurements' = 4 where 4 of 6 studies report 2... ### ICC flag_group = design row 5 :: [UNUSUAL] Measurement count differs from the rest of the pool: 'n_measurements' = 4 where 4 of 6 studies report 2... row 6 :: [UNUSUAL] Measurement count differs from the rest of the pool: 'n_measurements' = 4 where 4 of 6 studies report 2... Note the two design strata are k=2 (n=4) and k=4 (n=2); within each stratum k is constant, so V42's own majority guard would keep it silent -- grouping is exactly the remedy V42's comment describes ("a genuinely mixed-panel review has no majority and stays silent by construction") and it does not reach it. No " (within group ...)" suffix appears either, unlike D1/D2/G/H in the same run. grep confirms the plumbing gap: grep -n flag_group R/*.R -> only .default_flag_options / .build_group_key / functions_summary .validate_input_data signature (R/internal_flags.R:434-449) has no flag_group argument.

**Impact.** Multi-instrument RG reviews, multi-outcome COSMIN sheets and multi-domain ICC reviews get V37/V42/V43/V36/V35 false positives that the documented remedy cannot switch off, and the message states a pool composition the user has explicitly told the package is wrong. `.validate_flag_options()` does not warn either, because flag_group is a legal option name in both contexts.

**Fix.** Either thread `flag_group` into `.validate_input_data()` and wrap V23/V35/V36/V37/V38/V39/V42/V43 in `.by_group()` (they are already index-based, and `.row_ref()` already renders study ids, so the refactor is mechanical), or -- as a CRAN-safe minimum -- correct the two Rd/roxygen sentences and the `.default_flag_options()` comment to enumerate all eight unscoped Tier-1 checks by number.

### V42 and V43 are not gated on `measure`, so an alpha run on a COSMIN sheet is flagged about its ICC column -- the exact failure V31 was gated to prevent
`R/internal_flags.R:1812` — subsystem `flags-tier1` — refuted by **0 of 3** verifiers

**Claim.** V31 carries an explicit guard `icc_measure_ok <- is.null(measure) || !nzchar(measure) || identical(measure, "icc")` with a comment naming the motivating case: "a COSMIN-style extraction sheet holding alpha, omega and ICC columns side by side (one sheet, several properties, one pool per property) would otherwise carry it into every run". V42 (line 1812) and V43 (line 1861) were added in the same release, key only on `"icc" %in% colnames(x)`, and have no such guard. V36's icc/omega blocks are likewise ungated. So the guard was applied to one of four ICC-scoped checks.

**Evidence.** R: d <- data.frame(study_id=paste0("S",1:4), cronbach_alpha=c(.88,.91,.85,.90), n_items=20, n_sample=c(100,120,140,160), icc=c(.80,.85,.78,.82), n_measurements=c(2,2,2,3), icc_type=c("agreement","agreement","consistency","agreement")) summary(convert_df(d, measure="alpha", verbose=FALSE), flags=TRUE, split_adjusted=FALSE) Output (measure = "alpha" -- NO ICC is being estimated; every crude estimate is cronbach_alpha): row 1 | [INFO] Pool mixes ICC estimands: this row is 'agreement' (absolute agreement, ICC(2,1)) ... row 2 | [INFO] Pool mixes ICC estimands: this row is 'agreement' ... row 3 | [INFO] Pool mixes ICC estimands: this row is 'consistency' (consistency, ICC(3,1)) ... row 4 | [UNUSUAL] Measurement count differs from the rest of the pool: 'n_measurements' = 3 where 3 of 4 studies report 2 ... row 4 | [INFO] Pool mixes ICC estimands: this row is 'agreement' ... Control, same data with measure = "icc": the identical V42/V43 flags appear, plus V31. V31 correctly stays silent on the alpha run; V42/V43 do not.

**Impact.** Every alpha (or omega, or g) analysis run off a COSMIN sheet that also carries ICC columns is decorated with ICC pooling advice -- "split the analysis by icc_type, or enter it as a moderator" -- about a measure the run does not estimate. Since V43 is `[INFO]` and always active (no enable_cross_row gate), it cannot be switched off at all.

**Fix.** Hoist V31's `icc_measure_ok` into a local computed once, and reuse it as an extra condition on the V42 (line 1812) and V43 (line 1861) guards, and on the `icc` entry of V36's `rel_blocks` loop; symmetrically gate V38/V39 on `measure == "omega"` and V37/V44 on `measure %in% c("alpha","omega")`. Add a test pinning that a sheet with both alpha and icc, run at measure="alpha", emits no ICC-named flag.


## MINOR (41)

### Regression: the new tanh-based tetrachoric CI makes flag A6 fire a false [DISCORDANT] on ~5% of 2x2-derived correlation rows (0/120 on master)
`R/internal_flags.R:2232` — subsystem `2x2-core` — refuted by **0 of 3** verifiers

**Claim.** Commit 2b6f141 replaced the tetrachoric r-scale interval with the tanh back-transform of the Fisher-z interval (R/internal_multiple_formulas.R:1072). That interval is deliberately NOT of the form es +/- crit*se, so its half-width divided by se is no longer ~1.96. Flag A6 ('CI width inconsistent with SE', R/internal_flags.R:2232-2350) still compares every package-computed r row against 2*qt(.975, n-2)*se, with no exemption for info_used = '2x2'. B1b was updated for the change (the comment at :2429-2435 says so explicitly) but A6 was not — even though simulations/SOURCE-VERIFICATION.md §3 had predicted this exact consequence in advance ('would ... make A6 fire on every tetrachoric row (halfwidth/se = 2.4714 vs 1.9600)'). The predicted 2.4714 is reproduced exactly below.

**Evidence.** $ same 120 randomly generated 2x2 tables, run against each branch set.seed(42); N <- 120 # n per arm 20-300, event rates U(.05,.95) res <- summary(convert_df(dat, measure="r", verbose=FALSE), flags=TRUE) sum(grepl("CI width inconsistent", res$flags_crude)) master (git worktree @ 7b8eca7 v2.0.1) : A6 fired 0 / 120 = 0 % HEAD (dev/2.1.0 @ 7545c24) : A6 fired 6 / 120 = 5 % |r| of the fired rows: 0.075 0.118 0.776 0.834 0.957 0.981 # deterministic single-row reproduction, Bonett & Price 2005 Example 2, f=(4,6,1,89): d <- data.frame(study_id=c("bp2","x1","x2"), n_cases_exp=c(4,20,25), n_controls_exp=c(6,30,25), n_cases_nexp=c(1,10,20), n_controls_nexp=c(89,40,30)) r <- summary(convert_df(d, measure="r", verbose=FALSE), flags=TRUE) es_crude se_crude es_ci_lo_crude es_ci_up_crude 1 0.8641991 0.1090998 0.43418966 0.9734549 halfwidth/se = 2.471432 (rows 2,3: 1.914, 1.903) qt(.975, 98) = 1.984467 flag row1: "... [DISCORDANT] CI width inconsistent with SE: expected 0.433, actual 0.539 (from 2x2)"

**Impact.** Not a wrong effect size, but a false alarm in the diagnostic layer whose whole purpose is to be trusted: a reviewer following the flag column is told a correct, deliberately-constructed interval is inconsistent with its own SE, and the flag concentrates on the large-|r| rows that carry the most weight — exactly the studies a reviewer is most likely to drop or re-extract on the strength of a [DISCORDANT] tag. The prediction that this would happen is already written down in the repo, so shipping it unaddressed also weakens the audit trail.

**Fix.** In the A6 block (R/internal_flags.R:2232-2350) skip, or compute the expected width differently for, rows whose interval is a tanh back-transform. Concretely: when measure %in% c('r','rp') and info_used is a tetrachoric-producing route ('2x2', '2x2_sum', '2x2_prop', 'phi', 'chisq', 'chisq_pval' with a rebuilt table), set expected_width <- tanh(atanh(es)+z*se/(1-es^2)) - tanh(atanh(es)-z*se/(1-es^2)) instead of 2*z_crit*se. That is the interval's own definition, so the check stays live (a genuinely inconsistent tetrachoric CI still fires) rather than being switched off. Add a pin in tests/testthat/test-table-2x2-to-cor.R that A6 does not fire on f=(4,6,1,89).

**Verifier correction.** Flag A6 ("CI width inconsistent with SE", R/internal_flags.R:2232-2350) was not updated when commit 2b6f141 replaced the tetrachoric r-scale interval with the tanh back-transform of the Fisher-z interval (.tet_r, R/internal_multiple_formulas.R:1072-1078). A6 still compares every package-computed r row against 2*qt(.975, n-2)*se, so it emits a false [DISCORDANT] on tetrachoric rows whose deliberately asymmetric interval has half-width/se != 1.96. The new interval is correct by contract — tests_save/checked/test-2x2.R:279-288 pins it and documents the metafor divergence as intentional — and B1b's comment (internal_flags.R:2429-2435) was updated for the same change, so A6 is the sole un-updated consumer, in the same class as the existing P6/P7/P8/P9 construction-aware patches. Corrections to the filed version: - Prevalence is data-dependent, not "~5% of 2x2-derived rows": 5/120 on a uniform-event-rate random sweep, but 0/60 on the package's own metaumbrella::df.OR (ratios 1.913-1.954, inside tolerance). The deterministic Bonett & Price Example-2 case (24% width error) is a rare-cell/high-|r| extreme, not typical. - The flag fires at BOTH tails of |r|, not only on the high-weight large-|r| rows: half-width/se is below 1.96 for small |r| with a large SE and above it only for |r| > 1/sqrt(3) ~ 0.577 (fired |r| in my run: 0.046, 0.195, 0.838, 0.887, 0.957). The impact framing ("concentrates on the large-|r| rows a reviewer is most likely to drop") overstates this. - The inherited SOURCE-VERIFICATION §3 prediction that A6 would fire "on every tetrachoric row (halfwidth/se = 2.4714)" is itself an overstatement; ordinary tables sit at 1.88-2.11 and most stay inside A6's max(0.05, 0.30/sqrt(n)) tolerance. - Fix corrections: use qnorm(.975), NOT A6's z_crit = qt(.975, n-2), so the expectation is exact by construction; guard |es| = 1 (division by 1 - es^2); drop "rp" (partial correlations never route through .tet_r); and prefer adding the tanh width as an accepted ALTERNATIVE width for package-computed r rows — the pattern already used for the user-row t-width and the P6 md band — over an info_used whitelist, since convert_df() hardcodes phi_to_cor/chisq_to_cor to tetrachoric (they are not exposed as arguments) and the phi/chisq "lipsey" branches also build tanh intervals. - Scope is genuinely tetrachoric-only in practice: the other non-Wald r construction (or_to_cor bonett/pearson, r_lo = cos(pi/(1+or_ci_lo^c))) stays at ratios 1.939-1.985 across OR 1.2-20 and never fires.

### es_from_2x2_sum()/es_from_2x2_prop() have no guard on the derived control cells: an impossible table yields a plausible logRR with a finite SE and CI
`R/es_from_2x2.R:262` — subsystem `2x2-core` — refuted by **0 of 3** verifiers

**Claim.** es_from_2x2_sum() computes n_controls_exp = n_exp - n_cases_exp (:262) and n_controls_nexp = n_nexp - n_cases_nexp (:263) with no check that the result is non-negative; es_from_2x2_prop() does the same at :323-326 after round(prop*n). A negative control cell is arithmetically impossible, but the RR arm of es_from_2x2() never divides by it — logrr = log((a/n_exp)/(c/n_nexp)) uses only the row margins — so the route emits a fully populated, finite, plausible-looking log risk ratio with a finite SE and CI built from an 'exposed risk' greater than 1. CLAUDE.md's own R/internal_guards.R contract states the reason this matters: 'all 89 es_from_*() functions are exported, documented, exercised by their own examples, and called directly by metaumbrella — none of that path sees any validation', and 'the guard contains, and must be unconditional'. The guard sweep in tests/testthat/test-input-guards.R covers only dispersion and interval arguments, so the count family is outside it. Inside convert_df() with correct_inputs = TRUE the row is correctly NA'd and flagged by the part-total check, so the exposure is the direct-call path and the documented correct_inputs = FALSE inspection mode.

**Evidence.** $ Rscript (devtools::load_all(".")) > es_from_2x2_sum(n_cases_exp=50, n_exp=40, n_cases_nexp=10, n_nexp=40) logor logor_se logrr logrr_se logrr_ci_lo logrr_ci_up d d_se g rd rd_se nnt 1 NaN 0.2309401 1.609438 0.2645751 1.09088 2.127996 NaN 0.127324 NA -1 NaN -1 (only warning raised: base R "NaNs produced" from sqrt() in the RD SE) logrr = log((50/40)/(10/40)) = log(5) = 1.609438 -- an "exposed risk" of 1.25. The nearest legitimate reading of the same numbers (30 cases of 40) gives log((30/40)/(10/40)) = 1.098612, so the emitted value is 0.51 log units (a factor of 1.65 on the RR scale) away, with a CI that excludes the truth-adjacent value's region only by accident. Same shape for a proportion entered as a percentage: > es_from_2x2_prop(prop_cases_exp=80, prop_cases_nexp=60, n_exp=10, n_nexp=20) logor logor_se rd rd_se nnt 1 -0.004228336 NaN -20 NaN -0.05 Through convert_df(): correct_inputs=TRUE -> es NA + "[INVALID] Inconsistent input: 'n_cases_exp' > 'n_exp', both set to missing" (correct). correct_inputs=FALSE -> es_crude 1.609438, se_crude 0.2645751, flagged [INVALID] but emitted.

**Impact.** A direct call (the metaumbrella path, and the path every worked example in ?es_from_2x2_sum takes) returns a finite, correctly-signed, correctly-scaled-looking log risk ratio and standard error from a table that cannot exist. Because logor comes back NaN, an OR-targeted pipeline is safe, but a logrr/rr- or rd-targeted one is not: the row enters a pool as an ordinary study. Magnitude on the demonstrated input is 0.51 log units.

**Fix.** Add a count guard mirroring .positive_or_na() in R/internal_guards.R — e.g. .nonneg_cell_or_na(part, total) returning NA for both when part > total or either is negative — and apply it in es_from_2x2_sum() before the es_from_2x2() call (:260-266) and in es_from_2x2_prop() after the round() at :323-326 (also catching prop outside [0,1]). Then extend tests/testthat/test-input-guards.R's sweep to the three count-based 2x2 entry points so the case is pinned.

**Verifier correction.** The three 2x2 count entry points have no guard on negative/impossible cells, and the failure is not confined to direct calls: `es_from_2x2_prop()` is exposed on the DEFAULT `convert_df()` path. Mechanism (as filed, confirmed): `es_from_2x2_sum()` derives `n_controls_exp = n_exp - n_cases_exp` (R/es_from_2x2.R:262-263) and `es_from_2x2_prop()` the same after `round(prop * n)` (:323-326), with no non-negativity check. The RR arm of `es_from_2x2()` uses only the row margins — `rr = (a/n1)/(c/n2)`, `se_rr = sqrt(1/a - 1/n1 + 1/c - 1/n2)` — so it emits a finite, correctly-signed logRR with a finite SE and CI from a table that cannot exist. Only the OR arm goes NaN, because it alone divides by the negative cell. Corrected scope — three entry points, not two. `es_from_2x2()` itself is exported and equally unguarded: `es_from_2x2(41, -1, 20, 20)` returns logrr 0.7178 (se 0.1562), rd -0.525 (se 0.0749), nnt -1.905 (se 0.2717). Corrected scope — the pipeline is NOT safe for the prop route. The finding's statement that `convert_df(correct_inputs = TRUE)` catches this is true only for `es_from_2x2_sum` (whose `n_cases_exp`/`n_exp` are real columns seen by the part-total check). `prop_cases_exp`/`prop_cases_nexp` are absent from BOTH `.positive_columns()` and `.bounded_columns` in R/internal_flags.R, and the impossible count is manufactured inside the route by `round(prop * n)`, which no column-keyed Tier-1 check can observe. Default settings: convert_df(data.frame(prop_cases_exp = 1.2, prop_cases_nexp = 0.05, n_exp = 100, n_nexp = 100), measure = "rr") -> es_crude = 24, se_crude = 0.4340, info_used_crude = "2x2_prop", flags_crude = "" (no flag). The row is selected and pooled as an ordinary study. The trigger is a proportion entered as a percentage — the same error class the package explicitly guards for `baseline_risk` (`.baseline_risk_or_na()` plus a `.bounded_columns` entry commented "must be a proportion (0-1), not a percentage") and for `es_from_prop_single_group()` (prop outside [0,1] -> NA + per-row warning). The @param for `prop_cases_exp` already says "ranging from 0 to 1"; it is documented and unenforced. Corrected severity: major, not minor — driven by the unflagged default-path prop exposure, not by the direct-call path. The `es_from_2x2_sum` half alone is genuinely minor (correctly NA'd and `[INVALID]`-flagged under `correct_inputs = TRUE`). Corrected magnitude. The RD/NNT arm is NOT exposed on the finding's demonstrated input (50 of 40): `pt(1-pt) < 0` makes `rd_se` NaN and the row is dropped. RD/NNT exposure is real but arises for MILD overshoots, where the control-arm variance term outweighs the negative exposed-arm one (41 of 40 -> rd = -0.525 with finite se 0.0749, |rd| < 1 so flag B6 stays silent). The "0.51 log units" figure is anchored to an invented alternative reading of the counts and should be dropped: the emitted quantity is not a risk ratio at all (implied exposed risk > 1) and the error is unbounded. Corrected fix. (a) Put the containment in `es_from_2x2()` — any of the four cells negative -> that row's outputs NA — so all three entry points are covered from the single audited place R/internal_guards.R's own header calls for, rather than patching the two wrappers and leaving the exported parent open. (b) In `es_from_2x2_prop()`, additionally neutralise `prop_cases_exp`/`prop_cases_nexp` outside [0,1] BEFORE the `round()`, following `es_from_prop_single_group()`'s existing convention of a per-row warning rather than a silent NA or a hard stop. (c) Add `prop_cases_exp`/`prop_cases_nexp` to `.bounded_columns` in R/internal_flags.R so the default `convert_df()` path reports as well as contains — the guard alone would turn a silent wrong number into a silent NA, which is not the package's stated division of labour ("the flag reports; the guard contains"). (d) Extend the `tests/testthat/test-input-guards.R` sweep to the count/proportion family so the case is pinned.

### `or <= 0` is unguarded: a log-OR typed into the `or` column returns a plausible risk difference and NNT plus a NEGATIVE risk-difference standard error
`R/es_from_stand_OR.R:562` — subsystem `or-conversions` — refuted by **0 of 3** verifiers

**Claim.** `es_from_or_se()` guards `baseline_risk` (`.baseline_risk_or_na`) and `logor_se` (`.positive_or_na`) but applies no positivity guard to `or` itself, and `or` is in neither `.positive_columns()` nor `.bounded_columns()`. The log-OR/OR path self-blanks (log of a negative is NaN, so d/g/r/z come back NaN/NA), but the Grant risk-difference block at lines 562-575 is pure arithmetic in `or` and stays finite: `treatment_risk = or*BR/(1-BR+or*BR)` becomes negative, `rd = BR - treatment_risk` exceeds `BR`, and `rd_se = |drd_dor| * or * logor_se` inherits the sign of `or` and comes out NEGATIVE. Entering a log odds ratio into the `or` column is a realistic mix-up (the two columns sit side by side in `data_extraction_sheet()`), and for a protective effect it produces a negative value.

**Evidence.** R> suppressMessages(devtools::load_all('.'))
