R> es_from_or_se(or=-0.7, logor_se=0.2, baseline_risk=0.3, n_cases=50, n_controls=150) logor = NaN d = NaN rd = 0.72857143 rd_se = -0.12244898 nnt = 1.372549 Hand-check: treatment_risk = -0.7*0.3/(0.7 - 0.21) = -0.4285714 (a negative risk), rd = 0.3 - (-0.42857) = 0.72857, which also exceeds the baseline risk it is measured from. rd_se < 0 is a negative sampling standard deviation carried straight to the output. A second, more extreme case: R> es_from_or_se(or=-2, logor_se=0.2, baseline_risk=0.2, n_cases=50, n_controls=50) logor = NaN d = NaN r = NaN rd = 1.2 nnt = 0.833333 rd = 1.2 is outside [-1, 1] and nnt = 0.83 is inside (0,1); both are arithmetically impossible. (Tier-2 flags B4/B5/B6 do catch these two, but only under flags = TRUE, and the or = -0.7 case above yields rd = 0.73 / nnt = 1.37, which B6 does not catch at all.)

**Impact.** A single mis-keyed cell (log OR entered as OR) yields a risk difference and NNT that look entirely publishable, together with a negative RD standard error that becomes a wrong-signed inverse-variance weight in `rma()`. Every effect-size measure that would reveal the error (logor, d, g, r, z) is correctly blanked, so nothing in the default output points at the row.

**Fix.** In each of the five entry points that already call `.baseline_risk_or_na(baseline_risk)`, add `or <- ifelse(!is.na(or) & (!is.finite(or) | or <= 0), NA_real_, or)` (a new `.or_or_na()` helper in R/internal_guards.R, alongside `.positive_or_na`), and add `list(col = "or", lo = 0, up = Inf, ...)` — or simply "or", "rr", "irr", "hr" to `.positive_columns()` — so convert_df blanks it too. The same argument applies to `rr`/`irr`/`hr`.

**Verifier correction.** `or` (and `rr`) receive no positivity guard on entry to the standalone ratio routes, contrary to the policy stated in R/internal_guards.R. An arithmetically impossible `or <= 0` -- realistically, a log OR typed into the adjacent `or` column, which is negative for any protective effect -- self-blanks the log-scale outputs (logor/d/g/r/z all NaN, and those rows are dropped by summary()), but the Grant (2014) risk-difference block at R/es_from_stand_OR.R:562-575 is pure arithmetic in `or` and stays finite. For `measure = "rd"` or `"nnt"` only, the row survives into the summary carrying a fabricated point estimate (rd = 0.729 for or = -0.7, BR = 0.3), a NEGATIVE rd_se/nnt_se (the delta line `abs(drd_dor) * or * logor_se` omits the abs() on the `or` factor), and a transposed CI. `es_from_rr_se()` has the identical defect (rr = -0.5 -> rd = 0.45, rd_se = -0.03). Corrected impact: the negative SE does NOT become a wrong-signed inverse-variance weight -- metafor::rma(sei=) squares it, so the row enters with a positive, normal-magnitude weight (13.4% in a two-study FE fit) attached to a nonsense estimate, moving the pooled RD from -0.107 to +0.0055. The transposed CI and the fabricated point estimate are the real harm. Corrected detectability: the row is not fully silent. Base R emits "In log(or) : NaNs produced", and under `flags = TRUE` Category A already raises "[INVALID] Negative SE" and "[INVALID] Inverted CI" on the or = -0.7 case (plus B5 for nnt). The gap is confined to the default `flags = FALSE`. Corrected fix: add a `.or_or_na()`-style guard testing `or <= 0` (and non-finite) at the five `es_from_or_*` entry points that already call `.baseline_risk_or_na()`, and the matching one for `rr`. Do NOT implement this by adding "or"/"rr" to `.positive_columns()`: that list is checked with `< 0`, which misses `or = 0` -- and `or = 0` is the worse case, returning rd = 0.30 with rd_se = 0, i.e. an infinite inverse-variance weight. Also drop "irr"/"hr" from the proposed fix: neither is an input column in data_extraction_sheet(), so there is nothing to guard. Severity: minor (unchanged). Requires a data-entry error, silent only under the default flags = FALSE, and only for 2 of the 14 measures.

### `.se_from_or()` returns NaN rather than NA when the case margin is 0 or 1, propagating a NaN standard error to logor_se
`R/internal_multiple_formulas.R:705` — subsystem `or-conversions` — refuted by **0 of 3** verifiers

**Claim.** `.se_from_or()` builds its enumeration as `cases_exp <- 1:(n_cases - 1)`. At `n_cases = 1` this is `1:0 = c(1, 0)`, so `cases_nexp = c(0, 1)` and the term `1/cases_nexp` contains `1/0 = Inf`; at `n_cases = 0` it is `1:-1 = c(1, 0, -1)`. The `na.rm = TRUE` in the `mean()` does not remove Inf/NaN, so the imputed variance comes back NaN. `.positive_or_na()` in `es_from_or_se()` leaves NaN unchanged (`is.na(NaN)` is TRUE, so the `x <= 0` branch is not taken), and NaN reaches `es$logor_se`, `es$logor_ci_lo` and `es$logor_ci_up`. `d`/`g`/`r`/`z` are blanked correctly by the `row_miss` block, but the log-OR columns carry NaN rather than NA.

**Evidence.** R> suppressMessages(devtools::load_all('.')) R> for (nc in c(0,1,2,3,10)) cat(nc, es_from_or(or=2, n_cases=nc, n_controls=50)$logor_se, "\n") 0 NaN 1 NaN 2 1.445381112 3 1.26589889 10 0.8770824383 No warning or message is emitted for the NaN rows.

**Impact.** Small: a single-case study is rare, `is.na(NaN)` is TRUE so downstream `summary()` and `rma()` drop the row as they would for NA, and no wrong number is published. It is a tidiness/consistency defect rather than a numerical one — every other guard in the package returns NA_real_ — and a NaN in an exported column is the sort of thing an R CMD check example or a user's `is.na()` audit will surface inconsistently.

**Fix.** In `.se_from_or()`, return the all-NA `data.frame(value = NA, var = NA, se = NA)` when `n_cases < 2 || n_controls < 2`, and finish with `if (!is.finite(v_or_mean)) return(res)` before assigning, so a non-finite enumeration average never leaves the function.

**Verifier correction.** `.se_from_or()` (R/internal_multiple_formulas.R:696-716) returns `NaN` rather than `NA_real_` whenever its enumeration of candidate 2×2 tables comes back empty, and the NaN propagates unguarded to `logor_se` / `logor_ci_lo` / `logor_ci_up` beside a finite `logor`. Mechanism (corrected): the NaN is `mean(numeric(0))`, not a surviving `Inf`. All candidates are NA'd by the feasibility filter `controls_exp[controls_exp < 1 | controls_exp > n_controls - 1] <- NA`; the `1/0 = Inf` terms the original claim blames are annihilated by `Inf + NA = NA` and correctly stripped by `na.rm = TRUE`. Verified over 27,848 (or, n_cases, n_controls) triples with both margins >= 2: no non-finite term ever survives into a non-empty enumeration. Scope (corrected): the trigger is "empty enumeration", which is wider than `n_cases <= 1`. It also fires for `n_controls <= 1` (`es_from_or(or=2, n_cases=50, n_controls=1)` → NaN) and for legal-but-small margins with an extreme odds ratio (`or=100, n_cases=5, n_controls=5` → NaN, because every candidate `controls_exp` rounds to 0). Conversely, the NaN is confined to `es_from_or()`'s direct return value and to the intermediate `convert_df()` object (`$es_odds_ratio`): `summary()` blanks the row to `es = NA, se = NA` and counts it under "Missing ES in input rows", so no NaN reaches the summary output and no wrong number is ever published. The affected region is empirically negligible (0 NaN across 224 triples with n_cases >= 4, n_controls >= 20, OR in [0.1, 30]). Why it is still worth fixing: `.positive_or_na()` cannot catch it (`is.na(NaN)` is TRUE, so the `x <= 0` branch is skipped), no warning is emitted, and HEAD's NEWS already establishes the opposite convention for the structurally identical `rr_to_or = "grant"` failure in the same file ("a finite estimate with no usable variance is worse than none"). `.se_from_or()` is the last silent instance of that class. Fix (endorsed, with one simplification): the `if (!is.finite(v_or_mean)) return(res)` clause is sufficient and load-bearing; the `n_cases < 2 || n_controls < 2` short-circuit is a strict subset of it and can be dropped. The guard provably cannot blank a legitimate row (with a non-empty enumeration every term is bounded, so `v_or_mean` is finite by construction). Do NOT extend the fix to blanking `logor` as the grant repair did — there the point estimate was itself out of domain, whereas here the OR is the user's own input, and keeping `es` with `se = NA` is the documented convention (cf. bare omega).

### B3 "NNT magnitude < 1 (mathematically impossible)" is not rate-aware and fires [INVALID] on the package's own person-time NNT
`R/internal_flags.R:2483` — subsystem `rr-rd-nnt` — refuted by **0 of 3** verifiers

**Claim.** B3's comment asserts "mathematically impossible: cannot treat fewer than one person to prevent one event". That is true for a risk-based NNT (|RD| <= 1 forces |NNT| >= 1) but false for the person-time NNT the package itself computes via `es_from_cases_time()` (Mayne 2006): there the NNT is a DURATION (person-time per event prevented), and any incidence rate difference above 1 event per unit time gives |NNT| < 1. The check does not consult `info_used`, so `cases_time` rows are judged by the risk-based rule.

**Evidence.** R (Rscript 4.5.0, devtools::load_all('.')): df <- data.frame(study_id="B", n_cases_exp=500, n_cases_nexp=10, time_exp=100, time_nexp=100, baseline_rate=0.1) d <- as.data.frame(summary(convert_df(df, measure="nnt", verbose=FALSE), flags=TRUE)) OUTPUT: info_used_crude es_crude se_crude 1 cases_time -0.2040816 0.06650862 FLAG contains: "[INVALID] NNT magnitude < 1: NNT = -0.204 (from cases_time)" And for a benefit-side rate example (n_cases_exp=30,time_exp=10,n_cases_nexp=60,time_nexp=10, measure="nnt"): es = 0.3333 person-years, flagged "[INVALID] NNT magnitude < 1: NNT = 0.333 (from cases_time)". Derivation: rate_nexp = 6/py, rate_exp = 3/py, IRD = 3/py, NNT = 1/3 = 0.333 person-years -- a valid Mayne person-time NNT. That B5 immediately below (line 2513) DOES carry a dedicated `baseline_rate` branch shows the rate-based NNT is a recognised concept in this file; B3 simply does not use it.

**Impact.** Any person-time NNT with an IRD above 1 event per unit of person-time is stamped `[INVALID]`. The value is correct; the verdict is not. Combined with finding 2 the same row can collect two contradictory false `[INVALID]` tags, which erodes trust in the flag layer generally.

**Fix.** Gate B3 on the route: `if (measure == "nnt" && !identical(info_used[i], "cases_time") && is.finite(es[i]) && abs(es[i]) < 1 && es[i] != 0)`. `info_used` is already threaded into `.flag_bounds_violations()`'s caller for `.method_suffix()`. The pinned test tests_save/checked/test-flags.R:118-130 calls `.flag_bounds_violations()` without `info_used`, so it keeps passing under a `NULL`/absent default.

**Verifier correction.** Real, minor, correctly diagnosed -- but scoped too narrowly to B3, and the proposed one-line fix leaves a gap. The defect is that the WHOLE family of risk-scale bounds checks in .flag_bounds_violations() is applied to the rate-scale outputs of the single cases_time route, not just B3: (1) B3 (line 2483) flags a valid person-time NNT < 1 as [INVALID], as filed. Additionally, B3 is unreachable from every risk-based route (an extreme 2x2 of 1/99 vs 99/1 gives NNT = 1.0204), so on package-computed rows B3 fires only where it is wrong; it retains a legitimate purpose only for user-entered NNT. (2) The SAME route writes the incidence rate difference into the rd columns (R/es_from_stand_IRR.R:85), so measure = "rd" on the identical data emits three further false [INVALID] tags from B6/B6b (lines 2521-2545): "[INVALID] RD outside [-1, 1]: rd = 3 (from cases_time); [INVALID] RD CI bound outside [-1, 1]: CI lower = 1.141, CI upper = 4.859". This is arguably the more consequential half, since rd is a directly poolable measure. The finding does not mention it. The fix should therefore be a shared route-aware gate covering B3, B6 and B6b -- reusing the rate-method definition E4 already carries at line 3754 (rate_methods <- "cases_time") rather than a fresh inline literal -- so the three checks stay active for risk-scale rows and stand down for rate-scale ones. One caveat on the fix as proposed: B5's person-time branch reads baseline_rate only from the raw input column (line 3638), while es_from_cases_time() auto-computes it as n_cases_nexp/time_nexp when the column is absent. Simply suppressing B3 on cases_time rows therefore leaves auto-computed rows with NO lower-bound check at all. Surfacing the effective (auto-computed) baseline_rate to the flag layer would preserve the correct rate-scale bound |NNT_pt| >= 1/baseline_rate in place of the removed constant. (Separately, and outside this finding: B5's abs(es) comparison makes that bound invalid on the harm side, where irr > 1 leaves |IRD| unbounded.)

### `max_asymmetry` is a documented argument of es_from_rr_ci() and es_from_or_ci() that the function bodies never read
`R/es_from_stand_RR.R:277` — subsystem `rr-rd-nnt` — refuted by **0 of 3** verifiers

**Claim.** Both `es_from_rr_ci()` (R/es_from_stand_RR.R:277) and `es_from_or_ci()` (R/es_from_stand_OR.R:783) take `max_asymmetry = 10` and document it (`@param max_asymmetry A percentage indicating the tolerance before detecting asymmetry in the 95% CI bounds`, RR:241 / OR:745). Neither body references the variable anywhere: `awk '/^es_from_rr_ci <- function/,/^}/' | grep -c max_asymmetry` returns 1 (the signature line only), likewise for `es_from_or_ci`. `convert_df()` even forwards `max_asymmetry = max_asymmetry` to `es_from_or_ci()` (R/main_convert_df.R:719), so the user-facing argument is plumbed all the way to a dead end. CI asymmetry is now handled centrally by Tier-1 `.validate_input_data()` (per CLAUDE.md, "the old per-function .validate_ci_symmetry() hard stops have been removed"); these two signatures are the leftover.

**Evidence.** R (Rscript 4.5.0, devtools::load_all('.')): # a grossly asymmetric CI on the log scale: log(1.0)=0, log(2)=0.693, log(9)=2.197 a <- es_from_rr_ci(rr=2, rr_ci_lo=1.0, rr_ci_up=9.0, baseline_risk=0.1, max_asymmetry=0.001) b <- es_from_rr_ci(rr=2, rr_ci_lo=1.0, rr_ci_up=9.0, baseline_risk=0.1, max_asymmetry=1e9) identical: TRUE logrr_se: 0.5605268 a2 <- es_from_or_ci(or=2, or_ci_lo=1.0, or_ci_up=9.0, n_cases=50, n_controls=60, max_asymmetry=0.001) b2 <- es_from_or_ci(or=2, or_ci_lo=1.0, or_ci_up=9.0, n_cases=50, n_controls=60, max_asymmetry=1e9) identical(or): TRUE Static confirmation: $ awk '/^es_from_rr_ci <- function/,/^}/' R/es_from_stand_RR.R | grep -c max_asymmetry 1 $ awk '/^es_from_or_ci <- function/,/^}/' R/es_from_stand_OR.R | grep -c max_asymmetry 1

**Impact.** A user who tightens `max_asymmetry` on a direct call to these two exported routes believes they have armed an asymmetry check and gets nothing -- silent absence of a guard they were told exists. No wrong number, but it is CRAN-visible documentation that the code does not implement, and the argument is the kind a careful analyst would rely on when feeding exact/Newcombe intervals into a Wald-width back-calculation.

**Fix.** Either delete `max_asymmetry` from both signatures and their `@param` blocks (and drop the forwarding at R/main_convert_df.R:719), or wire it to the shared asymmetry check. Deleting is the smaller change and matches the centralisation already documented in CLAUDE.md; note it in NEWS.md since it is a signature change on two exported functions.

**Verifier correction.** Three exported functions -- es_from_rr_ci() (R/es_from_stand_RR.R:241/:277), es_from_or_ci() (R/es_from_stand_OR.R:745/:783) and es_from_means_ci() (R/es_from_ANOVA_means.R:349/:391) -- accept and @param-document max_asymmetry while never reading it, so a direct caller who tightens the tolerance gets no check (verified: identical() == TRUE and no warning between max_asymmetry = 0.001 and 1e9). convert_df() forwards the inert argument to all three (R/main_convert_df.R:719 OR, :783 means_ci, :1301 RR). Users of convert_df() are NOT exposed: .ci_triplets() registers or/rr at scale = "exp" (R/internal_flags.R:412-413) and V4 log-transforms before measuring asymmetry, so the central Tier-1 check covers that path; the gap is direct calls only. This is a documentation/API-hygiene defect with no numeric consequence -- severity doc, not minor. The correct fix is NOT deletion (a breaking signature change on three exported CRAN functions that would also require removing all three convert_df forwardings in the same commit), but the non-breaking wording already used by the maintainer in this working tree at R/es_from_stand_MD.R:142: "@param max_asymmetry not used by this function; asymmetry of the 95% CI bounds is checked by \code{\link{convert_df}} (see its \code{max_asymmetry} argument)." Apply that text to the three @param blocks and regenerate the corresponding .Rd files; no NEWS entry is needed since the API is unchanged.

### baseline_risk = 0 yields rd = 0 with rd_se = 0 (an infinite inverse-variance weight) from the OR and RR routes
`R/es_from_stand_OR.R:560` — subsystem `rr-rd-nnt` — refuted by **0 of 3** verifiers

**Claim.** `.baseline_risk_or_na()` (R/internal_guards.R:74-77) nulls `baseline_risk` only for `x < 0` or `x >= 1`, so `baseline_risk = 0` is admitted. At BR = 0 the Grant chain gives `treatment_risk = 0`, `rd = 0`, and `drd_dor = BR*(1-BR)/denom^2 = 0`, so `rd_se = |drd_dor| * or * logor_se = 0` exactly (R/es_from_stand_OR.R:559-561). The RR route has the identical structure (`rd_se = baseline_risk * rr * logrr_se`, R/es_from_stand_RR.R:207). A zero standard error is precisely what the sibling guard `.positive_or_na()` exists to prevent elsewhere in this same file family (it is applied to `rd_se` in `es_from_rd_se()` but not to the DERIVED `rd_se` here), and it is a zero sampling variance -- an infinite meta-analytic weight, or an `rma()` abort.

**Evidence.** R (Rscript 4.5.0, devtools::load_all('.')): es_from_or_se(or=0.5, logor_se=0.2, baseline_risk=0)[,c("rd","rd_se")] es_from_rr_se(rr=0.5, logrr_se=0.2, baseline_risk=0)[,c("rd","rd_se")] rd rd_se or 0 0 rr 0 0 End to end through the pipeline: df <- data.frame(study_id=c("A","B"), or=c(0.5,0.6), logor_se=c(0.2,0.2), baseline_risk=c(0, 0.3), n_cases=c(50,50), n_controls=c(50,50)) as.data.frame(summary(convert_df(df, measure="rd", verbose=FALSE), flags=TRUE)) info_used_crude es_crude se_crude 1 or_se 0.00000000 0.00000000 2 or_se 0.09545455 0.03254132 flags_crude[1]: "[INVALID] SE is zero (from or_se)" So the row IS selected and returned with se = 0; it is caught only by the C2 post-hoc flag, not neutralised at source. metafor::rma() on a vi = 0 study aborts with "Division by zero when computing the inverse variance weights".

**Impact.** Bounded: BR = 0 means a control arm with zero events, which is a real but uncommon input, and C2 does raise `[INVALID] SE is zero`. Still, a user who does not read flags feeds a zero-variance study into `rma()`. The point estimate rd = 0 is also arguably not the right answer -- with zero control events the Grant transform is degenerate, not exactly null.

**Fix.** Either narrow the guard to `x <= 0 | x >= 1` in `.baseline_risk_or_na()` (R/internal_guards.R:76) so BR = 0 becomes NA like BR = 1, or pass the derived `rd_se` through `.positive_or_na()` at R/es_from_stand_OR.R:561 and R/es_from_stand_RR.R:207 so that a zero SE nulls the row's rd/nnt rather than shipping an infinite weight. The second is the more conservative change and matches the existing treatment of `rd_se` in `es_from_rd_se()` (R/es_from_stand_RD.R:82-85).

**Verifier correction.** A derived rd_se of exactly 0 (zero sampling variance) can reach the output and be selected by summary(). It arises wherever the risk-difference SE is built from a degenerate risk pair: (a) es_from_or_se/es_from_or_ci/es_from_or_pval and es_from_rr_* with baseline_risk = 0, where drd_dor = BR(1-BR)/denom^2 = 0 (R/es_from_stand_OR.R:559-561) and rd_se = BR*rr*logrr_se = 0 (R/es_from_stand_RR.R:206); and ALSO (b) es_from_2x2() on a double-zero or double-full table, where RD_SE = sqrt(pt(1-pt)/n1 + pc(1-pc)/n2) = 0. The finding's restriction to the OR/RR routes is too narrow. Given the delta method's own assumption that baseline_risk is a known constant, rd = 0 IS the correct conditional point estimate at BR = 0; the defect is emitting it with se = 0 as a poolable row rather than declining it. metafor never does this: escalc(measure="RD") on a double-zero table returns a continuity-corrected vi = 0.0004 by default, or NA with drop00 = TRUE. Consequence is bounded and loud, not silent: metaConvert's C2 raises "[INVALID] SE is zero", metafor::rma(method="FE") aborts, and method="REML" runs with warnings, giving the row weight 1/tau^2 and suppressing Q/I^2/H^2. Severity minor is correct. The fix must be the SECOND option only: pass the derived rd_se through .positive_or_na() at the point of emission (R/es_from_stand_OR.R:561, R/es_from_stand_RR.R:206, and the 2x2 RD SE), so the row's rd/nnt drop out. The FIRST option in the finding -- narrowing .baseline_risk_or_na() to x <= 0 (R/internal_guards.R:76) -- is wrong and must not be applied: baseline_risk = 0 is deliberately kept as the rare-disease limit where the Grant conversions become the identity (verified logrr = log(or) with SE preserved) and is pinned by tests/testthat/test-baseline-risk-guard.R:90.

### NNT confidence bound returns +/-Inf instead of NA when a risk-difference CI limit is exactly 0 (strict inequality in the Altman crossing test)
`R/es_from_stand_RD.R:158` — subsystem `rr-rd-nnt` — refuted by **0 of 3** verifiers

**Claim.** All five NNT-producing routes test the Altman (1998) discontinuity with strict inequalities: `crosses_zero <- (rd_ci_lo_raw < 0 & rd_ci_up_raw > 0)` (R/es_from_stand_RD.R:158, R/es_from_stand_RR.R:216, R/es_from_stand_OR.R:571, R/es_from_stand_IRR.R:102, R/es_from_2x2.R:207). When a bound lands exactly on 0 the test is FALSE, the guard is skipped, and the reciprocal produces a literal `Inf` in a CI column -- while the immediately adjacent case (bound epsilon below 0) returns NA. The two neighbouring inputs get categorically different output types.

**Evidence.** R (Rscript 4.5.0, devtools::load_all('.')): rd <- 0.2; se <- 0.2/qnorm(.975) # makes rd_ci_lo exactly 0 es_from_rd_se(rd=rd, rd_se=se)[,c('rd','rd_se','nnt','nnt_se','nnt_ci_lo','nnt_ci_up')] rd rd_se nnt nnt_se nnt_ci_lo nnt_ci_up 1 0.2 0.1020427 5 2.551067 2.5 Inf rd_ci_lo = 0 With se nudged up so the bound goes negative, the same call returns nnt_ci_lo = NA, nnt_ci_up = NA (the documented Altman behaviour).

**Impact.** Small and boundary-measure-zero for continuous inputs, but reachable from rounded reported data (e.g. a source giving rd = 0.20 with a CI whose lower limit is printed as 0.00, or the p = 0.05 route). `Inf` in `es_ci_up` is not NA, so it survives downstream filtering, prints as "Inf" in a forest plot table, and can propagate into `range()`/`max()` diagnostics. Mathematically Inf is the correct Altman limit at that boundary, so this is an internal-consistency defect rather than a wrong number.

**Fix.** Use non-strict inequalities in all five routes: `crosses_zero <- (rd_ci_lo_raw <= 0 & rd_ci_up_raw >= 0) | rd == 0` (and the `ird` analogue in R/es_from_stand_IRR.R:102). This makes the boundary case return NA like its neighbours, consistent with how the package already reports the disjoint interval.

**Verifier correction.** All five NNT-producing routes test the Altman (1998) discontinuity with strict inequalities (`rd_ci_lo_raw < 0 & rd_ci_up_raw > 0`) at R/es_from_stand_RD.R:158, RR.R:216, OR.R:571, IRR.R:102 and 2x2.R:207. When a risk-difference bound lands exactly on 0 the guard is skipped and the reciprocal yields a literal Inf, where the neighbouring input (bound epsilon below 0) returns NA. Corrections to the filed version: 1. SCOPE IS WIDER THAN "boundary-measure-zero" for the entry paths that matter. `es_from_rd_ci()` and `es_from_rd_pval()` recompute the SE from the reported interval, so the standard reporting pattern "RD 0.20, 95% CI 0.00 to 0.40" (symmetric Wald CI with lower limit printed as 0.00) hits the exact boundary in 48/49 grid cases (up = 0.02..0.98, rd = up/2), and rd_pval = 0.05 hits in 17/60. It is the generic outcome of a common reporting pattern for those two routes. The 2x2/OR/RR/IRR delta-method routes are effectively unreachable, and `.check_data()`'s as.numeric(as.character()) round-trip perturbs a user-supplied `rd_se` column off the boundary (measured -3.33e-16), so the practically affected paths are es_from_rd_ci, es_from_rd_pval, and direct calls to the exported routes (the path metaumbrella uses). 2. THE NEGATIVE-RD SUB-CASE IS A WRONG NUMBER, NOT ONLY A TYPE INCONSISTENCY. "Inf is the mathematically correct Altman limit" holds only for RD > 0. For a directly entered negative RD (reverse_rd = FALSE) the code computes nnt_ci_lo = 1/rd_ci_up_raw = 1/(+0) = +Inf, whereas the confidence set is (-inf, -2.5] and the limit is -inf. Verified: es_from_rd_se(rd = -0.2, rd_se = 0.2/qnorm(.975)) returns nnt_ci_lo = Inf, nnt_ci_up = -2.5 -- an inverted interval (lo > up) with a sign-wrong bound. Under reverse_rd = TRUE the signs come out correct, which is why the sub-case was missed. 3. DOWNSTREAM REACH IS NARROWER THAN CLAIMED IN ONE RESPECT. For the RD routes the selected top-level `es_ci_up_crude` is NA regardless, because NNT's SE exemption makes the hierarchy select the all-NA `es_rd_se` slot ahead of `es_rd_ci`/`es_rd_pval`; so "prints as Inf in a forest plot table" does not apply to the selected CI columns. Inf does reach the user in `min_es_ci_up_crude` / `max_es_ci_up_crude` in summary() output, in the per-route data frames of the metaConvert object, and in the return value of direct route calls. The proposed fix is correct and safe as filed -- relax to `(rd_ci_lo_raw <= 0 & rd_ci_up_raw >= 0) | rd == 0` (and the `ird` analogue in IRR) -- with the note that four of the five routes already carry the `| rd == 0` clause and only 2x2.R has it written inline in the two ifelse() calls. No test pins the Inf; the archived Altman test in tests_save/checked/test-ES-NNT-RD.R uses a strictly-crossing 2x2 and is unaffected. Severity minor is confirmed: point estimate and SE are unaffected, pooling is unaffected, and the consequence is a wrong type (plus a wrong-signed inverted bound in the negative-RD sub-case) in diagnostic CI columns.

### REGRESSION (commit 1eae59c): the paired-t/F family under `pre_post_to_smd = "morris_dz"` silently returns NA when |r_pre_post| >= 1, although d_z is r-free
`R/internal_multiple_formulas.R:1819` — subsystem `prepost-two-group` — refuted by **0 of 3** verifiers

**Claim.** Commit 1eae59c replaced the inline `d_dz <- paired_t_exp / sqrt(n_exp)` in `es_from_paired_t()` / `es_from_paired_t_single_group()` with a delegation to `.single_group_pre_post_to_smd()` via `.paired_t_to_smd()`. The kernel is handed `mean_pre_sd = 0`, `mean_post_sd = 1`, and computes `sd_diff <- sqrt(mean_pre_sd^2 + mean_post_sd^2 - 2 * r_pre_post * mean_pre_sd * mean_post_sd)`. The r-term is identically zero because `mean_pre_sd = 0`, but in R `0 * NA` is `NA`, so when `.guard_r_pre_post()` has NA'd an invalid correlation (|r| >= 1) the whole radicand becomes NA and the route returns NA for d, d_se, g, g_se and the CIs. The package's own guidance text (R/internal_multiple_formulas.R:2045) states "'morris_dz': change-SD standardizer. Independent of r". Master's code path had no r in the dz branch at all, so it returned the correct value. Through `convert_df()` the row is dropped from the pool with NO flag (V11 bounds r_pre_post at [-1,1], so r = 1 exactly passes input validation). The same `0 * NA` poisoning is pre-existing (not a regression) on the mean-change wrappers, which use the same zero-pre-slot convention.

**Evidence.** $ Rscript -e "devtools::load_all('.'); for (rv in c(0.6,0.99,1)) {e<-es_from_paired_t(5.1,2.2,30,28,rv,rv,pre_post_to_smd='morris_dz'); cat('r =',rv,' d =',e$d,' g =',e$g,' g_se =',e$g_se,'\n')}" r = 0.6 d = 0.5153674 g = 0.5027168 g_se = 0.2926914 r = 0.99 d = 0.5153674 g = 0.5027168 g_se = 0.2926914 r = 1 d = NA g = NA g_se = NA master's formula (from `git diff master...HEAD -- R/es_from_PAIRED_STATISTICS.R`): d_exp_dz <- paired_t_exp / sqrt(n_exp) # no r anywhere 5.1/sqrt(30) - 2.2/sqrt(28) = 0.5153674 <-- what master returns for ALL three r values The defect is confined to morris_dz; a 600-row randomised differential test against the master implementation (n in {0,1,2,3,5,10,50,200,NA,Inf}, r in {-0.9,0,0.3,0.8,0.999,1,-1,1.5,NA}) gives, for d / d_se / g / g_se: morris_dz : 123 / 104 / 104 / 104 mismatches, ALL of them new==NA vs old==finite, all with |r|>=1 morris_drm : 0 / 0 / 0 / 0 mismatches Kernel level, showing the cause directly: > metaConvert:::.single_group_pre_post_to_smd(0, 2, 0, 1, 25, NA_real_, 'morris_dz') d var_d d_ci_lo d_ci_up g var_g g_ci_lo g_ci_up [1,] NA NA NA NA NA NA NA NA (0^2 + 1^2 - 2*NA*0*1 -> NA, though the third term is identically 0) End-to-end through convert_df, two rows identical except r_pre_post: d <- data.frame(study_id=c('A','B'), n_exp=30, n_nexp=28, paired_t_exp=5.1, paired_t_nexp=2.2, r_pre_post_exp=c(1,0.6), r_pre_post_nexp=c(1,0.6)) summary(convert_df(d, measure='g', pre_post_to_smd='morris_dz'), flags=TRUE) study_id es_crude se_crude info_used_crude 2 A NA NA <NA> 1 B 0.5027168 0.2926914 paired_t flags: "" "" <-- no flag on the dropped row morris_dz does not use r, so A and B must be identical.

**Impact.** A study whose reported pre-post correlation is entered as 1 (or -1, or any out-of-range value reaching the route directly) is silently dropped from a `morris_dz` pool: es = NA, se = NA, no quality flag. The pooled estimate then omits that study. Narrow trigger (requires |r| >= 1 AND the non-default morris_dz standardizer), but it is a behaviour change introduced by the refactor under review, in exactly the family the release notes claim is 'numerically identity-preserving'.

**Fix.** Make the r-term vanish when either SD is zero rather than relying on `0 * NA`. In `.single_group_pre_post_to_smd()` (both the morris_dz branch, line ~1819, and morris_drm, line ~1792) replace the cross term with a zero-safe product, e.g. cross <- ifelse(mean_pre_sd == 0 | mean_post_sd == 0, 0, 2 * r_pre_post * mean_pre_sd * mean_post_sd) sd_diff <- .guard_standardizer(sqrt(mean_pre_sd^2 + mean_post_sd^2 - cross)) Alternatively (narrower), have `.paired_t_to_smd()` pass `r_pre_post = 0` on rows whose method is "morris_dz", since the kernel does not use r there. Add a regression assertion to tests/testthat/test-paired-t-delegation.R pinning that morris_dz output is invariant to r.

**Verifier correction.** The finding stands as filed except for one detail. Commit 1eae59c is a real, unadvertised behaviour regression: routing es_from_paired_t()/es_from_paired_t_single_group() through .single_group_pre_post_to_smd() makes the morris_dz output depend on r_pre_post, although d_z = t/sqrt(n) and its SMCC variance 1/n + g^2/(2n) are both r-free. The cause is that the kernel's cross term `2 * r_pre_post * mean_pre_sd * mean_post_sd` is identically zero only in exact arithmetic; with mean_pre_sd = 0 and r NA'd by .guard_r_pre_post(), R's `0 * NA` is NA, poisoning the radicand. Rows with |r| >= 1 return NA for d/d_se/g/g_se/CIs where master returned the correct finite value, and through convert_df() they are dropped from the pool with no quality flag. The commit's "NOTHING AN USER CAN SEE CHANGES" grid used only three r values, none of them at or beyond the bounds; test-paired-t-delegation.R has no invariance pin. Trigger is narrow (needs |r| >= 1 AND the non-default morris_dz), so minor is the right severity. CORRECTION to the claim's parenthetical: r_pre_post_exp / r_pre_post_nexp are NOT in V11's `.bounded_columns` list at all, so the claim's "V11 bounds r_pre_post at [-1,1], so r = 1 exactly passes" is wrong. Verified end-to-end: r = 1.5 also passes input validation, produces es = NA and an EMPTY flags column. The silent-drop surface is therefore wider than filed, not narrower. CORRECTION to the fix priorities: the zero-safe cross term is correct arithmetic (Var(post - pre) = Var(post) when Var(pre) = 0) and safe, but it is the smaller half. Applying it to the morris_drm branch changes nothing observable (d there stays NA via the sqrt(2(1-r)) factor), and applying it to morris_dz also silently widens the PRE-EXISTING mean-change behaviour (es_from_mean_change_sd with r = 1 currently returns NA and would begin returning a finite d_z) -- defensible, since that d_z is r-free too, but it is a second behaviour change and should be recorded as one rather than folded into a regression fix. The more valuable half is adding r_pre_post_exp/_nexp to `.bounded_columns` (or an equivalent Tier-1 check) so that an out-of-range pre-post correlation is never silently swallowed on ANY pre-post route, including the morris_drm rows where NA is the correct estimate but the absence of a flag is not.

### PERFORMANCE REGRESSION: `.paired_t_to_smd()` runs a per-row mapply over every row, including rows carrying no paired data at all (~5 s per 3000 rows added to every convert_df call)
`R/internal_multiple_formulas.R:1401` — subsystem `prepost-two-group` — refuted by **0 of 3** verifiers

**Claim.** The pre-refactor paired-t code was fully vectorised arithmetic. `.paired_t_to_smd()` now calls the scalar kernel row by row through `mapply()`, and its `ok` filter keys ONLY on `n >= 2` -- it does not skip rows whose `paired_t` is NA. `convert_df()` fills every unsupplied column with NA and always calls all five paired routes (`es_from_paired_t`, `_pval`, `es_from_paired_f`, `_pval`, `es_from_paired_t_single_group`; the two-group ones call `.paired_t_to_smd()` twice each). So a dataset containing only means/SDs, with no paired columns whatsoever, still pays for 9 full per-row mapply loops as long as `n_exp`/`n_nexp` are populated.

**Evidence.** $ Rscript (R 4.5.0, devtools::load_all('.')) K <- 3000; NAv <- rep(NA_real_, K); ne <- sample(20:80,K,TRUE); nn <- sample(20:80,K,TRUE); r <- rep(.8,K) system.time(es_from_paired_t(NAv,NAv,ne,nn,r,r))[3] -> 1.83 s system.time(es_from_paired_t_pval(NAv,NAv,ne,nn,r,r))[3] -> 0.93 s system.time(es_from_paired_f(NAv,NAv,ne,nn,r,r))[3] -> 0.86 s system.time(es_from_paired_f_pval(NAv,NAv,ne,nn,r,r))[3] -> 0.94 s system.time(es_from_paired_t_single_group(NAv,ne,r))[3] -> 0.50 s TOTAL on all-NA paired input, 3000 rows -> 5.06 s Against the whole pipeline on a dataset with NO paired columns at all: d <- data.frame(n_exp=sample(20:80,3000,TRUE), n_nexp=sample(20:80,3000,TRUE), mean_exp=rnorm(3000,10), mean_sd_exp=runif(3000,1,3), mean_nexp=rnorm(3000,9), mean_sd_nexp=runif(3000,1,3)) system.time(convert_df(d, measure='g', verbose=FALSE)) user system elapsed 17.79 0.20 22.17 So ~5.1 s of 17.8 s user time (~28%) is spent iterating the paired kernel over rows that contain no paired statistic. With genuine paired data the cost is the same (2.39 s user for 5000 rows).

**Impact.** No wrong number, but every `convert_df()` run on a moderately sized extraction sheet is roughly 25-30% slower than on master, and the cost scales linearly with nrow. On CRAN this also lengthens example/test time. It is pure overhead on datasets that contain no paired-t/F data, which is the majority case.

**Fix.** Widen the skip in `.paired_t_to_smd()` from `ok <- is.finite(nn) & nn >= 2` to also require a usable statistic and correlation, e.g. `ok <- is.finite(nn) & nn >= 2 & !is.na(rec(paired_t))`, so all-NA rows never enter the mapply. Better still, keep the delegation but group rows by `pre_post_to_smd` value and call the (already vectorised) kernel once per group, the pattern `.cor_to_smd_vec()` uses at R/internal_multiple_formulas.R:1900 for exactly this reason.

**Verifier correction.** CONFIRMED with two precision corrections. (a) The percentage is right but the filed arithmetic mixes units: 5.06 s is elapsed while 17.8 s is user. The like-for-like figures are 3.53 s user for the five routes on 3000 all-NA rows, and Rprof attributes 3.24 s of 12.47 s sampled R time (26.0%) to `.paired_t_to_smd` inside a `convert_df()` run on data with no paired columns at all; an interleaved A/B at N=1000 gives ~21% end-to-end reduction. So "~25-30% slower than master" holds, but on the corrected denominator, not the filed one. (b) The two proposed fixes are not interchangeable, and the finding should say so: widening `ok` to `& !is.na(rec(paired_t))` recovers the whole cost ONLY on datasets with no paired data (verified output-identical: an NA paired_t already yields all-NA on both morris_dz and morris_drm, and the `invisible(.d_j(nn - 1))` warning call sits outside the filter so the warning stream is unchanged). A dataset that genuinely carries paired-t/F columns still pays the full per-row mapply; only the group-by-`pre_post_to_smd` vectorisation (the `.cor_to_smd_vec()` pattern at R/internal_multiple_formulas.R:1909) fixes that case, and it is viable because `.single_group_pre_post_to_smd()` is already vectorised arithmetic within each branch - only the branch selector is scalar. Both fixes should be applied; the `ok` widening alone is a partial fix. Provenance: introduced by commit 1eae59c (present in HEAD, not a working-tree-only change); master's paired-t code was fully vectorised.

### `es_from_paired_f`, `es_from_paired_f_pval` and `es_from_paired_t_pval` silently halve the two-group effect when the arms change in opposite directions, and the limitation is undocumented
`R/es_from_PAIRED_STATISTICS.R:265` — subsystem `prepost-two-group` — refuted by **0 of 3** verifiers

**Claim.** These routes recover each arm's t from an unsigned input (`sqrt(paired_f)` at line 265; `qt(p/2, df, lower.tail = FALSE)` at line 188) so BOTH arms' within-group d come out positive. The between-group estimate is then formed as `d <- d_exp - d_nexp`. When the control arm actually moved in the opposite direction (worsened while treatment improved -- an ordinary trial result), the correct contrast is |d_exp| + |d_nexp| but the code computes |d_exp| - |d_nexp|. `reverse_paired_f` / `reverse_paired_t_pval` are per-row flags, so there is no way to express opposite-direction arms through these entry points at all. Nothing in `?es_from_paired_f`, `?es_from_paired_t_pval`, `?es_from_paired_f_pval` or the Rd @details mentions the assumption, and no quality flag fires. The signed `es_from_paired_t` and the mean-change routes (which carry the sign in `mean_change_*`) are unaffected.

**Evidence.** $ Rscript (devtools::load_all('.')) n <- 30; r <- 0.6; sd_change <- 2; sigma_raw <- sd_change/sqrt(2*(1-r)) # = 2.236068 # exp arm change = +3, control arm change = -1 t_exp <- 3/(sd_change/sqrt(n)) # +8.215838 t_nexp <- -1/(sd_change/sqrt(n)) # -2.738613 TRUE d_rm = (3 - (-1))/sigma_raw = 1.788854 es_from_paired_t(t_exp, t_nexp, n, n, r, r, pre_post_to_smd='cooper')$d -> 1.788854 CORRECT es_from_paired_f(t_exp^2, t_nexp^2, n, n, r, r, pre_post_to_smd='cooper')$d -> 0.8944272 WRONG (50%) p_e <- 2*pt(-abs(t_exp), n-1); p_n <- 2*pt(-abs(t_nexp), n-1) es_from_paired_t_pval(p_e, p_n, n, n, r, r, pre_post_to_smd='cooper')$d -> 0.8944272 WRONG (50%) es_from_mean_change_pval(3, p_e, -1, p_n, r, r, n, n, pre_post_to_smd='cooper')$d -> 1.788854 CORRECT No warning, no message, and `summary(..., flags=TRUE)` raises nothing on such a row. grep over man/es_from_paired_f.Rd, man/es_from_paired_t_pval.Rd, man/es_from_paired_f_pval.Rd finds no statement of the same-direction assumption (only the generic `reverse_*` @param line).

**Impact.** A meta-analyst extracting a within-arm F or p-value from each arm of a pre-post-control trial in which the control group deteriorated gets an effect size roughly half the truth (exactly half when the two arms' |d| are equal, and it can be driven to 0 or sign-flipped when the control moved more than the treatment). This is the classic 'treatment improves, control worsens' pattern, so the failure mode is not exotic. Because both arms' |d| are legitimate, no cross-method or cross-row flag can detect it.

**Fix.** Two-part. (1) Document it: add an @details paragraph and an @note to `es_from_paired_f`, `es_from_paired_f_pval`, `es_from_paired_t_pval` (and their single-group siblings) stating that an F/p-value carries no sign, that both arms are assumed to have changed in the SAME direction, and that `es_from_paired_t` with signed t values (or `es_from_mean_change_*`, which carry the sign in the mean) must be used otherwise. (2) Optionally accept per-arm sign columns (e.g. `reverse_paired_f_exp`/`_nexp`) or reuse the existing `mean_change_exp`/`_nexp` sign when both are present, so the direction can actually be expressed.

**Verifier correction.** Real, but reframe the mechanism, tighten the magnitude wording, and drop the single-group siblings from the fix. MECHANISM (corrected): this is not an arithmetic error but an information limit of the input that the package neither documents nor lets the user work around. A paired F statistic and a two-sided paired p-value carry no sign, so `sqrt(paired_f_exp)` (R/es_from_PAIRED_STATISTICS.R:265) and `qt(p/2, df, lower.tail=FALSE)` (:188) can only return |t|. `es_from_paired_t` then forms `d <- d_exp - d_nexp`, so `es_from_paired_f`, `es_from_paired_f_pval` and `es_from_paired_t_pval` silently assume both arms changed in the SAME direction. Because `reverse_paired_f` / `reverse_paired_f_pval` / `reverse_paired_t_pval` are passed through as the whole-contrast `reverse_paired_t` (:198, :276) and no per-arm sign column exists (R/data_extraction.R:292-295), an opposite-direction pair cannot be expressed through these entry points at all. No note in the three Rd files, nothing in NEWS at HEAD, and no quality flag fires (verified end-to-end through convert_df + summary(flags=TRUE): flags_crude is empty). MAGNITUDE (corrected): "silently halves" is the equal-|d| special case. In general the routes return |d_exp| - |d_nexp| where the correct contrast is |d_exp| + |d_nexp|, an absolute bias of 2*|d_nexp| that can shrink the estimate toward zero or reverse its sign when the control arm moved more than the treatment arm. Reproduced: 0.8944 returned against a true 1.7889. SCOPE (corrected): the single-group siblings in R/es_from_PAIRED_SINGLE_GROUP.R do NOT share the defect. With one arm there is no contrast to mis-sign, and the existing whole-row `reverse_*` flag fully expresses a decrease, so nothing is inexpressible there. A one-line note that the F/p is unsigned and a decrease must be marked with `reverse_*` is still worth adding, but it is a different (and weaker) point than the two-group one, and the fix should not imply the two-group failure mode exists there. FIX (corrected): (1) add an @details paragraph plus an @note to `es_from_paired_f`, `es_from_paired_f_pval` and `es_from_paired_t_pval` only, stating that these routes assume both arms moved in the same direction, that the assumption is unverifiable from the reported statistic, and that `es_from_paired_t` with signed t values or the `es_from_mean_change_*` family (which carry the sign in the mean) must be used when the arms diverge. (2) Optionally add per-arm sign columns so the direction can be expressed. Note that the fix's suggestion to "reuse the existing mean_change_exp/_nexp sign when both are present" is close to inert in practice, since a row carrying mean_change values already selects a higher-ranked mean-change route in the convert_df hierarchy; a dedicated per-arm reverse column is the cleaner form.

### `md_se` / `mdw_se` in the pre/post routes are built from signed SDs, so a negative dispersion input (or one transposed CI bound) inflates the reported SE by up to 3x while d/g are correctly NA'd
`R/es_from_PAIRED_MEANS.R:198` — subsystem `prepost-two-group` — refuted by **0 of 3** verifiers

**Claim.** `es_from_means_se_pre_post()` converts SE to SD as `sd <- se * sqrt(n)` (lines 309-312) with no sign guard, and `es_from_means_ci_pre_post()` builds the SE as `(ci_up - ci_lo) / (2 * qt(.975, df))` (lines 455-458) with a raw subtraction rather than the package's own `.ci_width()` helper. A negative SE therefore reaches `es_from_means_sd_pre_post()`, whose `md_var_exp <- (mean_pre_sd_exp^2 + mean_sd_exp^2 - 2*r*mean_pre_sd_exp*mean_sd_exp)/n_exp` (line 198) uses the raw signed values. The SMD kernel NA-guards negative SDs, so d/g/d_se correctly become NA -- but `md`, `md_se`, `md_ci_lo`, `md_ci_up` (and `mdw_*` at R/es_from_PAIRED_SINGLE_GROUP.R:146) are computed outside that guard and are emitted with a wrong-signed cross term, i.e. `sd_pre^2 + sd_post^2 + 2r|sd_pre*sd_post|` instead of `... - 2r sd_pre sd_post`. The route returns a positive, plausible-looking SE and a plausible-looking CI, with no NA and no warning about md. The existing sweep in tests/testthat/test-input-guards.R does not catch it: it asserts only 'no sign flip / no negative SE / no transposed output CI', never SE magnitude, and `prim()` falls through to `md` whose sign is unchanged.

**Evidence.** $ Rscript (devtools::load_all('.')) ne <- nn <- 30; r <- 0.8; sd <- 2; se <- sd/sqrt(30) # correct es_from_means_se_pre_post(mean_pre_exp=10, mean_exp=13, mean_pre_se_exp=se, mean_se_exp=se, mean_pre_nexp=10, mean_nexp=11, mean_pre_se_nexp=se, mean_se_nexp=se, n_exp=30, n_nexp=30, r_pre_post_exp=.8, r_pre_post_nexp=.8) md = 2 md_se = 0.3265986 d = 1 # sqrt((4+4-2*.8*4)/30 * 2) = 0.3265986 CORRECT # ONE arm's endpoint SE entered negative md = 2 md_se = 0.7302967 d = 1 # sqrt((4+4+6.4)/30 + 1.6/30) -> 2.236x too large # same thing via a transposed endpoint CI (a realistic extraction error) es_from_means_ci_pre_post(... mean_ci_lo_exp = up, mean_ci_up_exp = lo ...) md = 2 md_se = 0.7302967 md_ci = [0.5381522, 3.461848] d = 1 ratio md_se bad/ok = 2.236068 # single-group analogue es_from_means_se_pre_post_single_group(10, 13, se, se, 30, .8) -> mdw_se = 0.2309401 CORRECT es_from_means_se_pre_post_single_group(10, 13, se, -se, 30, .8) -> mdw_se = 0.6928203 (3.00x) No warning is emitted about md/mdw; the returned md_ci is a well-formed, non-transposed, 2.24x-too-wide interval. Under `convert_df(..., correct_inputs = TRUE)` (the default) the bad input is NA'd first, so the exposure is direct calls to the exported routes (the documented rationale for R/internal_guards.R) and `correct_inputs = FALSE` runs.

**Impact.** A meta-analysis pooling `measure = "md"` or `"mdw"` from these routes, driven by direct calls (as metaumbrella does) or with `correct_inputs = FALSE`, gets a study whose inverse-variance weight is 5x too small (2.24^2) or 9x too small (3.00^2) from a single transposed CI bound, with no NA and no flag. The d/g path is safe.

**Fix.** Route the two pre/post SE/CI entry points through the existing guards: in `es_from_means_ci_pre_post()` use `.ci_width(lo, up)` (R/internal_guards.R) instead of `ci_up - ci_lo`; in `es_from_means_se_pre_post()` NA any negative `mean_*_se_*` before `sd <- se * sqrt(n)`. Belt and braces: NA-guard the SDs inside the `md_var`/`mdw_var` expressions at R/es_from_PAIRED_MEANS.R:198-202 and R/es_from_PAIRED_SINGLE_GROUP.R:146 the same way `.single_group_pre_post_to_smd()` already does. Extend the sweep in tests/testthat/test-input-guards.R to assert that a corrupted input never changes an SE by more than it changes the corresponding clean call (or simply that the SE becomes NA).

**Verifier correction.** Four pre/post-means entry points bypass the R/internal_guards.R helpers, so a negative SE input or a transposed CI bound inflates md_se/mdw_se by 2.2x-3.0x instead of being neutralised to NA: es_from_means_se_pre_post (R/es_from_PAIRED_MEANS.R:309-312, `sd <- se * sqrt(n)` with no `.positive_or_na()`), es_from_means_ci_pre_post (R/es_from_PAIRED_MEANS.R:455-458, raw `ci_up - ci_lo` instead of `.ci_width()`), and the two single-group analogues at R/es_from_PAIRED_SINGLE_GROUP.R:249-250 and :354-355 (the latter is NOT named in the original finding and shows the same measured 3.00x). The signed value reaches the paired-difference variance `(s_pre^2 + s_post^2 - 2r*s_pre*s_post)/n` (R/es_from_PAIRED_MEANS.R:198-202, R/es_from_PAIRED_SINGLE_GROUP.R:146) with the cross term wrong-signed. The mean_change family (es_from_mean_change_sd/se/ci) is NOT affected: it passes mean_pre_sd = 0, so the cross term vanishes. Corrections to the filing: (a) d/g point estimates are NOT NA'd — only d_se/g_se are, so the route emits a plausible d alongside the bad SE; (b) under convert_df(correct_inputs = FALSE) a Tier-1 `[INVALID] Negative input: 'mean_se_exp'` flag DOES fire, so the truly silent exposure is direct calls to the exported routes (the metaumbrella path, which is the documented rationale for internal_guards.R); (c) the error inflates the SE, so the weight is 5x/9x too SMALL — under-weighting, not spurious precision. Fix: apply `.positive_or_na()` to the four `mean_*_se_*` arguments in the two SE routes and `.ci_width()` in the two CI routes. Do NOT apply the finding's suggested "belt and braces" `.positive_or_na()` inside the md_var/mdw_var expressions of es_from_means_sd_pre_post(): `.positive_or_na()` rejects zero, and es_from_mean_change_se/ci deliberately supply mean_pre_se = 0 / a (0,0) pre-CI, so that change returns md_se = NA, d = NA and g = NA for the entire mean-change family (verified). Extend the test-input-guards.R sweep with a magnitude assertion (a corrupted input must not change any SE, or must NA it) rather than only the current sign/negativity/transposition checks, which the sweep already runs on these routes and which all pass.

### V6 / attr(res,"r_defaulted") fires on every single-group (within-subject) row even when r_pre_post_exp WAS supplied, and the message names a value that was never used
`R/main_convert_df.R:586` — subsystem `prepost-single-group` — refuted by **0 of 3** verifiers

**Claim.** `.r_defaulted <- (.r_defaulted_exp | .r_defaulted_nexp) & .has_pre_post` ORs the two arms. For single-group data (measure = "dw"/"gw"/"mdw", or any one-arm pre/post row) there is no non-exposed arm, so `r_pre_post_nexp` is structurally NA and `.r_defaulted_nexp` is always TRUE. Every such row therefore gets the flag `[INFO] Default r_pre_post = 0.8 used (not provided by user)` and `attr(res, "r_defaulted") == TRUE`, which `summary()` then uses to append `(r-sensitive: depends on the default r_pre_post — re-check with the true pre-post correlation)` to any A6/E2/E2b flag on that row. The statement is false: the user supplied the correlation, and the arithmetic used the supplied value, not 0.8. The existing negative control in tests/testthat/test-V6-r-defaulted-columns.R ("a row supplying r_pre_post explicitly is NOT flagged") only covers the two-group shape where BOTH `r_pre_post_exp` and `r_pre_post_nexp` can be filled, so the single-group case is not pinned as intentional.

**Evidence.** R> x <- rbind(mk(mean_pre_exp=50, mean_exp=55, mean_pre_sd_exp=-10, mean_sd_exp=11, n_exp=40, r_pre_post_exp=-0.5), + mk(mean_pre_exp=50, mean_exp=55, mean_pre_sd_exp= 10, mean_sd_exp=11, n_exp=40, r_pre_post_exp=-0.5)) R> summary(convert_df(x, measure="mdw", verbose=FALSE, correct_inputs=FALSE), flags=TRUE) study_id es_crude se_crude flags_crude 1 bad 5 1.665833 [INVALID] Negative input: 'mean_pre_sd_exp' = -10; [INFO] Default r_pre_post = 0.8 used (not provided by user) 2 good 5 2.876630 [INFO] Default r_pre_post = 0.8 used (not provided by user) The flag claims r = 0.8; the SE 2.876630 is sqrt((100+121-2*(-0.5)*10*11)/40), i.e. the SUPPLIED r = -0.5 was used (r = 0.8 would give sqrt((221-176)/40) = 1.0607). And on a clean 4-row within-subject sheet with r_pre_post_exp = 0.6 supplied on every row: R> attr(convert_df(x, measure="dw", verbose=FALSE), "r_defaulted") NA NA1 NA2 NA3 TRUE TRUE TRUE TRUE R> x2 <- x; x2$r_pre_post_nexp <- 0.6 # add the (meaningless) second arm R> attr(convert_df(x2, measure="dw", verbose=FALSE), "r_defaulted") NA NA1 NA2 NA3 FALSE FALSE FALSE FALSE The effect sizes are identical in both runs (es = 0.5000000 / 0.4861017 etc.), confirming the supplied r_pre_post_exp is what the arithmetic used in both cases — only the flag differs.

**Impact.** Every within-subject (dw/gw/mdw) review that correctly reports its pre-post correlations is told, on every row, that the package imputed r = 0.8, and any A6/E2/E2b DISCORDANT flag on those rows is annotated as an artefact of an assumed correlation. A reviewer acting on that either discounts a real discordance as r-sensitive noise, or re-runs a sensitivity analysis that cannot change anything. The named value (0.8) can be arbitrarily far from the r actually used (-0.5 above).

**Fix.** Make the arm test conditional on the arm actually carrying data, e.g. compute per-arm r-consuming masks and OR only over the arms present: ```r .exp_cols <- grep("_exp$", .r_consuming_columns(), value = TRUE) .nexp_cols <- grep("_nexp$", .r_consuming_columns(), value = TRUE) .has_exp <- rowSums(!is.na(x[, intersect(.exp_cols, colnames(x)), drop = FALSE])) > 0 .has_nexp <- rowSums(!is.na(x[, intersect(.nexp_cols, colnames(x)), drop = FALSE])) > 0 .r_defaulted <- (.r_defaulted_exp & .has_exp) | (.r_defaulted_nexp & .has_nexp) ``` Add the missing negative control to tests/testthat/test-V6-r-defaulted-columns.R: a single-group row supplying only `r_pre_post_exp` must NOT be flagged.

**Verifier correction.** Real, minor, and correctly diagnosed — but under-scoped in two ways. (1) The same OR is duplicated at R/main_convert_df.R:409 for the `verbose = TRUE` console note (`.n_imputed <- sum((.r_defaulted_exp | .r_defaulted_nexp) & .rows_with_pp)`), so the message "Note: r_pre_post was not reported for 1 row(s) with pre/post, mean-change or paired data; the default r_pre_post = 0.8 was assumed" is emitted verbatim on a single-group row that DID report it (verified). The finding names only line 586; the fix must be applied at both call sites or the console keeps making the false claim after the flag is fixed. (2) The trigger is not specific to `measure = "dw"/"gw"/"mdw"`. It is any row whose r-consuming data sits in one arm only while that arm's r is supplied — including a two-group row carrying pre/post or mean-change data in the exp arm only (nexp endpoint-only), and symmetrically the nexp-only shape with `r_pre_post_nexp` supplied. Single-group rows are the common instance, not the definition. The proposed patch is correct as written (regex split is clean, zero-column rowSums is safe, all 39 existing V6 assertions pass, false positive silenced). Preferred implementation: add an arm-aware companion to `.rows_with_r_consuming_data()` in R/internal_flags.R returning `has_exp`/`has_nexp`, and consume it at BOTH line 409 and line 586, so the "single source of truth" invariant that test-V6-r-defaulted-columns.R explicitly pins ("the verbose note and flag V6 agree on which rows are r-consuming") is preserved rather than re-forked. Add the missing negative control: a single-group row supplying only `r_pre_post_exp` must not raise V6, must have `r_defaulted == FALSE`, and must produce no verbose note.

### Scalar recycling: a length-1 r_pre_post_exp or n_exp silently NAs mdw_se and the mdw CI on every row but the first, while d/g/mdw stay correct
`R/es_from_PAIRED_SINGLE_GROUP.R:146` — subsystem `prepost-single-group` — refuted by **0 of 3** verifiers

**Claim.** The SMD arithmetic is routed through a `data.frame()` (line 111), which recycles length-1 arguments, so `d`/`g` are right for all rows. The `mdw` block at lines 146-150 instead indexes the raw arguments directly — `r_pre_post_exp[nn_miss]`, `n_exp[nn_miss]`, `mean_pre_sd_exp[nn_miss]` — so a length-1 `r_pre_post_exp` or `n_exp` returns NA for every index above 1. The result is a fully populated `mdw` column whose `mdw_se`/`mdw_ci_lo`/`mdw_ci_up` are NA on all rows but the first. This is the exact failure mode CLAUDE.md pins as invariant #1 for the reliability path ("a length-1 n_sample ... every row after the first gets an NA SE while keeping a correct point estimate — a fully populated alpha column that rma() then silently thins"); the fix was applied there and not here. `convert_df()` is unaffected (data.frame columns are always full length); direct calls (the metaumbrella / one-off path) are.

**Evidence.** R> es_from_means_sd_pre_post_single_group( + mean_pre_exp = c(10, 20, 30), mean_exp = c(12, 23, 34), + mean_pre_sd_exp = c(5,5,5), mean_sd_exp = c(6,6,6), + n_exp = c(40,40,40), r_pre_post_exp = 0.5, pre_post_to_smd = "bonett")[, c("d","d_se","g","mdw","mdw_se","mdw_ci_lo","mdw_ci_up")] d d_se g mdw mdw_se mdw_ci_lo mdw_ci_up 1 0.4 0.1873900 0.3922497 2 0.8803408 0.2193426 3.780657 2 0.6 0.1941111 0.5883745 3 NA NA NA 3 0.8 0.2031474 0.7844994 4 NA NA NA R> # identical call with r as a length-3 vector d d_se g mdw mdw_se mdw_ci_lo mdw_ci_up 1 0.4 0.1873900 0.3922497 2 0.8803408 0.2193426 3.780657 2 0.6 0.1941111 0.5883745 3 0.8803408 1.2193426 4.780657 3 0.8 0.2031474 0.7844994 4 0.8803408 2.2193426 5.780657 R> # scalar n_exp reproduces it identically ... n_exp = 40, r_pre_post_exp = rep(0.5,3) -> mdw_se = 0.8803408, NA, NA R> # and it propagates through the mean-change wrapper R> es_from_mean_change_sd_single_group(mean_change_exp=c(2,3,4), mean_change_sd_exp=c(5,5,5), n_exp=c(40,40,40), r_pre_post_exp=0.5)[, c("d","d_se","mdw","mdw_se")] d d_se mdw mdw_se 1 0.4 0.1643168 2 0.7905694 2 0.6 0.1717556 3 NA 3 0.8 0.1816590 4 NA

**Impact.** A user (or metaumbrella) calling the single-group route directly with a common pre-post correlation supplied once, or one common sample size, gets an `mdw` column that looks complete but whose SEs are NA past row 1. `summary()`/`rma()` then drop those rows from the pool without comment — the pooled within-group MD is computed from a single study while the printout still says the column is populated.

**Fix.** Recycle before indexing, using the `es_from_pearson_r()` idiom already adopted for the reliability routes: ```r .k <- length(mean_pre_exp) rec <- function(v, nm) { if (length(v) == 1L) rep_len(v, .k) else if (length(v) == .k) v else stop(...) } r_pre_post_exp <- rec(r_pre_post_exp, "r_pre_post_exp"); n_exp <- rec(n_exp, "n_exp") mean_pre_sd_exp <- rec(mean_pre_sd_exp, "mean_pre_sd_exp"); mean_sd_exp <- rec(mean_sd_exp, "mean_sd_exp") ``` Apply the same to `es_from_paired_t_single_group()` for consistency (its `.paired_t_to_smd()` already does `rep_len`).

**Verifier correction.** In es_from_means_sd_pre_post_single_group() (R/es_from_PAIRED_SINGLE_GROUP.R:145-150) the mdw block indexes the raw arguments with nn_miss, whereas the SMD block above it reads from a data.frame() that already recycled length-1 columns. Consequently, when ANY of r_pre_post_exp, n_exp, mean_pre_sd_exp or mean_sd_exp is EXPLICITLY passed as a length-1 vector alongside multi-row data, mdw_se / mdw_ci_lo / mdw_ci_up are NA on every row but the first, while mdw, d, g and the converted r/z/logor stay correct. Omitting r_pre_post_exp entirely is safe (the missing() branch rep()s the 0.8 default to full length); only an explicitly supplied scalar triggers it. Propagates through the wrappers es_from_mean_change_sd_single_group() and es_from_means_se_pre_post_single_group(). convert_df() is unaffected (data.frame columns are always full length), and summary.metaConvert is therefore NOT on the affected path — the harm is confined to direct callers (metaumbrella, one-off use) who feed mdw/mdw_se to rma() themselves and get rows dropped without comment. es_from_paired_t_single_group() is already correct (.paired_t_to_smd() calls rep_len), as is the two-group es_from_means_sd_pre_post() (its md block is fully vectorised, no nn_miss indexing) — so this is a single-site slip, not a package-wide idiom. Severity minor confirmed: the failure emits missing values, not wrong ones, but it silently reduces k. Fix as filed (recycle length-1 arguments before indexing, erroring on incompatible lengths, per the es_from_cronbach_alpha()/es_from_pearson_r() idiom at R/es_from_ALPHA.R:154-164) is correct and produces byte-identical output for correct-length inputs; the "apply the same to es_from_paired_t_single_group()" half is cosmetic consistency only, since that route has no defect.

### Kernel delegation regression: morris_dz, which is r-free, now returns NA when |r_pre_post| >= 1
`R/internal_multiple_formulas.R:1819` — subsystem `prepost-kernel` — refuted by **0 of 3** verifiers

**Claim.** The paired-t routes now express a paired t as a single-group problem with mean_pre_sd = 0, mean_post_sd = 1 and route it through .single_group_pre_post_to_smd(). The kernel's morris_dz branch builds its standardizer as `sqrt(mean_pre_sd^2 + mean_post_sd^2 - 2 * r_pre_post * mean_pre_sd * mean_post_sd)`. With mean_pre_sd = 0 the cross term is mathematically zero, but in R `2 * NA * 0 * 1` is NA, so an r that .guard_r_pre_post() has NA'd (|r| >= 1, e.g. a correlation typed as a percentage) propagates into the standardizer and nulls the whole row. Master's inline morris_dz branch was literally `paired_t / sqrt(n)` and never touched r, so it returned the correct d_z. d_z is by definition independent of the pre-post correlation, so the new NA is not a conservatism, it is a row silently disappearing over an input the estimator does not use.

**Evidence.** es_from_paired_t_single_group(paired_t_exp = c(2.1, 2.1), n_exp = c(20, 20), r_pre_post_exp = c(0.8, 1), pre_post_to_smd = "morris_dz") new d = 0.46957428, NA master's inline branch (git show master:R/es_from_PAIRED_SINGLE_GROUP.R, d_dz <- paired_t_exp/sqrt(n_exp)): t/sqrt(n) = 2.1/sqrt(20) = 0.46957428 for BOTH rows Same divergence through es_from_paired_t() (r_pre_post_exp = c(0.8, 1)): d d_se g g_se 1 0.2563736 0.3312765 0.2452701 0.3185804 2 NA NA NA NA Everything else about the delegation is byte-parity: over a 1280-cell grid (t in {-3,0.5,2.1,7} x {-1.2,4.2}, n_exp in {2,3,10,45,200}, n_nexp in {2,5,30,120}, r in {0,0.3,0.8,0.99}, both methods) new vs master's inline arithmetic gives max |d diff| = 1.78e-15 max |d_se diff| = 8.88e-16 max |g diff| = 4.44e-16 max |g_se diff| = 4.44e-16 NA patterns identical (TRUE) and 8 edge cases (NA t, n = 0/1, NA n, mixed per-row methods, scalar recycling, negative r, n = 2/3) all match to <= 2.2e-16 with identical NA patterns.

**Impact.** A study whose pre-post correlation was transcribed as 80 rather than 0.80 (or as exactly 1) is dropped from a morris_dz pool instead of contributing its correct, r-independent d_z. It is a silent row loss rather than a wrong number, and only bites on out-of-range r, hence the low severity; but it is an unintended behaviour change introduced by an explicitly behaviour-preserving refactor, and its own docblock claims the kernel 'reduces to 1' for these inputs.

**Fix.** Make the cross term zero-aware where a standardizing SD is structurally zero, e.g. in the morris_dz and morris_drm branches replace `2 * r_pre_post * mean_pre_sd * mean_post_sd` with a term that is 0 when either SD is 0 (`cross <- ifelse(mean_pre_sd == 0 | mean_post_sd == 0, 0, 2 * r_pre_post * mean_pre_sd * mean_post_sd)`), or have .paired_t_to_smd() pass r_pre_post = 0 to the dz branch, which the branch does not use. Add the r = 1 / r = 80 row to tests/testthat/test-paired-t-delegation.R.

**Verifier correction.** Confirmed, with the scope widened. The morris_dz branch of .single_group_pre_post_to_smd() (R/internal_multiple_formulas.R:1813) builds its standardizer as sqrt(sd_pre^2 + sd_post^2 - 2*r*sd_pre*sd_post). Whenever a caller zeroes the pre slot by construction, the cross term is mathematically zero but evaluates to NA in R once .guard_r_pre_post() has NA'd an |r| >= 1, nulling a d_z that carries no r at all (d_z = mean_change/SD_change; from a paired t it is exactly t/sqrt(n), and its SMCC variance 1/n + g^2/(2n) is likewise r-free). Affected callers: (a) NEW REGRESSION from the paired-t/F delegation — es_from_paired_t(), es_from_paired_t_pval(), es_from_paired_f(), es_from_paired_f_pval() and their _single_group counterparts, all of which reach the kernel through .paired_t_to_smd() with mean_pre_sd = 0, mean_post_sd = 1. Master's inline branches computed d_dz <- paired_t/sqrt(n) and never touched r, so these rows previously returned the correct d_z. (b) PRE-EXISTING, not a regression — the mean-change single-group family (es_from_mean_change_sd_single_group and the routes that funnel into es_from_means_sd_pre_post_single_group with mean_pre_sd = 0), which behaves identically on master. Because of (b) the fix belongs in the kernel, not in .paired_t_to_smd(): make the cross term zero-aware (0 whenever either SD is 0), which repairs both families at once and is mathematically exact (with SD_pre = 0 the change SD is SD_post for any r). Verified not to leak into the other branches: morris_drm still returns NA via its sqrt(2(1-r)) factor, bonett via its zero standardizer, morris_dav via J on mi = 2(n-1)/(1+r^2). Severity stays minor: it is a silent row loss on invalid input, not a wrong number. Two details worth adding to the report — r_pre_post_exp/nexp are absent from .bounded_columns, so an out-of-range r is neither corrected nor flagged in Tier 1; and on the dropped rows es_guidance_crude misleadingly reports "No partial input data found", so nothing in the output identifies r as the cause. The docblock at R/internal_multiple_formulas.R:1388 ("sd_diff then reduces to 1, which yields d = t/sqrt(n) under morris_dz") states an invariant the code does not hold, and should be fixed with the code.

### V40 tells the user an alpha of exactly 1 "yields no effect size and drops out of the pool" while, under alpha_to_es = "hakstian_whalen", the row is kept with es = 1 and se = 0
`R/internal_flags.R:656` — subsystem `alpha` — refuted by **0 of 3** verifiers

**Claim.** V40 skips only the `raw` scale (`if (identical(as.character(sc), "raw")) next`). On the Hakstian-Whalen scale the transform of alpha = 1 is perfectly defined (1 - (1-1)^(1/3) = 1, no logarithm involved) and `valid_es` in es_from_ALPHA.R:210 excludes alpha == 1 only when `alpha_to_es == "bonett"`, so the row IS computed: es = 1, se = 0. The emitted message is wrong on both counts - it claims "has no hakstian_whalen transform (log(0) is undefined)" and "this row yields no effect size and drops out of the pool" - and it sits on the same row as the contradicting "[INVALID] SE is zero" flag. This is the self-contradictory flag pair CLAUDE.md records audit #43 as having been fixed for.

**Evidence.** $ convert_df(d, measure="alpha", alpha_to_es=<scale>) |> summary(flags=TRUE) # d: s1 alpha .85, s2 alpha 1.00, s3 alpha .90; n=200, k=10 throughout ### alpha_to_es = hakstian_whalen study_id es_crude se_crude info_used_crude 1 s1 0.4686707 0.01873678 cronbach_alpha 2 s2 1.0000000 0.00000000 cronbach_alpha <-- kept, se = 0 3 s3 0.5358411 0.01636808 cronbach_alpha flags_crude[2] = "[UNUSUAL] 'cronbach_alpha' = 1 exactly. A perfect alpha has no hakstian_whalen transform (log(0) is undefined), so this row yields no effect size and drops out of the pool. ...; [INVALID] SE is zero (from cronbach_alpha); [UNUSUAL] Near-perfect alpha: 1 (threshold: 0.99) (from cronbach_alpha)" ### alpha_to_es = bonett (message is correct here) study_id es_crude se_crude 21 s2 NA NA ### alpha_to_es = raw (V40 correctly silent; documented boundary es=1, se=0) 2 s2 1.00 0.00000000

**Impact.** A user running the Hakstian-Whalen scale (the scale the RG method literature uses, newly added this release) is told a row was dropped when it was in fact retained with a zero standard error. A zero sei is not benign: metafor::rma() either aborts ("Division by zero when computing the inverse variance weights") or, with a fixed-effects weighting the user supplies, gives the row infinite weight. The [INVALID] SE-is-zero flag does fire, so it is not fully silent, but the two flags directly contradict each other and the wrong one names the remedy.

**Fix.** In the V40 loop (internal_flags.R:650-666) skip whenever the active scale leaves alpha = 1 computable - i.e. `if (identical(as.character(sc), "raw") || identical(as.character(sc), "hakstian_whalen")) next` - and instead rely on the existing C2 "SE is zero" flag; or keep the flag but make the message scale-specific (only the bonett branch may say "log(0) is undefined" / "drops out of the pool").

**Verifier correction.** Real but mis-scoped, and the proposed fix is unsafe. WHAT IS TRUE: under `alpha_to_es = "hakstian_whalen"`, cronbach_alpha = 1 is retained (es = 1, se = 0) because es_from_ALPHA.R's `valid_es` excludes alpha == 1 only when `alpha_to_es == "bonett"`. V40 nonetheless tells the user the row "yields no effect size and drops out of the pool", contradicting the co-emitted "[INVALID] SE is zero". The parenthetical "(log(0) is undefined)" is also false for HW, whose transform 1 - (1-a)^(1/3) is defined at a = 1; what actually happens is that its variance 18k(n-1)(1-a)^(2/3)/((k-1)(9n-11)^2) collapses to 0 — the same degenerate boundary the raw scale is deliberately allowed to keep. CORRECTED SCOPE: the "drops out of the pool" falsehood is alpha + hakstian_whalen ONLY. The "(log(0) is undefined)" falsehood additionally applies to omega + hakstian_whalen (there the row genuinely does drop, but because es_from_OMEGA.R gates on `omega < 1`, not because the transform is undefined). ICC cannot reach this: es_from_ICC.R accepts only "bonett"/"raw". CORRECTED MECHANISM: not a lone flag bug, but a divergence between two sibling routes — es_from_ALPHA.R:210 excludes 1 on the bonett scale only, es_from_OMEGA.R:279 excludes 1 on every non-raw scale — with V40 hardcoding omega's convention for both. IMPACT (unchanged, and honest): a zero sei makes metafor::rma() abort with "Division by zero when computing the inverse variance weights" (verified), so this is a loud, misleading-message defect, not a silent weighting error. Minor. FIX MUST BE CHANGED: the filed fix `if (sc == "raw" || sc == "hakstian_whalen") next` is applied inside a loop covering alpha/omega/icc with a per-measure scale, so it would also silence V40 for omega under HW, where omega = 1 really is dropped — reinstating the silent drop V40 exists to prevent. Correct options: (a) make V40's skip route-aware (skip when the active route actually computes the value: alpha drops only under bonett; omega drops under bonett and HW) and make the parenthetical scale-specific, reserving "log(0) is undefined" for bonett; or (b) align es_from_ALPHA.R's `valid_es` with es_from_OMEGA.R by excluding alpha == 1 on both non-raw scales, which makes V40's existing assumption true and removes the se = 0 row — but that is a behaviour change that also departs from the deliberate raw-scale boundary convention, so it is a design decision, not a mechanical repair. Either way, add HW coverage to tests/testthat/test-reliability-generalization.R:482, which currently pins bonett and raw only.

### alpha = 1 with a reported natural-scale SE returns alpha_se = Inf and a [-Inf, Inf] CI on the hakstian_whalen scale
`R/es_from_ALPHA.R:219` — subsystem `alpha` — refuted by **0 of 3** verifiers

**Claim.** `valid_es` (line 210) admits alpha == 1 on every scale except bonett, and the reported-SE delta map `se_map()` divides by `3 * (1 - a)^(2/3)`, which is 0 at a = 1. The row therefore leaves the route with `alpha_se = Inf` and `alpha_ci = [-Inf, Inf]` rather than NA. The guard comment at line 205 anticipates only the bonett `log(0)` case; the HW scale has a finite point estimate at a = 1 but no finite delta-mapped SE.

**Evidence.** $ es_from_cronbach_alpha(1, 200, 10, cronbach_alpha_se = 0.02, alpha_to_es = "hakstian_whalen") alpha alpha_se alpha_ci_lo alpha_ci_up n_sample n_items info_used 1 1 Inf -Inf Inf 200 10 cronbach_alpha $ es_from_cronbach_alpha(1, 200, 10, cronbach_alpha_se = 0.02, alpha_to_es = "raw") alpha alpha_se alpha_ci_lo alpha_ci_up 1 1 0.02 0.9608007 1.039199 # raw is fine: identity map # by contrast the no-SE HW path returns a finite (zero) SE, see finding 2 $ es_from_cronbach_alpha(1, 200, 10, alpha_to_es = "hakstian_whalen")$alpha_se [1] 0

**Impact.** Low. Category A ("ES/SE is Inf/NaN") catches it in summary(), and an Inf SE gets zero weight rather than a wrong one, so nothing is silently mis-weighted. It is an edge case that escapes the route's own "degrade to NA instead of emitting Inf or NaN" contract, which the comment at line 205 explicitly claims.

**Fix.** Extend the reported-SE route's validity test: exclude rows where the delta-map denominator vanishes, e.g. add `& !(cronbach_alpha == 1 & alpha_to_es %in% c("bonett", "hakstian_whalen"))` to the `from_se` selection, or wrap the mapped value in `.positive_or_na()`-style finiteness handling so a non-finite mapped SE becomes NA.

**Verifier correction.** Real, minor, but the fix and scope are wrong as filed. The `alpha = 1` boundary rule in `es_from_ALPHA.R` was written for a two-scale world (bonett vs raw); the later-added `hakstian_whalen` scale has a boundary rule only in route 2's `ci_usable` test, and nowhere else. Consequences: (a) `valid_es` (line 210) admits a == 1 on HW, and the reported-SE delta map `se_map()` divides by 3*(1-a)^(2/3) = 0, so the row leaves the route (and reaches `summary()`) as es = 1, se = Inf, CI = [-Inf, Inf] — a violation of the route's own contract at lines 200-206 and inconsistent with `metafor::escalc(measure = "ABT")`, which recodes non-finite values to NA with a message; the emitted ci_up = Inf is also outside the HW parameter space (T <= 1). (b) The V40 flag message at `R/internal_flags.R:658-665` is factually wrong on this scale, telling the user the row "has no hakstian_whalen transform (log(0) is undefined)" and "yields no effect size and drops out of the pool" when HW has no log, is defined at a = 1, and does yield es = 1. The filed fix ("add the exclusion to the `from_se` selection", or NA the mapped value) is NOT safe: routes 2 and 3 both gate on `is.na(alpha_es_se)`, and the HW closed form evaluates to exactly 0 at a = 1 (verified), so that patch silently drops the user's reported SE and replaces Inf (zero weight, benign, caught by Category A) with 0 (infinite weight) — strictly worse. The repair must cover the whole SE cascade: on non-raw scales, a == 1 should yield se = NA from all three routes (the ES itself can stay at 1, which matches `escalc(measure = "AHW", ai = 1)` returning yi = 1), and V40's message must be made scale-specific rather than asserting log(0) and a dropped row.

### V41 (k-dependent ICC floor) fires on average-measures ICCs, destroying a legitimate value that the route itself computes correctly
`R/internal_flags.R:682` — subsystem `icc` — refuted by **0 of 3** verifiers

**Claim.** V41 tests `icc < -1/(k-1)` on the raw input column without consulting `icc_type`. That floor is the bound on a SINGLE-measurement ICC. For an average-measures ICC (`icc_type = 'ICC(2,k)'/'ICC(3,k)'/'average'`) the reported quantity is ICC_k, and the inverse Spearman-Brown map rho_1 = rho_k/(k-(k-1)rho_k) sends EVERY rho_k in [-1,1] to a rho_1 that is at or above -1/(2k-1) > -1/(k-1). So no in-range average-measures ICC can ever violate the single-measures floor, yet V41 declares such rows '[INVALID] Impossible ICC' and (under the default `correct_inputs = TRUE`) sets `icc` to NA. The route, called directly, computes the same row correctly. V41's own test block (tests/testthat/test-reliability-generalization.R:1024-1083) never exercises an average-measures row, so the interaction between the two features added in this same release is untested.

**Evidence.** $ Rscript -e "..." # 1. the value is legal: step-down lands well above the floor ICC(2,k) = -0.6 k = 3 -> step-down rho1 = -0.1428571 (floor -1/(k-1) = -0.5 ) legal? TRUE # 2. the ROUTE computes it correctly > es_from_icc(icc = -0.6, n_sample = 60, n_measurements = 3, + icc_type = "ICC(2,k)", icc_to_es = "raw") icc icc_se icc_ci_lo icc_ci_up n_sample n_measurements icc_type 1 -0.1428571 0.06135886 -0.2631183 -0.02259599 60 3 agreement # 3. through convert_df() the same row is nulled > d <- data.frame(study_id="a", icc=-0.6, n_sample=60, n_measurements=3, icc_type="ICC(2,k)") > s <- summary(convert_df(d, measure="icc", icc_to_es="raw", verbose=FALSE, + split_adjusted=FALSE), flags=TRUE, guidance=FALSE) study_id es se info_used 1 a NA NA NA FLAG: [INVALID] Impossible ICC for 3 measurements: 'icc' = -0.6 is below the lower bound -1/(k-1) = -0.5, set to missing # independent confirmation that the step-down is the right map (psych::ICC, n=40,k=4): ICC2k= 0.8699047 step-down -> 0.625702 psych ICC2= 0.625702 ICC3k= 0.9038208 step-down -> 0.7014314 psych ICC3= 0.7014314

**Impact.** A meta-analyst pooling test-retest / inter-rater ICCs loses any average-measures row whose reported ICC_k is negative and below -1/(k-1) (k >= 3), and is told the value is arithmetically impossible when it is not. The row silently leaves the pool (es = NA) rather than entering at rho_1. Narrow — negative average-measures ICCs are uncommon — but it is a data-destroying false positive with a confidently wrong message, and it also suppresses the V25 note on the same row.

**Fix.** Gate V41 on the single-measures family, e.g. compute `icc_type_eff <- .normalise_icc_type(...)` before the V41 block and add `& !.icc_is_average(icc_type_eff)` to the `icc_below_floor` condition; or apply V41 to the stepped-down value `.icc_step_down(icc, k)` for average rows. Add an average-measures case to the V41 test block.

**Verifier correction.** The finding stands as filed; two refinements. (a) Scope is a little broader than "negative average-measures ICCs are uncommon" suggests: the trigger band is rho_k < -1/(k-1), which widens with k, so at k = 6 an ordinary ICC(3,k) = -0.25 (route value -0.0345) is nulled, not just extreme negatives. (b) Under correct_inputs = FALSE the row emits a directly self-contradictory flag pair — "[INVALID] Impossible ICC for 3 measurements: 'icc' = -0.6 is below the lower bound -1/(k-1) = -0.5" alongside "[INFO] Average-measures ICC (-0.6, k = 3) stepped down to the single-measurement ICC -0.1429 with Spearman-Brown" — i.e. exactly the contradictory-pair failure the V41/V25 interlock was written to avoid; under the default correct_inputs = TRUE the icc is NA'd before the average-measures block, so the [INFO] step-down note is suppressed and only the wrong [INVALID] survives. Also worth noting when fixing: V41's own justification (the SE factor (1+(k-1)rho) collapsing, 18.8x weight) cannot apply to average rows, since the stepped-down rho_1 >= -1/(2k-1) bounds that factor at >= k/(2k-1) >= 0.5, i.e. at most ~4x weight inflation. The fix as proposed is correct, but the icc_type_eff computation currently sits below the V41 block and must be hoisted above it. Adjacent and out of scope: ICC_k is genuinely unbounded below (ICC(3,k) = 1 - MSE/MSR), so V11's [-1,1] bound on `icc` is also strictly wrong for the average family — but the route's own abs(icc) <= 1 step-down gate drops those rows regardless, so that is a separate design question rather than part of this fix.

### icc = 1 under icc_to_es = "raw" is dropped with no flag at all, because V40 skips the raw scale on a premise that holds for alpha but not for ICC
`R/internal_flags.R:656` — subsystem `icc` — refuted by **0 of 3** verifiers

**Claim.** V40 exists precisely so that a reliability of exactly 1.00 is not 'dropped without a message'. It short-circuits with `if (identical(as.character(sc), "raw")) next` on the stated ground that 'on the raw scale 1.00 is a perfectly usable boundary value (es = 1, se = 0)'. That is true for `es_from_cronbach_alpha()` but false for `es_from_icc()`, whose gate is scale-independent: `valid_es <- !is.na(icc) & icc >= -1 & icc < 1` (R/es_from_ICC.R:245). So under `icc_to_es = "raw"` an ICC of 1.00 yields es = NA with no explanatory flag, and `es_guidance` reports the row as '[complete input]' and suggests adding an SE, which cannot help.
