**Evidence.** $ Rscript -e "..." > es_from_icc(icc = 1, n_sample = 60, n_measurements = 2, icc_to_es = "raw") icc icc_se icc_ci_lo icc_ci_up n_sample n_measurements icc_type info_used 1 NA NA NA NA 60 2 agreement icc > es_from_cronbach_alpha(cronbach_alpha = 1, n_sample = 60, n_items = 10, alpha_to_es = "raw") alpha alpha_se alpha_ci_lo alpha_ci_up n_sample n_items info_used 1 1 0 1 1 60 10 cronbach_alpha # <- alpha DOES return the boundary > d <- data.frame(study_id=c("a","b"), icc=c(1,0.8), n_sample=60, n_measurements=2) > s <- summary(convert_df(d, measure="icc", icc_to_es="raw", verbose=FALSE, + split_adjusted=FALSE), flags=TRUE, guidance=TRUE) study_id es se 2 a NA NA 1 b 0.8 0.04686801 es_guidance[a] = "ICC (needs n_sample + n_measurements, or a reported icc_se / icc_ci_lo + icc_ci_up) [complete input]: only add 'icc_se' + 'icc_ci_lo' + 'icc_ci_up'" flags[a] = "[INFO] ICC agreement-type SE assumes negligible between-rater variance ..." # <- no V40, no explanation of why es is NA (On the default bonett scale the behaviour is correct: V40 fires and the drop is explained.)

**Impact.** A study reporting 'ICC = 1.00' (common for a short subscale, or a paper rounding .996) analysed on the raw scale disappears from the pool with no message and with guidance that misdirects the user. Sample-size accounting in a PRISMA flow becomes wrong by that row. Not a wrong number, but the exact unexplained-drop failure mode V40 was written to close.

**Fix.** Either (a) make the raw-scale ICC boundary usable like alpha's (relax `valid_es` to `icc <= 1` when `icc_to_es == "raw"`, emitting es = 1, se = 0), or (b) keep the route as is and remove ICC from V40's `raw` short-circuit so the flag fires on both scales with a message that says the row drops on either scale.

**Verifier correction.** V40's raw-scale short-circuit (`if (identical(as.character(sc), "raw")) next`, R/internal_flags.R:656) rests on the premise "on the raw scale 1.00 is a perfectly usable boundary value (es = 1, se = 0)", which holds for `es_from_cronbach_alpha()` (R/es_from_ALPHA.R:207) and `es_from_omega()` (R/es_from_OMEGA.R:279) but not for `es_from_icc()`, whose gate `valid_es <- !is.na(icc) & icc >= -1 & icc < 1` (R/es_from_ICC.R:245) is scale-independent. Two consequences, both reporting failures rather than numeric ones: (1) Under `icc_to_es = "raw"`, an ICC of exactly 1.00 yields es = NA with no V40 flag, while `es_guidance` reports "[complete input]: only add 'icc_se' + 'icc_ci_lo' + 'icc_ci_up'" — a remedy verified not to work, since the point-estimate gate fires before any SE route. (2) Broader than filed: on the DEFAULT bonett scale V40 does fire, but its remedy clause "or set the analysis scale to 'raw', where 1 is a usable boundary" is false for ICC, so the flag routes the user into failure (1). The misdirection therefore reaches the default configuration. The finding's fix option (a) is wrong and should not be taken: emitting es = 1, se = 0 on the raw scale gives an infinite inverse-variance weight (the raw ICC SE is (1-rho) x the transformed-scale SE, exactly 0 at rho = 1) and reproduces alpha's known hazard, which CLAUDE.md records as making rma() abort. The ICC route's decision to drop the boundary is the better statistics. Correct fix: (b) remove ICC from V40's raw short-circuit so the flag fires on both scales, AND make the message scale-aware — for ICC it must say the row drops on either scale rather than pointing at 'raw'. If the boundary is ever opened for ICC, follow omega's shape (permit icc == 1 on the raw scale only when the study reported its own icc_se or CI, never from the closed-form (n, k) route). Severity minor: no wrong estimate, SE, or weight; the harm is an unexplained drop plus actively misleading guidance, on rows reporting ICC = 1.00.

### The average-measures step-down runs AFTER the .ci_lower/.ci_upper transposition guard and can itself invert the interval, yielding a plausible SE from a sign-flipped CI
`R/es_from_ICC.R:221` — subsystem `icc` — refuted by **0 of 3** verifiers

**Claim.** `ci_lo <- .ci_lower(...)`, `ci_up <- .ci_upper(...)` run at lines 197-198, then `.icc_step_down()` is applied to each bound at lines 221-222. `.icc_step_down(x, k) = x/(k-(k-1)x)` has a denominator that changes sign once `x > k/(k-1)`, so a reported upper bound above that threshold is mapped to a NEGATIVE number while the lower bound stays positive: the ordering guard has already been spent and nothing re-checks it. The comment at lines 217-219 asserts 'Spearman-Brown is monotone increasing ... so the CI bounds map straight through and keep their order', which is only true on x < k/(k-1). `icc_ci_lo`/`icc_ci_up` are not in `.bounded_columns` (only `icc` is), so an out-of-range bound is never neutralised upstream either, and the CI-triplet check does not fire (the point estimate is inside the reported interval).

**Evidence.** $ Rscript -e "..." > es_from_icc(icc = 0.90, n_measurements = 10, icc_type = "ICC(2,k)", + icc_ci_lo = 0.80, icc_ci_up = 1.20, icc_to_es = "raw") icc icc_se icc_ci_lo icc_ci_up n_sample n_measurements icc_type 1 0.4736842 0.4555477 -0.4191729 1.366541 NA 10 agreement > metaConvert:::.icc_step_down(0.80, 10); metaConvert:::.icc_step_down(1.20, 10) [1] 0.2857143 [1] -1.5 # <- upper bound now BELOW the lower bound # the emitted SE is |(-1.5) - 0.2857143| / (2*qnorm(.975)) = 0.4555477, # i.e. a finite, plausible-looking standard error built from a transposed interval. # Correct handling would be NA (an ICC CI bound of 1.20 is out of range). By contrast, in-range bounds map correctly and monotonically: > es_from_icc(icc=0.94, n_measurements=6, icc_type="ICC(2,k)", + icc_ci_lo=0.86, icc_ci_up=0.98, icc_to_es="raw") icc icc_se ... # stepped: est 0.7230769 lo 0.5058824 up 0.8909091 1 0.7230769 0.09822291 # se = (0.8909091-0.5058824)/(2*qnorm(.975)) = 0.09822291 OK

**Impact.** Requires an out-of-range reported CI bound (> k/(k-1), so > 1) on an average-measures row, which is a data-entry error — but the package's own contract is that one bad cell must not produce a silently wrong number. Here it produces a finite SE and a finite CI instead of NA, so the row enters the pool with a fabricated weight and no flag.

**Fix.** Return NA from `.icc_step_down()` when `k - (k-1)*rho_k <= 0`, and/or re-apply `.ci_lower()`/`.ci_upper()` after the step-down block. Adding `icc_ci_lo`/`icc_ci_up` to `.bounded_columns` at [-1, 1] would close it upstream as well.

**Verifier correction.** ICC confidence-interval bounds (`icc_ci_lo` / `icc_ci_up`) are never range-checked -- neither by V11's `.bounded_columns` (which holds only `icc`) nor by the CI triplet at R/internal_flags.R:407 (which tests ordering and containment only) -- so an impossible bound (> 1) reaches the SE construction in `es_from_icc()` and produces a finite, plausible-looking standard error with no `[INVALID]` flag, silently pre-empting the closed-form (n, k) route. The average-measures step-down is the most spectacular instance, not the whole defect. `.icc_step_down(x, k) = x/(k-(k-1)x)` is a Mobius map: increasing on each branch, but with a pole at `x = k/(k-1)`, so a bound above that threshold maps to a negative number below the (positive) mapped lower bound. The comment at R/es_from_ICC.R:217-219 is therefore correct only on `x < k/(k-1)`, and `.icc_step_down()`'s own comment (lines 441-444) justifies its safety with "V11 already bounds icc to [-1, 1]" -- a guarantee that covers `icc` but not the two CI-bound arguments it is called with at lines 221-222. But the same wrong-SE outcome occurs with no sign flip at all: `ci_up = 1.05, k = 10` keeps the denominator positive and the ordering intact yet emits `se = 0.4141` on the raw scale from a stepped bound of 1.909; and a plain single-measures row with `icc_to_es = "raw"` and `ci = [0.80, 1.20]` emits `se = 0.1020` with no step-down involved. Impact direction, corrected: the emitted SE is INFLATED relative to correct handling, not deflated -- 0.3196 vs the closed form's 0.1121 on the Bonett default (~8x less inverse-variance weight; ~60x on the raw scale). The row therefore enters the pool with a meaningless but conservatively small weight, not the over-weighting that "fabricated weight" implies. Severity stays minor. Fix, corrected: (a) add `icc_ci_lo` / `icc_ci_up` to `.bounded_columns` at [-1, 1] -- the upstream fix, which closes both the flip and the (1, k/(k-1)) blow-up, and matches the treatment `icc` already gets; and (b) return NA from `.icc_step_down()` when `k - (k-1)*rho_k <= 0`, plus a same-check guard for the direct-call route (exported calculator, called by metaumbrella, which never sees `.validate_input_data()`). DROP the finding's second suggestion -- re-applying `.ci_lower()`/`.ci_upper()` after the step-down block is a verified no-op, since the SE is built with `abs()`, the `ci_usable` gate is symmetric, and the output CI is rebuilt as `es +/- z*se`. Corroborating internal inconsistency worth citing in the fix: `test-reliability-generalization.R:844` already pins that an `icc_ci_up` of exactly 1.00 is refused on the Bonett scale, so a strictly impossible 1.20 passing is an inconsistency within the route's own contract.

### V38 (mixed omega estimands) never fires when omega_type is left blank on the default rows — the rescue line that would make it fire is dead code
`R/internal_flags.R:1915` — subsystem `omega` — refuted by **0 of 3** verifiers

**Claim.** V38 is documented (CLAUDE.md, ?es_from_omega) as always-on and unable to false-fire, because "two omega_type values in one pool are different estimands by definition". The code contains a line whose evident purpose is to make a blank omega_type resolve to the documented default "total" before the comparison: `ot[is.na(ot) & is.finite(om)] <- "total"`. That line can never execute. `.normalise_omega_type()` is called on the line immediately above and already maps NA -> "unspecified" (R/es_from_OMEGA.R:396), and `convert_df()` has additionally rewritten the stored column through the same normaliser at R/main_convert_df.R:544-545 before validation runs. The very next statement then excludes "unspecified" from the comparison. Net effect: a pool of blank rows (which the extraction sheet documents as defaulting to 'total') plus explicit 'hierarchical' rows is silently pooled with no flag. The ICC sibling V43 handles exactly this case correctly and deliberately — R/main_convert_df.R:555-559 preserves NA for icc_type with the comment "NA is left as NA... V31 and es_from_icc() already treat an absent value as the default" — and CLAUDE.md states for V43: "An absent/NA icc_type resolves to agreement (the documented default), so blanks + explicit consistency IS a mix and correctly fires." omega has no such NA preservation. A second, smaller inconsistency falls out of the same cause: es_from_omega() itself sets NA -> "total" (R/es_from_OMEGA.R:243), so a direct call records omega_type = "total" for a blank while the pipeline records "unspecified" for identical input — and that column is what a user meta-regresses on.

**Evidence.** R code run: cat("normalise(NA) ->", metaConvert:::.normalise_omega_type(NA), "\n") d <- data.frame(study_id=c("A","B","C"), omega=c(.86,.72,.88), omega_se=0.02, omega_type=c(NA,"hierarchical",NA), n_sample=300, n_items=12) s <- summary(convert_df(d, measure="omega", verbose=FALSE, split_adjusted=FALSE), flags=TRUE) cat("V38 fires on blanks+hierarchical? ", any(grepl("mixes omega estimands", s$flags)), "\n") d2 <- d; d2$omega_type <- c("total","hierarchical","total") # control s2 <- summary(convert_df(d2, measure="omega", verbose=FALSE, split_adjusted=FALSE), flags=TRUE) cat("fires? ", any(grepl("mixes omega estimands", s2$flags)), "\n") cat("direct route records:", es_from_omega(0.8, omega_se=.02, omega_type=NA)$omega_type, "\n") cat("pipeline records:", paste(attr(r,'raw_data')$omega_type, collapse=","), "\n") # ICC analogue d3 <- data.frame(study_id=c("A","B"), icc=c(.8,.75), icc_se=c(.05,.06), icc_type=c(NA,"consistency"), n_sample=100, n_measurements=2) s3 <- summary(convert_df(d3, measure="icc", verbose=FALSE, split_adjusted=FALSE), flags=TRUE) Actual output: normalise(NA) -> unspecified V38 fires on blanks+hierarchical? FALSE s$flags = [1] "" "" "" <-- three empty flag strings fires? TRUE <-- control: explicit 'total' + 'hierarchical' does fire direct route records: total pipeline records: unspecified,hierarchical,unspecified V43 fires on blanks+consistency? TRUE <-- the ICC sibling handles it So the guard line at internal_flags.R:1915 is provably unreachable (its `is.na(ot)` condition is FALSE for every row by construction), and the flag is silent on the case it was written to catch.

**Impact.** A reliability-generalisation reviewer who records omega_type only when it is NOT the default — the ordinary extraction habit, and the one the sheet's "'total' (default)" wording encourages — pools omega_total with omega_hierarchical and receives no flag at all. Those are different estimands (omega_h is systematically smaller; in Flora's own PCS example .91 vs .97), so the pooled coefficient estimates neither, and nothing else in the package can reveal it: omega_type has zero effect on the arithmetic, so no numeric check can see the mix. This is the exact scenario V38 exists for. Frequency scales with how many rows are left blank, i.e. most real datasets.

**Fix.** Mirror the icc_type treatment in convert_df(). In R/main_convert_df.R:544-545, preserve NA through the normaliser the way icc_type does: if ("omega_type" %in% colnames(x)) { keep_na <- is.na(x$omega_type) x$omega_type <- .normalise_omega_type(x$omega_type, warn = FALSE) x$omega_type[keep_na] <- NA } The existing line at internal_flags.R:1915 then becomes live and resolves blanks to "total" as intended. Alternatively, if blanks are meant to be excluded, delete line 1915 and say so in the V38 comment and in ?es_from_omega, and drop the `omega_type[is.na(omega_type)] <- "total"` at es_from_OMEGA.R:243 so the two entry points agree on what they record.

**Verifier correction.** V38 (mixed omega estimands) is silent when omega_type is left blank, because a REGRESSION in commit 17c5813 killed the line written to handle that case. R/internal_flags.R:1915 (`ot[is.na(ot) & is.finite(om)] <- "total"`) is unreachable: .normalise_omega_type()'s `out[is.na(out)] <- "unspecified"` (R/es_from_OMEGA.R:396) catches NA as well as unrecognised keys, and convert_df() has already rewritten the stored column through the same normaliser at R/main_convert_df.R:544-545 before validation runs. The next statement excludes "unspecified", so a pool of blank rows plus explicit 'hierarchical' rows is pooled with no flag — while the ICC sibling V43 fires on the identical shape, because R/main_convert_df.R:555-559 preserves icc_type NA on purpose. Not a design choice: at 515ce68 V38 used raw `as.character(x[["omega_type"]])`, NA survived and the rescue line was live. 17c5813's own commit message says its target was unrecognised strings being relabelled as 'total' (a correct fix); NA falling into the same bucket was collateral, and no test pins it (test-reliability-generalization.R:467 pins only the unrecognised-string case). Impact as filed is honest, not inflated: [INFO] severity, zero effect on any computed number, no wrong weight. The harm is that ?es_from_omega documents omega_type = "total" as the default and promises "convert_df() flags a pool that mixes them", and the flag does not — for the most common extraction habit (record the type only when it is not the default). Since omega_type has no arithmetic effect, no other check in the package can see the mix. The .91 vs .97 Flora (2020) gap matches the repo's own vignette. Severity: minor. Fix is correctly located (main_convert_df.R, not internal_flags.R — patching the flag alone cannot work, since it already receives the normalised column), with two corrections to the finding's write-up: 1. The primary fix does NOT close the entry-point inconsistency the finding raises second: with NA preserved, the pipeline records NA where a direct es_from_omega() call records "total". That is acceptable only because it is exactly what icc_type already does, so the fix buys consistency with the sibling rather than identity between entry points. 2. After the fix, the "it cannot false-fire: two different omega_type values in one pool are different estimands by definition" line (CLAUDE.md and the V38 code comment) becomes inaccurate and must be reworded, since the flag would then rest on a blank resolving to a documented DEFAULT rather than on a value the extractor recorded.

### omega_se_source = "closed_form" silently produces se = NA (row dropped from the pool) when n_sample is absent, and ?es_from_omega's own @param tags tell the user n_sample and n_items are not used for the SE
`R/es_from_OMEGA.R:7` — subsystem `omega` — refuted by **0 of 3** verifiers

**Claim.** The Rd states, in @param n_sample: "Not used to compute the standard error (see Details); carried through for reporting and moderator analyses", and in @param n_items: "Carried through for reporting; not used in the standard error." Both statements are false whenever the same function's own omega_se_source = "closed_form" is selected — that branch (lines 329-343) is gated on `!is.na(n_sample) & n_sample > 2 & !is.na(n_items) & n_items >= 2` and computes the SE from exactly those two quantities. The extraction sheet repeats the claim: data_extraction.R:538 describes n_items as "not used in omega's variance". When the gate fails the branch is skipped silently — no warning, no flag — and summary() then drops the row. The guidance system does not close the gap either: .method_required_columns()'s omega entry (internal_guidance.R:451-453) lists only `specific = "omega"`, `shared = "omega_se"`, so it advises adding omega_se and never mentions n_sample, even in a run where the user has explicitly opted into the (n, k) route. The 2.0.1 NEWS.md entries make no mention of omega, es_from_omega(), measure = "omega", omega_to_es or omega_se_source at all, so a user has no other place to learn this.

**Evidence.** R code run (a user who followed the @param text and did not extract n_sample): d <- data.frame(study_id=c("A","B"), omega=c(.86,.90), n_items=c(10,10)) r <- convert_df(d, measure="omega", verbose=FALSE, split_adjusted=FALSE, omega_se_source="closed_form") s <- summary(r, guidance=TRUE) cat("es:", s$es, " se:", s$se, "\n"); cat("guidance:", s$es_guidance, "\n") cat("direct:", es_from_omega(0.88, n_items=8, omega_se_source='closed_form')$omega_se, "\n") cat("with n:", es_from_omega(0.88, n_sample=300, n_items=8, omega_se_source='closed_form')$omega_se, "\n") Actual output: es: NA NA se: NA NA guidance: McDonald's omega (needs omega_se on the natural scale, or omega_ci_lo + omega_ci_up) [complete input]: only add 'omega_se' (x2) direct: NA with n: 0.08757958 No warning is emitted anywhere. Adding n_sample = 300 alone turns the same rows into a fully poolable SE. Also confirmed that the closed form itself is correct and IS on the selected scale (so this is purely a doc/gating-visibility defect, not an arithmetic one): metafor::escalc(measure="ABT", ai=.86, mi=12, ni=300) -> sqrt(vi) = 0.08556598344 es_from_omega(.86, n_sample=300, n_items=12, omega_to_es="bonett", omega_se_source="closed_form")$omega_se = 0.08556598344 metafor::escalc(measure="AHW", ai=.86, mi=12, ni=300) -> yi = 0.4807505898, sqrt(vi) = 0.01479623897 es_from_omega(..., omega_to_es="hakstian_whalen", omega_se_source="closed_form") -> omega = 0.4807505898, omega_se = 0.01479623897

**Impact.** Studies vanish from the pool, silently, in exactly the workflow the new option was added to serve (reproducing the published omega RG literature, which computes variances this way). The pooled estimate, tau^2 and I^2 all change by omission, and the user is told to go find an omega_se that no primary study reports — the very thing omega_se_source was introduced to avoid. Because summary() reports es = NA rather than raising anything, the loss is invisible unless the user counts rows.

**Fix.** Three one-line changes. (1) Amend @param n_sample / @param n_items in R/es_from_OMEGA.R to say "not used for the standard error unless omega_se_source = 'closed_form', which requires both", and fix the matching sheet text at R/data_extraction.R:538. (2) Make the guidance conditional: pass omega_se_source through to .add_es_guidance() and, when it is "closed_form", add n_sample/n_items to the omega entry's required columns so a bare row is told what it actually needs. (3) Add omega, measure = "omega", omega_to_es and the two *_se_source arguments to the 2.0.1 NEWS.md block.

**Verifier correction.** `?es_from_omega` contradicts itself about `n_sample`/`n_items`, and the missing-data guidance does not know about the closed-form route. `@param n_sample` ("Not used to compute the standard error (see Details)") and `@param n_items` ("Carried through for reporting; not used in the standard error") in `R/es_from_OMEGA.R:7-11` are stated unconditionally, and are false in the one mode the same help page's `@param omega_se_source` introduces: under `omega_se_source = "closed_form"` the SE branch at lines 329-343 is gated on, and computed from, exactly those two quantities. The stale text ships in `man/es_from_omega.Rd:33`. The same page's `@details` SE-precedence enumeration, its `@return` "required input data" line, and its bare-omega example comment all likewise stop at "otherwise NA"; `R/data_extraction.R:538` ("not used in omega's variance") and `vignettes/Psychometrics.Rmd:274` repeat it. (The vignette's separate sentence "Omega has no closed-form sampling variance in (n, k)" is statistically correct and must stay — the option borrows *alpha's* variance rather than supplying omega's.) The functional half is the guidance system: `.method_required_columns()`'s omega entry (`R/internal_guidance.R:451-453`) lists only `specific = "omega"`, `shared = "omega_se"`, and `.method_descriptions()` says "needs omega_se … or omega_ci_lo + omega_ci_up". So in a run where the user has explicitly opted into `omega_se_source = "closed_form"` but omitted `n_sample`, the row silently gets `se = NA`, is dropped by `summary()`, and the guidance names only `omega_se` — advice that is valid but incomplete, sending the user to look for a statistic no primary study reports when `n_sample` alone would have sufficed. Reproduced: two rows with `omega` + `n_items` and no `n_sample` give `es: NA NA / se: NA NA` with guidance "only add 'omega_se'"; adding `n_sample = 300` yields a finite SE. The arithmetic is not in question: `omega_se = 0.08556598` matches `sqrt(vi)` from `metafor::escalc(measure = "ABT", ai = .86, mi = 12, ni = 300)` bit-for-bit, and the AHW scale matches likewise. Corrections to the filed version: (a) the NEWS claim is mostly wrong — HEAD's NEWS.md documents omega, `es_from_omega()`, `measure = "omega"` and `omega_to_es` extensively; only `omega_se_source` is absent, and that argument does not exist at HEAD at all (it is uncommitted), so its NEWS entry belongs to the commit that lands it. What HEAD NEWS *does* have that goes stale is "Omega does not use it [`n_items`] — omega has no (n, k) sampling variance" (lines 93-96). (b) "a user has no other place to learn this" is false: `?convert_df`'s `@param omega_se_source` documents the option in full and `@param omega_to_es` cross-references it. (c) Impact is milder than filed: `n_sample` is already in `data_extraction_sheet(measure = "omega")`, and returning NA rather than erroring with no `n` is deliberate and pinned (`tests/testthat/test-reliability-se-source.R:115-117`). This is a doc-contradiction plus an incomplete diagnostic message, with no numeric consequence. Fix: correct the `@param`/`@details`/`@return`/example text, the sheet string and the vignette sentence to say "not used for the standard error unless `omega_se_source = 'closed_form'`, which requires both"; and make the guidance conditional by recording `omega_se_source` on the metaConvert object and threading it into `.add_es_guidance()`. That last part is not a one-liner (the argument is currently stored nowhere outside `main_convert_df.R`), and it must stay conditional: unlike alpha, whose default *is* `closed_form`, omega defaults to `"reported"`, so listing `n_sample`/`n_items` unconditionally would advise a route that cannot work.

### D1 (cross-row ES outlier) minimum-deviation floor omits "rp"/"zp", so a homogeneous partial-correlation pool raises outlier flags that the identical values entered as ordinary correlations do not
`R/internal_flags.R:2795` — subsystem `correlation-family` — refuted by **0 of 3** verifiers

**Claim.** The `min_dev` switch in `.flag_cross_row_outliers()` names `"r" = 0.3` and `"z" = 0.5` but has no entry for `"rp"` or `"zp"`, so both fall to the `0` default ("no floor for md, nnt, prop"). The floor exists precisely to stop D1 flagging ordinary heterogeneity in a tight pool; without it, a partial-correlation pool whose IQR is small gets outlier flags at deviations far below the 0.3 the same code deems non-diagnostic for `r`. This is the counterpart (false positive) of the previous finding's false negative, from the same missing-`rp` gate family, and in the very function whose sibling checks c273547 repaired.

**Evidence.** Ten studies with rp = .30 .30 .31 .31 .32 .32 .33 .33 .34 .40 at n = 200, q = 0, entered through `linreg_t` (measure="rp"), then the identical ten values entered as `pearson_r` (measure="r"). ```r rpv <- c(.30,.30,.31,.31,.32,.32,.33,.33,.34,.40); n <- 200; df <- n-2 tt <- rpv*sqrt(df)/sqrt(1-rpv^2) d1 <- data.frame(study_id=paste0("S",1:10), linreg_t=tt, n_sample=n, n_covariates=0) summary(convert_df(d1, measure="rp", verbose=FALSE), flags=TRUE) d2 <- data.frame(study_id=paste0("S",1:10), pearson_r=rpv, n_sample=n) summary(convert_df(d2, measure="r", verbose=FALSE), flags=TRUE) ``` ACTUAL OUTPUT: == measure = 'rp' == (1 quality flag raised) es flag 1 0.30 ... 9 0.34 10 0.40 [UNUSUAL] ES outlier, IQR method: rp = 0.4 (from linreg_t) == measure = 'r' (same values) == (0 quality flags raised) es flag 1 0.30 ... 10 0.40 The deviation from the median is |0.40 - 0.32| = 0.08, well under the 0.3 floor the same switch applies to `r`; because `rp` falls through to floor 0 it is flagged.

**Impact.** Spurious `[UNUSUAL] ES outlier` flags on ordinary, homogeneous partial-correlation pools. Not a wrong number, but it degrades the signal-to-noise of the flag column exactly where the package is telling users to look, and it makes the rp diagnostic behaviour inconsistent with the r diagnostic behaviour for the same values.

**Fix.** In the `min_dev` switch at R/internal_flags.R:2794-2796, extend the two entries: `"r" = , "rp" = 0.3,` and `"z" = , "zp" = 0.5,` (matching the fall-through style already used for `"d" = , "g" = 1.0`).

**Verifier correction.** CONFIRMED, with the scope widened. The `min_dev` switch in `.flag_cross_row_outliers()` (R/internal_flags.R:2792-2803) names `"d"`/`"g"` = 1.0, `"r"` = 0.3, `"z"` = 0.5, `"rd"` = 0.3 and the reliability coefficients, but omits FOUR measures that belong to those families: `"rp"`, `"zp"`, `"dw"` and `"gw"`. All four fall to the `0` default whose comment reserves it for "md, nnt, prop" (arbitrary-unit measures where a floor is meaningless). Consequence: D1 fires on homogeneous rp/zp/dw/gw pools at deviations the identical code calls non-diagnostic for r/z/d/g. Verified by direct call across measures on one pool (es = .30 .30 .31 .31 .32 .32 .33 .33 .34 .42, se = .07): d/g/r/rp/z/zp/rd/alpha fired=0 (rp/zp only after patching), dw/gw/md/mdw fired=1 — md/mdw correctly floorless, dw/gw not. End-to-end reproduction of the rp half: ten studies rp = .30 .30 .31 .31 .32 .32 .33 .33 .34 .40 at n=200, q=0 via `linreg_t` with `measure="rp"` raise `[UNUSUAL] ES outlier, IQR method: rp = 0.4 (from linreg_t)` on row 10 (|0.40 − 0.32| = 0.08, well under the 0.3 floor `r` gets); the identical values as `pearson_r` with `measure="r"` raise nothing. `measure="zp"` flags `zp = 0.424` where `measure="z"` on `atanh()` of the same values is silent. Passing `flag_options = list(outlier_min_deviation = 0.3)` silences the rp flag, confirming the floor is the sole mechanism. Statistical basis is sound: rp is bounded [-1,1] identically to r (and an rp pool mixing covariate sets is if anything MORE heterogeneous, so 0.3 is conservative for it); zp is on the same Fisher scale as z; dw/gw are SMDs in SD units identical to d/g. Every sibling gate in the package already groups them this way — F1/F2 (`"r" = , "z" = , "rp" = , "zp" = 0.3`; `"d" = , "g" = , "dw" = , "gw" = 0.5`), E1/E3 (`measure %in% c("r","rp")`, `c("d","g","dw","gw","md","mdw")`), Category G's `signed_measures`, `.parameters_for_measure()`'s `cor_like`. Commit c273547 performed exactly this repair for F1/F2/E1/E3 and did not reach D1; nothing in NEWS.md at HEAD claims a deliberate D1 exclusion. CORRECTED FIX (the filed one-liner closes only half the gap): ```r "d" = , "g" = , "dw" = , "gw" = 1.0, # 1 SD "r" = , "rp" = 0.3, "z" = , "zp" = 0.5, ``` `md`/`mdw`/`nnt`/`prop` correctly keep the 0 fallback. CLAUDE.md's Category D sentence ("defaults: 1.0 for d/g/logor/logrr/logirr/loghr, 0.3 for r/rd, 0.5 for z") needs the same widening. One caveat worth stating, not a reason to refuse: a 0.3 floor makes D1 hard to reach for typical rp pools (partial correlations are attenuated by conditioning), but that is precisely the trade-off the package already accepted for `r`, so the fix restores consistency rather than creating a new blind spot. SEVERITY: minor, as filed and as verified. This is flag-column signal-to-noise only — no estimate, SE, CI or inverse-variance weight changes, and the false positives are confined to tight pools where the IQR rule alone is over-sensitive. Fix verified regression-free (targeted flag suites 0 fail/0 error; no archived test pins D1 for rp/zp/dw/gw).

### smd_var = "hedges_olkin" leaks into the r/z sampling variance through prec_ratio, so the documented "crude precision ratio is exactly 1 / bit-identical to metafor RBIS" invariant holds only for the default smd_var
`R/internal_multiple_formulas.R:1156` — subsystem `correlation-family` — refuted by **0 of 3** verifiers

**Claim.** `.smd_to_cor()` computes `vd_crude <- (n_exp+n_nexp)/(n_exp*n_nexp) + d^2/(2*(n_exp+n_nexp))` - hard-coded to the Borenstein/LS2 form - and rescales Soper's closed-form biserial variance by `vd/vd_crude`. Its own comment states the ratio "is then exactly 1" in the crude case "since vd_crude below is the same expression .es_from_d() uses". That is only true when `smd_var = "borenstein"`. Under the documented opt-in `smd_var = "hedges_olkin"` (alias "viechtbauer"), `.es_from_d()` builds the default d variance as `(leading + (d*J)^2/(2N))/J^2`, which is NOT `vd_crude`, so an ordinary crude two-group row gets `prec_ratio = 1.0257` and both `r_se^2` and `z_se^2` are inflated. `smd_var` is documented throughout as affecting the SMD standard error only; nothing says it also moves the biserial correlation's variance away from Soper's exact form, and `z_se^2` stops being the stabilised `1/(N-1)` that the viechtbauer transform is constructed to deliver. The pinning test (tests/testthat/test-smd-to-cor-variance.R:28, "the crude precision ratio is exactly 1 (no drift on ordinary rows)") only exercises the default.

**Evidence.** ```r suppressMessages(devtools::load_all('.', quiet=TRUE)) a <- es_from_cohen_d(cohen_d=0.5, n_exp=30, n_nexp=30, smd_var='borenstein') b <- es_from_cohen_d(cohen_d=0.5, n_exp=30, n_nexp=30, smd_var='hedges_olkin') mf <- metafor::escalc(measure='RBIS', di=0.5, n1i=30, n2i=30) ``` ACTUAL OUTPUT: r bor=0.308856745889533 HO=0.308856745889533 metafor=0.308856745889533 vr bor=0.022735844578656 HO=0.023320273940359 metafor=0.022735844578656 HO/mf=1.025705 vz bor=0.016949152542373 HO=0.017384833845853 1/(N-1)=0.016949152542373 vd bor=0.068750000000000 HO=0.070517232287240 ratio=1.025705 The point estimate is untouched; the variance is exactly the d-scale convention ratio (1.025705) away from Soper's / metafor's value, and z_se^2 is no longer 1/(N-1). Sweep over other crude cells (same script pattern): n=30/30 d=0.5 vr HO/metafor = 1.025705 n=20/60 d=0.5 1.019138 n=30/30 d=1.3 vr HO/metafor = 1.021885 n=20/60 d=1.3 1.016907 Under the default this is exact (verified over es_from_cohen_d / means_sd / means_sd_pooled / means_se / student_t / anova_f / etasq / md_sd / md_se / md_ci at n=50/50, d=0.6: `identical(e$r, as.numeric(mf$yi)) && identical(e$r_se^2, as.numeric(mf$vi))` TRUE in all cases).

**Impact.** Only reachable for users who opt into `smd_var = "hedges_olkin"` (or the per-row `smd_var` column) AND ask for `measure = "r"`/"z". Those rows get a correlation sampling variance ~1.7-2.6% larger than Soper's exact biserial variance (up to ~5% weight change), with no note anywhere that the SMD variance convention propagates into the correlation. Small, and defensible as coherent propagation, but it contradicts the stated invariant and is untested.

**Fix.** Either (a) make `vd_crude` in .smd_to_cor() match the smd_var convention actually in force (thread `smd_var`/`use_LS` in from .es_from_d() and build vd_crude the same way the default d_se is built), so the crude ratio is 1 under both conventions and RBIS agreement is preserved; or (b) if the leak is intended, correct the comment at R/internal_multiple_formulas.R:1147-1155 and add a `smd_var = "hedges_olkin"` case to tests/testthat/test-smd-to-cor-variance.R so the behaviour is pinned rather than incidental. I recommend (a): the biserial variance has its own exact closed form and should not depend on which of two estimators of Var(d) the user selected.

**Verifier correction.** CONFIRMED with two corrections (magnitude scope, and the fix). Accurate statement: `.smd_to_cor()` normalises Soper's biserial variance by a hard-coded Borenstein/LS2 `vd_crude`, while `.es_from_d()` builds the default `d_se` under whichever `smd_var` convention is in force. Under the opt-in `smd_var = "hedges_olkin"` (alias "viechtbauer"), a crude two-group row therefore gets `prec_ratio != 1`, and both `r_se^2` and `z_se^2` are inflated by essentially `1/J^2 = 1 + 3/(2(N-2))` approx — a pure small-sample estimator-convention factor carrying no design information. Only the default `smd_to_cor = "viechtbauer"` branch is affected; `lipsey_cooper` propagates `vd` directly by design and breaks no closed-form invariant. The point estimate is untouched. Correction 1 — magnitude. The variance inflation is N-dependent, not a flat 1.7-2.6%: 11.6% at N=16, 8.8% at N=20, 5.5% at N=30, 2.6% at N=60, 1.9% at N=80, 0.74% at N=200, 0.29% at N=500. The corresponding inverse-variance weight loss is ~10.4% at N=16 and ~5.2% at N=30, so "up to ~5% weight change" understates the small-N end (though it is exactly the regime where the whole distinction matters). Direction is conservative (weight reduced), so no interval becomes anti-conservative. Correction 2 — the fix. Recommended fix (a) as written ("build vd_crude the same way the default d_se is built") repairs the crude and ANCOVA default-SE rows but introduces a NEW, previously absent smd_var dependence on user-supplied-SE rows: today their `vd` is the user's number and `vd_crude` is convention-free, so `smd_var` does not touch them; under (a) the denominator would move by J^2 and their `r_se` would start depending on `smd_var`. The surgical fix is instead to normalise only the convention the package itself applied — i.e. when `d_se` was defaulted AND `smd_var` resolves to "LS", divide the convention factor back out before forming `prec_ratio` (equivalently, pass `.smd_to_cor()` the LS2-convention default variance as the numerator's reference), leaving user-supplied-SE, ANCOVA (1-R^2), pre-post and Glass rescaling exactly as they are. Whichever route is taken, the comment at R/internal_multiple_formulas.R:1152-1155, the NEWS "still bit-identical to metafor RBIS" sentence and cran-comments item 4 must be scoped to `smd_var = "borenstein"`, and tests/testthat/test-smd-to-cor-variance.R needs a `hedges_olkin` case so the behaviour is pinned rather than incidental.

### D1's `outlier_min_deviation` floor omits dw/gw (and rp/zp), so a within-group SMD pool raises ES-outlier flags the identical d/g pool deliberately suppresses
`R/internal_flags.R:2795` — subsystem `flags-tier2` — refuted by **0 of 3** verifiers

**Claim.** The `min_dev` switch in `.flag_cross_row_outliers()` lists `"d" = , "g" = 1.0`, `"r" = 0.3`, `"z" = 0.5`, but not `"dw"`, `"gw"`, `"rp"` or `"zp"`, which therefore fall through to the `0` default whose comment says "no floor for md, nnt, prop". dw/gw are standardised SMDs on exactly the same scale as d/g, and rp/zp on the same scale as r/z — this release added rp/zp everywhere else in the flag layer (B1, B1b, C4, F1/F2 ceilings and floors, E1/E3 thresholds) but not here. The floor exists specifically to "avoid flagging moderate heterogeneity in homogeneous meta-analyses"; that rationale applies unchanged to the omitted measures.

**Evidence.** Function-level, one pool, eight measures: ```r opts <- metaConvert:::.default_flag_options(); f <- metaConvert:::.flag_cross_row_outliers es <- c(0.50,0.50,0.51,0.51,0.52,0.52,0.53,0.53,0.54,0.70) # max dev from median = 0.18 for (m in c("d","dw","gw","r","rp","z","zp","logor")) f(es, rep(0.2,10), opts, info_used=rep("means_sd",10), measure=m, n_total=rep(100,10), n_exp=rep(50,10), n_nexp=rep(50,10)) ``` ``` median 0.52 IQR 0.02 3IQR 0.06 max dev 0.18 measure=d D1 flags rows: (none) measure=dw D1 flags rows: 10 measure=gw D1 flags rows: 10 measure=r D1 flags rows: (none) measure=rp D1 flags rows: 10 measure=z D1 flags rows: (none) measure=zp D1 flags rows: 10 measure=logor D1 flags rows: (none) ``` End-to-end through convert_df/summary (single-group pre/post rows, r_pre_post defaulted): ```r summary(convert_df(d, measure="gw", verbose=FALSE), flags=TRUE, guidance=FALSE) ``` ``` es 1 0.50 ... 10 0.70 10 : [INFO] Default r_pre_post = 0.8 used (not provided by user); [UNUSUAL] ES outlier, IQR method: gw = 0.7 (from means_sd_pre_post_single_group) ``` (measure="dw" behaves identically: `[UNUSUAL] ES outlier, IQR method: dw = 0.709`.)

**Impact.** Any within-group (pre/post) SMD review — the whole `dw`/`gw` measure family added in 1.1.0 — and any partial-correlation review gets spurious [UNUSUAL] ES-outlier flags on textbook-homogeneous pools, while the same numbers on the between-group measure are correctly silent. Reviewers are sent to primary sources for nothing.

**Fix.** Extend the switch: `"d" = , "g" = , "dw" = , "gw" = 1.0`, `"r" = , "rp" = 0.3`, `"z" = , "zp" = 0.5`. This mirrors the grouping already used by C1, F1/F2 and `.flag_internal_consistency`'s `diff_max`/`dispersion_max`.

**Verifier correction.** Accurate as filed, with three corrections to framing: (1) NOT a regression introduced by this release. `git show HEAD:R/internal_flags.R` shows the identical switch (lines 2701-2706) already missing dw/gw/rp/zp, and rp/zp were already present in the other flag-layer lists at HEAD (the working-tree diff there is comment rewording only). The finding's parenthetical "this release added rp/zp everywhere else in the flag layer but not here" mis-attributes timing; the omission is pre-existing and longstanding. (2) Impact magnitude should be the measured false-positive differential, not "any within-group SMD review gets spurious flags". Measured over 800 pools/cell at true effect 0.5, SE 0.20, tau in {0,.05,.15}: pools raising >=1 spurious D1 flag are 3.1-11.6% for gw against 0.0% for the identical g pool (k = 40 down to 6); rows flagged 0.08-2.2% vs 0.00%. The differential is systematic and one-sided, but the evidence's 10-row example (IQR 0.02) is an atypically tight pool. (3) Scope note worth keeping with the fix: `md`/`mdw` must stay floorless (arbitrary outcome units, as the existing comment says), and `rd` already has 0.3 — the proposed patch respects both. `zp = 0.5` mirrors `z`'s explicit min_dev entry and does not conflict with the separate, deliberate decision at line 3358-3363 to leave `z`/`zp` on the SMD fallback for E1/E3's diff_max/dispersion_max.

### D3/V27 mixes two spread scales inside one md pool (pooled SD vs se*sqrt(N) = 2*SD for balanced arms), producing a false "SE entered as SD" flag on a correct study
`R/internal_flags.R:3010` — subsystem `flags-tier2` — refuted by **0 of 3** verifiers

**Claim.** D3 defines a row's spread as the pooled arm SD when both arm SDs are present and as `se * sqrt(n_eff)` otherwise. For the default two-group `md` measure `SE_MD = SD * sqrt(1/n1 + 1/n2)`, so `se*sqrt(N) = 2*SD` at balanced arms — not `SD`. CLAUDE.md states the opposite ("for raw MD, SE_MD = SD/sqrt(N), so this recovers the pooled-SD scale"), which holds only for the within-subject `mdw` case. In a pool where some rows report arm SDs and some do not, the two definitions differ by exactly a factor of 2, and the check's own design principle (kept scale-consistent within a pool, as D2 is) is violated.

**Evidence.** Scale mismatch, identical studies: ```r SD <- 10; n1 <- n2 <- 50; N <- 100 se_md <- SD*sqrt(1/n1+1/n2) ``` ``` true pooled SD = 10 SE_md = 2 se*sqrt(N) = 20 <-- 'implied SD' spread used by D3 (rows 1-4 have arm SDs, rows 5-8 do not): [1] 10 10 10 10 20 20 20 20 median = 15 ``` Consequence — same 10 studies, only the *presence of transcribed arm SDs* changes: ```r SDtrue <- c(10,10,11,9,10,12,9,10,10, 3.5) # study 10 genuinely lower-variance (2.9x) se <- SDtrue*sqrt(1/50+1/50) f(rep(2,10), se, opts, info_used=rep("md_sd",10), measure="md", n_total=rep(100,10), n_exp=rep(50,10), n_nexp=rep(50,10), sd_exp=<...>, sd_nexp=<...>) ``` ``` A) NO row reports arm SDs (all spreads = se*sqrt(N) = 2*SD): flagged rows: B) ALL rows report arm SDs (all spreads = SD): flagged rows: C) MIXED - only the low-variance study reports its arm SDs: flagged rows: 10 message on row 10 in case C: [UNUSUAL] SD outlier: spread = 3.5 is 5.7x below the pool median (20) - an SD this small for the scale can mean a standard error was entered as a standard deviation (from md_sd) ``` The study is identical in all three runs; only case C fires, and it fires solely because the pool median was doubled by the rows that did not transcribe arm SDs.

**Impact.** Mixed-completeness md extraction sheets (the normal case) get false [UNUSUAL] flags accusing a legitimate low-variance study of an SE-as-SD extraction error; symmetrically, a genuine SE-as-SD row that lacks arm SDs needs to be 10x rather than 5x below the median to be caught, halving the check's sensitivity in exactly the rows it was built for. CLAUDE.md's stated formula for this check is also wrong for the default measure.

**Fix.** Use an arm-size-aware implied SD for two-group rows: `implied_sd <- se / sqrt(1/n_exp + 1/n_nexp)` when both arm sizes are present, falling back to `se*sqrt(n_exp)` for within-subject rows (n_nexp NA), instead of the single `se * sqrt(n_eff)`. Verified: `2 / sqrt(1/50+1/50) = 10` = the pooled SD. Also correct the CLAUDE.md sentence, which describes only the mdw case.

**Verifier correction.** Real defect, correctly located and correctly severity-rated (minor). Three refinements to the filed version: (1) THE FIX NEEDS A THIRD BRANCH, or it introduces a new scale. The filed fix covers "both arm sizes present" and "n_nexp NA (within-subject)", but D3's current `n_eff` also falls back to `n_total` for rows that have neither arm size (e.g. rows whose n came in only as `n_sample`). Those rows must be put on the same scale with a balanced-arms `se * sqrt(n_total) / 2`, mirroring D2's own documented `2/sqrt(N)` fallback 170 lines above, or they will sit at 2x while the fixed rows sit at 1x. (2) THE mdw CASE IS OVERSTATED IN THE FINDING'S OWN FAVOUR. The finding says CLAUDE.md's `SE_MD = SD/sqrt(N)` "holds only for the within-subject mdw case". Strictly it does not hold there either: for a paired design `se * sqrt(n)` recovers the SD of the *change*, = SD_arm * sqrt(2(1-r)), which equals the arm SD only at r = 0.5. mdw pools happen to be internally consistent in practice only because single-group extractions have no `mean_sd_nexp`, so `pooled_sd` is NA for every row and the `pooled_sd`/`implied_sd` mixture never arises. The CLAUDE.md sentence should be rewritten as "SE_MD = SD * sqrt(1/n1 + 1/n2) for two-group md; the implied SD is se / sqrt(1/n1 + 1/n2)", not merely qualified as mdw-only. (3) THE PRIMARY OBSERVED DIRECTION ON REAL DATA IS MASKING, NOT FALSE-ALARMING. The finding leads with the false-positive scenario. On the repo's only real mixed-completeness md pool (41977094-SAFE-MD), the mismatch runs the other way: Rosen's genuinely tight data reads as 1.89x below the median under the current code but 3.08x under the correct scale. Both directions follow from the same mismatch and both are in the finding, but the false-positive framing is the less likely manifestation, since it requires the arm-SD rows to be the minority. Separate observation, outside this finding's scope but relevant to anyone fixing D3: CLAUDE.md's motivating case for the default-on branch does not reproduce against the current working-tree extraction. For 41065428 (mdw) the doc claims "spread 2.4 vs pool median 14.2"; running `convert_df(..., measure = "mdw")` on extraction_41065428_team.xlsx now gives Abouelgreed spread 1.57 against a pool median of 7.80 - a ratio of 4.97, just under the K = 5 gate, so D3 currently fires on nothing in the pilot at all. Whoever repairs the scale should re-derive that motivating number rather than trusting the documented one.

### E1 under exp=TRUE prints a number labelled "(log scale)" that is ~1.9x the true log-scale dispersion, and fires or not depending on which estimate the hierarchy happened to select
`R/internal_flags.R:3406` — subsystem `flags-tier2` — refuted by **0 of 3** verifiers

**Claim.** For exp-scale measures E1 approximates the log-scale dispersion as `dispersion[i] / es[i]`, using the *selected* estimate as the delta-method centre rather than the median that `dispersion` was computed around. Two consequences: (a) the printed value is not the log-scale max|ES - median| it claims to be; (b) the verdict depends on which of the row's estimates the hierarchy selected, so two identical rows can be flagged differently. This also breaks the E1 == E3 identity at k = 2 that CLAUDE.md asserts ("because diff_max = 2 * dispersion_max for every measure, E1 now coincides exactly with E3 at k = 2"), which I confirmed does hold on the additive scale.

**Evidence.** Identity holds on the additive scale (verified): ```r fe <- metaConvert:::.flag_internal_consistency; ds <- metaConvert:::.dispersion_stat for (dd in c(0.9,1.0,1.1)) { v <- c(0, dd); fe(2, ds(v), NA, max(v)-min(v), o, "d", ...) } ``` ``` diff=0.9 disp=0.45 -> E1:FALSE E3:FALSE diff=1.0 disp=0.50 -> E1:FALSE E3:FALSE diff=1.1 disp=0.55 -> E1:TRUE E3:TRUE ``` Breaks under exp=TRUE — two methods giving OR 1.2 and OR 4.0 for the same row: ```r mn<-1.2; mx<-4.0; v<-c(mn,mx) fe(2, ds(v), NA, mx-mn, o, "logor", min_es=mn, max_es=mx, exp=TRUE, es=<selected>) ``` ``` selected OR=1.2: disp_work=1.167 (thr 1) diff_work=log(4.0/1.2)=1.204 (thr 2) -> E1:TRUE E3:FALSE selected OR=4.0: disp_work=0.350 (thr 1) diff_work=log(4.0/1.2)=1.204 (thr 2) -> E1:FALSE E3:FALSE TRUE log-scale max|v-median| = 0.602 (should be compared to 1) message: [DISCORDANT] High dispersion across estimates: max|ES - median| = 1.167 (log scale) (threshold: 1) true log-scale max|v-median(v)| = 0.602 ratio printed/true = 1.938 ``` So the flag reports 1.167 where the quantity it names is 0.602, and it is a false positive (0.602 < threshold 1).

**Impact.** For OR/RR/IRR/HR rows with several estimation methods, E1 can raise a [DISCORDANT] "High dispersion" flag on rows whose true log-scale dispersion is under the threshold, with a printed number ~2x too large; whether it fires is decided by the hierarchy's selection rather than by the data. A reviewer reading the number cannot reconcile it with the min/max estimates shown in the same row.

**Fix.** Compute the log-scale dispersion exactly rather than by delta method. `dispersion_es` is `max|v - median(v)|` over the per-method estimates; store the per-method values (or the log-scale dispersion alongside) in `.generate_df()` when exp = TRUE so E1 can use `max|log(v) - median(log(v))|` directly. If the delta form must be kept, at least centre it on the median of min/max (geometric mean `sqrt(min*max)`) rather than the selected estimate, which removes the selection dependence.

**Verifier correction.** Real defect, correct severity (minor), correct file/line and correct fix — but the mechanism is under-stated in one respect and the magnitude figure is example-specific. Accurate version: under exp = TRUE (the forced default for or/rr/irr/hr), E1 approximates the log-scale dispersion as `dispersion[i] / es[i]`, where `dispersion` is the natural-scale max|ES - median| and `es[i]` is the hierarchy-SELECTED estimate rather than the median the deviation was measured around. Consequences: (a) the printed number is not the quantity its own message names ("max|ES - median| ... (log scale)"); (b) the error factor is median/es_sel and is UNBOUNDED — measured 1.47x on a realistic 2x2+means_sd row (printed 1.076 vs true 0.731, a false positive at threshold 1) and 3.9x on v = c(3, 30); the "~1.9x" in the evidence is one example, not the scale of the problem; (c) errors run in BOTH directions — a large selected estimate suppresses a genuine discordance (v = c(3, 30): true log MaxAD 1.151 > 1, not flagged when es_sel = 30); (d) the strongest framing is not "two identical rows can be flagged differently" (contrived) but that the statistic is NOT INVARIANT UNDER REVERSAL OF THE EXPOSURE/REFERENCE CODING — the very invariance the log scale provides. Demonstrated: the same study with arms relabelled goes from "[DISCORDANT] High dispersion ... = 1.076 (log scale)" to no E1 flag at all. It also breaks the E1 == E3 identity at k = 2 that CLAUDE.md asserts, since the identity would require es_sel to be the logarithmic mean (max-min)/log(max/min). The primary fix (store/compute .dispersion_stat(log(v)) in .generate_df() when exp && measure is a log measure) is exact and the values are in scope there; the fallback fix (geometric-mean centre sqrt(min*max)) was verified to restore reversal-invariance and to land at 0.747 vs the true 0.731. Scope note, separate finding: E2/E2b's CI overlap is likewise computed on the natural scale under exp = TRUE and changed (22.9% -> 17.6%) under the same relabelling.

### min/max cross-method diagnostics are silently blanked whenever the tied routes have NA CI bounds - systematic for every NNT row whose risk-difference CI crosses zero
`R/internal_generate_df.R:423` — subsystem `pipeline-wiring` — refuted by **0 of 3** verifiers

**Claim.** In `.generate_df()`, `col_min`/`col_max` are first taken as `which.min`/`which.max` of the per-route effect sizes. Lines 423-430 then treat `col_min == col_max` as a tie and re-derive BOTH indices from `df_ci_lo_min_max`. `col_min == col_max` is also true in the ordinary case where several routes return the SAME value (e.g. `2x2` and `2x2_sum` on one contingency table). When those routes have NA confidence bounds, `which.min(all-NA)` returns `integer(0)`, `[1]` yields NA, and every min_*/max_* column plus `diff_min_max` (and hence the Category-E cross-method dispersion / direction checks) becomes NA even though `n_estimations = 2`. This is exactly the state metaConvert deliberately creates for NNT: per the Altman (1998) rule implemented in the NNT routes, the NNT CI bounds are set to NA whenever the risk-difference CI crosses zero. Every non-significant NNT row therefore loses its cross-method diagnostics.

**Evidence.**  suppressMessages(devtools::load_all('.', quiet=TRUE)) mk <- function(a,b,c,d) data.frame(study_id="s", n_cases_exp=a, n_controls_exp=b, n_cases_nexp=c, n_controls_nexp=d, n_exp=a+b, n_nexp=c+d) # RD CI crosses zero -> NNT CI is disjoint -> bounds NA summary(convert_df(mk(30,70,20,80), measure="nnt", verbose=FALSE), digits=10, flags=FALSE, guidance=FALSE) # RD CI clear of zero -> NNT CI present summary(convert_df(mk(60,40,20,80), measure="nnt", verbose=FALSE), digits=10, flags=FALSE, guidance=FALSE) ACTUAL OUTPUT: CI crosses zero n_est=2 all=2x2 + 2x2_sum es=-10 | min_info=NA min_es=NA | max_info=NA max_es=NA | diff=NA route 2x2 nnt= -10 ci= NA NA route 2x2_sum nnt= -10 ci= NA NA CI clear of zero n_est=2 all=2x2 + 2x2_sum es=-2.5 | min_info=2x2 min_es=-2.5 | max_info=2x2 max_es=-2.5 | diff=0 route 2x2 nnt= -2.5 ci= -3.62265 -1.908546 route 2x2_sum nnt= -2.5 ci= -3.62265 -1.908546 Both rows have two usable NNT estimates. Only the row with non-NA CI bounds gets min_info / min_es_value / max_es_value / diff_min_max; the first row reports NA for all of them, so E1/E3/E5 have nothing to test. (This block is unchanged from master - `git diff master -- R/internal_generate_df.R` shows only the omega additions - so it is pre-existing, not a regression of this release.)

**Impact.** For every NNT comparison whose risk difference is not significant (i.e. most of them in a typical review), the summary() diagnostic columns min_info/min_es_value/min_es_se/min_es_ci_*/max_* and diff_min_max come back NA, and the Category-E cross-method checks (large min-max range, direction disagreement, dispersion) are silently inoperative for those rows - a reviewer relying on metaDETECT sees a clean row that was never actually checked. No effect size is wrong; the loss is diagnostic coverage. The same failure mode reaches any measure where two tied routes both return NA CI bounds.

**Fix.** Guard the tie-break so it (a) only fires when more than one column actually carries a usable estimate, and (b) falls back to the original indices when the CI-based re-derivation yields NA. E.g. at R/internal_generate_df.R:423 keep `old_min <- col_min; old_max <- col_max`, restrict `cols_equal` to rows with `rowSums(!is.na(df_value_min_max)) > 1`, and after the two `apply()` calls do `col_min[is.na(col_min)] <- old_min[is.na(col_min)]` (same for col_max). Restricting the `which.min`/`which.max` over `df_ci_lo_min_max` to columns whose `df_value_min_max` entry is non-NA would also close the related hazard of naming a route whose point estimate was nulled.

**Verifier correction.** `.generate_df()` (R/internal_generate_df.R:423-430) treats `col_min == col_max` as a tie and re-derives both indices from `df_ci_lo_min_max`; when the tied routes have NA CI bounds, `which.min(all-NA)[1]` yields NA and the min_*/max_* diagnostic columns plus `diff_min_max` are blanked. In practice this is confined to NNT rows whose risk-difference CI crosses zero (the Altman 1998 disjoint-NNT rule), where a 2x2 table yields the always-tied `2x2 + 2x2_sum` pair. The consequence is loss of DISPLAY completeness, not loss of checking power. `col_min == col_max` holds iff all non-NA estimates are numerically identical, so on exactly these rows min_es == max_es == es and diff_min_max == 0 by construction: E3 (min-max range) and E5 (direction disagreement) are mathematically incapable of firing, and E5 does not apply to NNT at all (nnt is absent from `signed_measures`). E1 is unaffected — `dispersion_es` is computed on an independent long-format path that explicitly keeps NNT rows with NA CIs, and returns 0 for the reproducer. What is actually lost: `min_info`/`max_info`/`min_es_value`/`max_es_value`/`diff_min_max` read NA instead of their well-defined values, the `es_consistency` string degrades to "min: NA = NA, max: NA = NA, ...", and `.minmax_suffix()` cannot append the route names to whatever flag does fire. The filed impact ("Category-E dispersion/range/direction checks silently inoperative; reviewer sees a clean row that was never checked") is incorrect. These rows are not clean: `overlap_min_max` falls back to 0 when the CI bounds are NA, so every disjoint-CI NNT row already emits `[DISCORDANT] Zero CI overlap between min and max estimates` — a false positive on a row where the two routes agree exactly. That spurious-flag behaviour is a separate pre-existing defect (independent of the tie-break, since the CI is NA whichever column is selected) and is the more consequential of the two. The proposed fix (restrict `cols_equal` to rows with >1 usable estimate; fall back to the pre-tie-break indices when the CI-based re-derivation yields NA) is correct and introduces no error.

### es_formulas() re-runs convert_df() without alpha_se_source / omega_se_source / icc_agreement_se, and treats omega as having no conversion formula
`R/main_convert_df.R:1840` — subsystem `pipeline-wiring` — refuted by **0 of 3** verifiers

**Claim.** `attr(res, "conversion_args")` (R/main_convert_df.R:1840-1849) records the conversion choices so `es_formulas()` can re-run the analysis, but it omits the three SE-source switches this release added: `alpha_se_source`, `omega_se_source`, `icc_agreement_se`. `.run_with_parameter()` builds its call as `cargs[intersect(names(cargs), names(formals(convert_df)))]` (R/es_formulas.R:143), so those three silently revert to their defaults in every re-run, and es_formulas() reports estimates the user's own settings never produce. Separately, `.formula_parameters()` (R/es_formulas.R:36-51) and `.parameters_for_measure()` (:66-86) have no `omega_to_es` entry and no `"omega"` case, so `es_formulas()` on an omega object announces that no formula can influence the measure - which is false, `omega_to_es` changes the analysis scale exactly as `alpha_to_es` does (verified: bonett -1.89712 / hakstian_whalen 0.46867 / raw 0.85 on the same input).

**Evidence.**  suppressMessages(devtools::load_all('.', quiet=TRUE)) da <- data.frame(study_id=paste0("s",1:2), cronbach_alpha=c(.85,.9), n_sample=c(200,150), n_items=c(10,12)) o <- convert_df(da, measure="alpha", alpha_se_source="reported", verbose=FALSE) summary(o, flags=FALSE, guidance=FALSE)$es_crude # -> NA NA (correct: no SE source) es_formulas(o, verbose=FALSE) ACTUAL OUTPUT: original summary es: NA,NA row_id parameter formula es 1 1 alpha_to_es bonett -1.897 2 1 alpha_to_es hakstian_whalen 0.469 3 1 alpha_to_es raw 0.850 4 2 alpha_to_es bonett -2.303 5 2 alpha_to_es hakstian_whalen 0.536 6 2 alpha_to_es raw 0.900 The object the user built yields es = NA for both studies; es_formulas() on that very object returns six non-NA estimates, because the re-run used the default alpha_se_source = "closed_form". do <- data.frame(study_id="s", omega=.85, omega_se=.03, n_sample=200, n_items=10) es_formulas(convert_df(do, measure="omega", verbose=FALSE)) ACTUAL OUTPUT: No conversion formula can influence the 'omega' measure, so no alternative was evaluated. omega es_formulas nrow: 0 yet omega_to_es demonstrably moves the estimate (same data, omega_se_source='closed_form'): bonett es= -1.89712 se= 0.10594 raw es= 0.85 se= 0.01589 hakstian_whalen es= 0.46867 se= 0.01874

**Impact.** A user running the release's new sensitivity tool on a reliability pool is shown effect sizes their own convert_df() call does not produce (rows they deliberately left unpooled by choosing alpha_se_source = "reported" or icc_agreement_se = "drop" come back populated), and is told - incorrectly - that the omega scale choice has no influence, which is the single most consequential choice in an omega reliability generalisation. Nothing is miscomputed inside a route; the sensitivity table just does not describe the user's analysis.

**Fix.** Add `alpha_se_source = alpha_se_source, omega_se_source = omega_se_source, icc_agreement_se = icc_agreement_se` to the `conversion_args` list at R/main_convert_df.R:1840, and add `omega_to_es = c("bonett", "raw", "hakstian_whalen")` to `.formula_parameters()` plus an `else if (measure == "omega") k <- "omega_to_es"` branch in `.parameters_for_measure()` (and list omega_to_es in `.scale_changing_parameters()` alongside alpha_to_es/icc_to_es, since it changes the analysis scale rather than the formula).

**Verifier correction.** Claim stands as filed, with two refinements. (a) The ICC half, asserted without a demonstration, does reproduce: `convert_df(measure=\"icc\", icc_agreement_se=\"drop\")` yields es_crude = NA NA, while `es_formulas()` on that object returns four populated rows (bonett -1.609 se 0.181, raw 0.800 se 0.036; second row -1.204/0.191 and 0.700/0.057). (b) The two halves of the fix must land together, not separately: adding `omega_to_es` to `.formula_parameters()`/`.parameters_for_measure()` without also adding `omega_se_source` to `conversion_args` would give a user who set `omega_se_source = \"closed_form\"` on SE-less studies an omega sensitivity table that collapses to all-NA. `flag_options` is the fourth argument missing from `conversion_args` but is correctly excluded, since `.run_with_parameter()` re-runs with `flags = FALSE`. Fix otherwise verified safe: `conversion_args` has a single consumer (R/es_formulas.R:308), none of the three switches is a per-row column so `.resolve_formula()` is unaffected, and placing `omega_to_es` in `.scale_changing_parameters()` is statistically required because ln(1-w), w and 1-(1-w)^(1/3) are three different scales for which a numeric `deviation`/`spread` would be meaningless.

### convert_df(): per-row method columns (or_to_cor, smd_to_cor, cor_to_smd, or_to_rr, rr_to_or) are silently ignored on the user-input route — every native route honours them
`R/main_convert_df.R:1386` — subsystem `user-guards-ancova` — refuted by **0 of 3** verifiers

**Claim.** convert_df() explicitly supports specifying these method arguments per row as dataset columns (the loop at R/main_convert_df.R:441-463 merges column and argument, and the error message it raises says "should be 1, or should be specified in a column of the dataset"). Every native route is called via `with(x, es_from_*(..., or_to_cor = or_to_cor))`, which picks up the merged column. The two user-input calls instead pass the *scalar function arguments* (`or_to_rr = or_to_rr, or_to_cor = or_to_cor, smd_to_cor = smd_to_cor, cor_to_smd = cor_to_smd, rr_to_or = rr_to_or`), so every row silently gets the dataset-level default rather than its own requested conversion. Pre-existing (identical on master), not a regression, but it produces a different number from the one the user asked for.

**Evidence.** # per-row smd_to_cor column, target measure = r USER route (user_es_original_measure_crude = 'd', user_es = 0.5, se = 0.2, n = 50/50): study_id es_crude se_crude info_used_crude 1 a 0.3068753 0.1147461 user_input_crude (smd_to_cor = 'viechtbauer') 2 b 0.3068753 0.1147461 user_input_crude (smd_to_cor = 'lipsey_cooper' REQUESTED, IGNORED) NATIVE cohen_d route, same column, same data: 1 a 0.3068753 0.11652518 cohen_d 2 b 0.2425356 0.09272323 cohen_d <-- honours the column Ground truth (direct calls): es_from_cohen_d(0.5, 50, 50, smd_to_cor='viechtbauer')$r = 0.3068753 es_from_cohen_d(0.5, 50, 50, smd_to_cor='lipsey_cooper')$r = 0.2425356 => row b is 26.5% too large on the user route. # per-row or_to_cor column, target measure = r, OR = 2.5, logor_se = 0.2 USER route: a 0.3366893 ; b 0.3366893 (both = 'bonett', the convert_df default) NATIVE route: a 0.3463355 ('pearson') ; b 0.3306956 ('digby')

**Impact.** A review that sets a per-row conversion method (e.g. mixing 'pearson' and 'digby' OR-to-r conversions, or 'lipsey_cooper' for point-biserial rows) gets the dataset default on every user-input row, with no message. Here that is a 26% error in the reported r for the smd_to_cor case. Silent because info_used still reads 'user_input_crude' and no flag inspects which conversion was used.

**Fix.** In R/main_convert_df.R, pass the merged per-row columns to both user routes: `or_to_rr = x$or_to_rr, or_to_cor = x$or_to_cor, smd_to_cor = x$smd_to_cor, cor_to_smd = x$cor_to_smd, rr_to_or = x$rr_to_or`, and make `.dispatch_user_conversion()` subset them by `idx` alongside the numeric arguments (the 'd'/'r'/'z' branches already `rep()`/`length.out` them, so they only need the subset vector rather than a scalar). Alternatively, if per-row methods are deliberately unsupported here, emit the same kind of one-off message convert_df already uses for smd_denom.

**Verifier correction.** Real defect, correct file/line, correct severity (minor); two corrections. MECHANISM (as filed, confirmed): R/main_convert_df.R:441-463 merges the per-row method columns (rr_to_or, or_to_rr, or_to_cor, smd_to_cor, smd_var, smd_denom, pre_post_to_smd, cor_to_smd, unit_type) into `x`, and the loop's own stop() text documents the feature. Every native route reads them via `with(x, ...)`. The two user-input calls at R/main_convert_df.R:1376-1409 sit outside `with(x, ...)` and pass the unmerged scalar arguments, so user_input_crude/user_input_adj rows silently get the dataset default. Reproduced: per-row smd_to_cor = c("viechtbauer","lipsey_cooper") gives 0.3068753 on both user rows where the native cohen_d route gives 0.3068753 / 0.2425356. CORRECTION 1 — framing/impact. This is not "a 26% error in r"; it is the silent substitution of the biserial correlation for the point-biserial one the user requested (CLAUDE.md's own E8 note calls viechtbauer-vs-lipsey_cooper "a documented choice about which correlation to estimate"). The 26.5% figure is the correct magnitude of that substitution and is honestly stated, but the delivered number is internally consistent, not arithmetically wrong — which is why nothing downstream catches it. CORRECTION 2 — the proposed fix is wrong as written and would introduce a worse defect. Passing `x$smd_to_cor` / `x$cor_to_smd` etc. and subsetting by `idx` is right, but the parenthetical "the 'd'/'r'/'z' branches already rep()/length.out them, so they only need the subset vector" is false: - R/es_from_USER.R "r" and "z" branches call `.cor_to_smd_vec(..., cor_to_smd = rep(cor_to_smd, length.out = length(nn)))` where `nn <- which(!is.na(r) & !is.na(r_se) & !is.na(n_s))`. With a per-row vector this takes elements 1..length(nn) instead of elements `nn`, so any row dropped for a missing SE or N shifts every later method assignment onto the wrong study — a cross-row misalignment strictly worse than today's uniform default. Must become `cor_to_smd[nn]`. - The "d" and "g" branches use `rep(smd_to_cor, len)` with no `length.out`; I verified this still returns correct per-row values (only by an indexing coincidence — the first `len` entries of `rep(v, len)` equal `v`, and `.es_from_d` indexes `dat_r` by `nn_miss` in 1:len). Change to `rep(smd_to_cor, length.out = len)` so it is correct by construction. - The logor/or (es_from_or_se), logrr/rr (es_from_rr_se) and md (es_from_md_se) branches already accept vector method arguments — the native routes feed them columns — so they need only the `[idx]` subset. The alternative remedy the finding offers (a one-off message, on the model of the smd_denom note) remains valid and is lower-risk.

### unit_type: the documented default "raw_scale" is overwritten with NA by missing(), so cor_to_smd = "mathur" returns NA for d/g/OR/R/Z whenever the caller relies on the default — and this release's rewritten @param text now asserts that default explicitly
`R/es_from_stand_COR.R:110` — subsystem `user-guards-ancova` — refuted by **0 of 3** verifiers

**Claim.** Seven exported routes (`es_from_pearson_r`, `es_from_fisher_z`, `es_from_spearman_rho`, `es_from_linreg_t`, `es_from_linreg_b_se`, `es_from_linreg_b_ci`, `es_from_linreg_b_pval`) declare `unit_type = "raw_scale"` in the signature and then immediately do `if (missing(unit_type)) unit_type <- rep(NA, length(...))`. `.cor_to_smd_vec()` reads it as `ifelse(unit_type == "sd", ...)`, and `ifelse(NA, a, b)` is NA — so the whole mathur conversion collapses to NA rather than to the documented raw-units behaviour. The arithmetic bug is pre-existing, but this release rewrote every one of those @param blocks to state "Defaults to '\"raw_scale\"'", so the shipped reference manual now makes a claim the code does not honour (a CRAN-visible documentation/code mismatch).

**Evidence.** $ es_from_pearson_r(pearson_r=0.5, n_sample=100, cor_to_smd='mathur', unit_increase_iv=1, sd_iv=2) no unit_type (doc default raw_scale): d= NA $ ... unit_type='raw_scale' : d= 0.2886751346 $ ... unit_type='sd' : d= 0.5773502692 Code path: R/es_from_stand_COR.R:110 if (missing(unit_type)) { unit_type <- rep(NA, length(pearson_r)) } R/internal_multiple_formulas.R:1929 increase <- ifelse(unit_type[i] == "sd", unit_increase_iv[i]*sd_iv[i], unit_increase_iv[i]) -> ifelse(NA, ., .) == NA convert_df() is unaffected: its merge loop fills the NA cells of the unit_type column with the argument default before the routes are called (R/main_convert_df.R:441-463).

**Impact.** Direct callers of the seven routes who use cor_to_smd = 'mathur' with unit_increase_iv/sd_iv and rely on the documented default get NA effect sizes rather than the raw-units result, with no warning. metaumbrella and any user script following the new @param text is exposed. No wrong number is produced — the failure is a silent NA plus a false statement in the reference manual.

**Fix.** Replace `if (missing(unit_type)) unit_type <- rep(NA, n)` with `unit_type <- rep(unit_type, length.out = n)` (or `if (missing(unit_type)) unit_type <- rep("raw_scale", n)`) in the seven routes, so the declared default actually reaches `.cor_to_smd_vec()`; alternatively harden `.cor_to_smd_vec()` to treat NA as raw units. Either way add an assertion that `es_from_pearson_r(..., cor_to_smd='mathur')` without unit_type equals the same call with unit_type='raw_scale'.

**Verifier correction.** Seven exported routes (es_from_pearson_r, es_from_fisher_z, es_from_spearman_rho, es_from_linreg_t, es_from_linreg_b_se, es_from_linreg_b_ci, es_from_linreg_b_pval) declare `unit_type = "raw_scale"` in the signature and then overwrite it with `if (missing(unit_type)) unit_type <- rep(NA, n)`. The consequence is NOT `ifelse(NA, ., .)` in .cor_to_smd_vec(): the mathur branch of the `nn_miss` filter in es_from_pearson_r() (R/es_from_stand_COR.R:142-147) and es_from_linreg_t() (R/es_from_REGRESSION.R:291-296) requires `!is.na(unit_type)`, so the row is excluded before .cor_to_smd_vec() is called. With cor_to_smd = "mathur" and unit_type omitted, d/g/logOR (and their SEs/CIs) are silently NA; r/z are unaffected (verified: r = 0.5, z = 0.5493 identical across arms, while d = NA vs 0.2887 for "raw_scale" and 0.5774 for "sd" at r = .5, n = 100, sd_iv = 2, unit_increase_iv = 1). The declared default is asserted in three places the code does not honour: the Rd Usage block, the @param text rewritten this release in 23f5c17 ("Defaults to '\"raw_scale\"'"; v2.0.1 said only 'Must be either "sd" or "raw_scale"'), and the new design note at R/internal_guards.R:96. convert_df() is affected too, on one path the finding missed. Its default path is fine (the merge loop at R/main_convert_df.R:441-463 fills NA cells with the argument default), but `.validate_unit_type(column = TRUE)` (R/internal_guards.R:136-141) neutralises an unrecognised cell such as "SD" to NA while warning "Those rows are treated as raw units" — and the same gate then drops the row. A 3-row convert_df(cor_to_smd = "mathur", unit_type = c("raw_scale","SD",NA)) reports "Crude estimates: 2/3", so the warning states the opposite of what happens; the design note at internal_guards.R:113-116 repeats the same false assertion. Fix: in the seven routes replace the missing() branch with `unit_type <- rep(unit_type, length.out = n)` (or `rep("raw_scale", n)` when missing). Do NOT rely on "harden .cor_to_smd_vec() to treat NA as raw units" alone — that line is unreachable with NA from these routes; that route would additionally require dropping `!is.na(unit_type)` from both nn_miss filters. Also decide the intended NA semantics once and align the three assertions with it: either NA means raw units (then the column-neutralisation warning becomes true and the neutralised row is estimated), or NA means "not supplied, drop the row" (then the formals default, the @param default sentence, and the neutralisation warning must all be corrected). Add an assertion that es_from_pearson_r(..., cor_to_smd = "mathur") without unit_type equals the same call with unit_type = "raw_scale"; the existing test-cor-to-smd-mathur.R:65 block does not cover this because its "default" arm passes unit_type = "value" explicitly, and test-method-defaults-consistency.R:330 checks only formals(). Statistics are sound throughout: the Mathur & VanderWeele (2020) conversion is implemented correctly and no wrong number is produced. Severity: minor.

### User-input within-subject measures: entering 'dw'/'gw'/'mdw' silently drops the row from a g/d pool, while the numerically identical 'd'/'g'/'md' entry converts fine — the mirroring documented in CLAUDE.md is one-directional
`R/es_from_USER.R:351` — subsystem `user-guards-ancova` — refuted by **0 of 3** verifiers

**Claim.** CLAUDE.md states that for the within-group SMD/MD measures the dispatcher "treats the within-group point estimate as numerically identical to its between-group counterpart" and mirrors results both ways. In fact the `"dw"`, `"gw"` and `"mdw"` branches of `.dispatch_user_conversion()` only build a bare data.frame carrying the entered value into its own slot and its direct counterpart (dw->d, gw->g, mdw->md); they never call `.es_from_d()`, so no cross-family conversion (d<->g, or the OR/R/Z family) is produced. The result is an asymmetry: a `d` entry populates g/gw, but a `dw` entry populates neither g nor gw; a `g` entry populates d/dw, but a `gw` entry populates neither d nor dw.

**Evidence.** convert_df() over six rows differing only in user_es_original_measure_crude (all user_es = 0.5, se = 0.2, n = 50/50): ### measure = g (and identically measure = gw) study_id orig es_crude se_crude 1 s1 d 0.49616204 0.19846482 2 s2 g 0.50000000 0.20000000 3 s3 md 0.49616204 0.20154197 41 s4 dw NA NA <-- dropped 4 s5 gw 0.50000000 0.20000000 6 s6 mdw NA NA <-- dropped ### measure = d (and identically measure = dw) 1 s1 d 0.50000000 0.20000000 2 s2 g 0.50386764 0.20154706 3 s3 md 0.50000000 0.20310096 4 s4 dw 0.50000000 0.20000000 5 s5 gw NA NA <-- dropped 6 s6 mdw NA NA <-- dropped (Compare row s2 'g' -> d = 0.50386764 with row s5 'gw' -> d = NA: identical inputs, one converts, one does not.)

**Impact.** Studies entered as a within-subject d/g are silently excluded from a between-group (or the opposite within-group) pool, so k shrinks without any wrong number being visible — the row appears only in the 'Missing ES in input rows' line of summary(). Selective loss of within-subject studies biases a pooled estimate by omission. Low frequency (needs a user_input row whose entered measure is dw/gw and a target in the other family), hence minor.

**Fix.** Route the 'dw' and 'gw' branches of `.dispatch_user_conversion()` through the same code as 'd' and 'g' (for 'gw', un-correct by J first, exactly as the 'g' branch does), then mirror the resulting d/g/dw/gw slots, so the mapping is symmetric. If the one-directional behaviour is deliberate, correct the CLAUDE.md paragraph and add an @note to ?es_from_user_crude naming the combinations that return NA.

**Verifier correction.** R/es_from_USER.R:340-374 — the `mdw`, `dw` and `gw` branches of `.dispatch_user_conversion()` build a bare data.frame carrying the entered value into its own slot and its direct counterpart (dw->d, gw->g, mdw->md) and never call `.es_from_d()`/`es_from_md_se()`. So no d<->g Hedges step, no md->SMD step, and no SMD->r/z/OR step is produced for these entered types, whereas the `d`/`g`/`md` branches produce all of them. Because measure="dw" extracts the `d` column and measure="gw" extracts the `g` column (R/internal_generate_df.R:216-232), the loss is NOT a within-vs-between-pool issue: the affected combinations are entered `dw` with any g-family target (measure="g" or "gw"), entered `gw` with any d-family target ("d" or "dw"), and entered `mdw` with any SMD/correlation/ratio target. The most plausible real case is a within-group review (measure="gw") dropping a study entered as `dw` — same family. Rows land as NA with info_used = NA; they are reported in summary()'s missing-ES line and by guidance=TRUE, and the user can work around it by typing the value as "d"/"g"/"md", so the impact is a small, recoverable k loss, not a wrong number. The broken contract is `?es_from_user_crude` / `?es_from_user_adj` @details ("when the user_es_original_measure_crude is one of the known measures (d, g, md, mdw, dw, gw, or, ...), conversions towards the other effect size measures are performed"), not the CLAUDE.md paragraph — CLAUDE.md and the archived tests (tests_save/checked/test-ES-USER-CONVERSION.R:955-1013) both describe exactly the one-directional {md,d,g}->{mdw,dw,gw} plus within->direct-counterpart behaviour the code implements. The fix proposed in the finding must NOT be applied as written: routing dw/gw through `.es_from_d()` would apply the pooled-df correction J(n_exp+n_nexp-2) where every native pre/post kernel uses J(n-1) (R/internal_multiple_formulas.R:1757/1790/1817) — J(98)=0.99232 vs J(49)=0.98460 at n=50, a ~0.8% bias plus two mixed corrections inside one gw pool — and would emit r/z/logOR from a paired SMD, for which the point-biserial and Cox maps (both assuming two independent groups of size n1, n2) are undefined. The correct remedy is (i) fix the @details sentence and add an @note naming the entered-measure/target combinations that return NA, and, optionally, (ii) add only the narrow d<->g step for the within-group entered types, using the within-subject df J(n-1) with n identified from the row, and continue to refuse the r/z/OR family.

### metaConvert-package.Rd tells users see_input_data() covers all 22 measures; it now rejects 6 of them by name
`man/metaConvert-package.Rd:16` — subsystem `docs-vs-code` — refuted by **0 of 3** verifiers

**Claim.** The package landing page says: "To visualize all the types of input data that can be used to estimate the 22 effect size measures available in metaConvert, you can use the see_input_data() function." (line 16-17, updated from 21 to 22 in this release's diff) and repeats the pointer at line 53. But see_input_data() was given a by-name guard this release (R/data_extraction.R:864-872) whose supported set is only c("all","d","g","md","or","rr","nnt","r","z","logvr","logcvr","irr") — it errors for "rd", "prop", "alpha", "omega", "icc" and "hr", i.e. 6 of the 22 the sentence names, including the three psychometric measures the Psychometrics vignette is built on. The guard's own error message is itself already stale: it enumerates "rd", "alpha", "icc", "prop", "hr" and omits "omega", which is new in this release and also unsupported.

**Evidence.** $ Rscript -e 'devtools::load_all("."); for (m in c("alpha","icc","omega","prop")) {s <- try(see_input_data(measure=m), silent=TRUE); cat(m, ":", if (inherits(s,"try-error")) "ERROR" else "ok", "\n")}; print(formals(see_input_data)$measure)' alpha : ERROR icc : ERROR omega : ERROR prop : ERROR Full message for measure = "omega": Error : see_input_data(): unsupported 'measure' value "omega". Supported values are "all", "d", "g", "md", "or", "rr", "nnt", "r", "z", "logvr", "logcvr", "irr". The risk difference and the psychometric measures ("rd", "alpha", "icc", "prop", "hr") are not covered by this table; use data_extraction_sheet() for those. ^ "omega" missing from this list formals(see_input_data)$measure: c("all","d","g","md","or","rr","nnt","r","z","logvr","logcvr","irr") By contrast see_input_data.Rd's own \item{measure} (lines 18-21) IS accurate — it names the exclusions. Only metaConvert-package.Rd contradicts the code.
