**Evidence.** $ es_from_user_crude(user_es_original_measure_crude='or', user_es_crude=0.20, user_se_crude=0.30, user_es_target_measure_crude='or', n_exp=50, n_nexp=50) Warning: "For rows with user_es_original_measure_crude = 'or'/'rr', user_se_crude must be on the LOG scale (SE of logOR/logRR), not the natural scale. Detected SE > ES in 1 row(s), which suggests the SE may be on the natural scale. If so, provide CI bounds instead ..." The entry is correct: logor = -1.609438 ; expected log(0.2) = -1.609438 (OR = 0.20 with SE(logOR) = 0.30 is an ordinary protective estimate, 95% CI [0.111, 0.360].)

**Impact.** No wrong number is produced, but the warning tells the user their correct entry is probably wrong and instructs them to replace it. Acting on it (re-entering a natural-scale SE, or substituting CI bounds that were never reported) is how the advice turns into a wrong number. It fires on essentially every protective OR/RR with a moderately imprecise estimate, so it will be tuned out — which also destroys its value for the case it was written for.

**Fix.** Restrict the check to the direction where it discriminates and use the log scale: flag only when `abs(log(user_es_crude))` is small relative to `user_se_crude` in a way a natural-scale SE would produce — e.g. `user_es_crude > 1 & user_se_crude > log(user_es_crude) * k`, or better, test whether the supplied SE reproduces the supplied CI on the log scale when a CI is present, and stay silent otherwise. Apply the same change at R/es_from_USER.R:864 (adjusted route).

**Verifier correction.** Real defect, severity `minor` (code-behaviour, not documentation): the OR/RR scale-sanity warning at R/es_from_USER.R:573-586 (and the identical adjusted copy at :864-876) tests `user_se_crude > user_es_crude`, comparing a log-scale SE against a natural-scale ratio. Under the hypothesis it is testing (SE mistakenly on the natural scale) the implied log SE is s/OR, so for OR < 1 the error hypothesis is always *less* plausible than the correct one and a firing is evidence against the error, not for it. Measured over 300k simulated 2x2 trials with entirely correct log-scale SEs: 55.2% of protective rows (97.9% at OR < 0.2, 73.0% at 0.2-0.4) are falsely warned, versus 0.00% of OR > 1 rows; against the mis-entry it targets it fires only 21.0% (OR < 1) and 7.7% (OR > 1) of the time, so on the protective side the likelihood ratio of a firing is 0.38. No wrong number is produced — the returned logOR/SE/CI are correct — and following the warning's own advice (supply the reported CI) also yields the right answer via the `ratio_from_ci` block, so the harm is alarm fatigue on a warning that names only a row count, plus a user being told a correct entry is probably wrong. FIX: add `user_es_crude > 1` (resp. `user_es_adj > 1`) to the existing predicate — behaviour is unchanged in the only region where the statistic discriminates (0.00% FP there), the check goes silent where it is anti-informative, and both archived pins in tests_save/checked/test-ES-USER-CONVERSION.R:935-953 still pass. Do NOT adopt the filed fix's `user_se_crude > log(user_es_crude) * k` variant: at k = 1 it measures 19.0% FP / 62.7% power on OR > 1, i.e. it introduces a 19% false-positive rate on valid harmful-direction estimates where the current rule has none. A CI-consistency cross-check (does the supplied SE reproduce the supplied CI on the log scale?) is a sound additional check for rows carrying both. Separately, `irr`/`hr` are log-transformed at :562-571 before the predicate runs and so get no scale check at all.

### README.md (shipped to CRAN) says the package estimates 14 effect size measures; DESCRIPTION and the reference manual say 22
`README.md:25` — subsystem `docs-vs-code` — refuted by **0 of 3** verifiers

**Claim.** The true count, taken from the code, is 22: convert_df()'s measure formal (R/main_convert_df.R:327-330) lists exactly 22 values, and man/metaConvert-package.Rd's itemised list (updated 21 -> 22 this release) has 22 bullets that match it one-for-one. DESCRIPTION:30 says 22. But README.md:25 still says 14, and README.Rmd:30 (its source) says 14. README.md is NOT in .Rbuildignore — only ^README\.Rmd$ is — so README.md ships in the tarball and is rendered on the CRAN package page directly above the DESCRIPTION that says 22. CLAUDE.md:9 also says 14 (not shipped; ^CLAUDE\.md$ is ignored). The "11" in vignettes/Tutorial.Rmd:181 is the title of the published RSM companion paper and is correct as a citation.

**Evidence.** $ Rscript -e 'devtools::load_all("."); m <- eval(formals(convert_df)$measure); cat(length(m), ":", paste(m, collapse=", "), "\n")' 22 : d, g, md, dw, gw, mdw, logor, logrr, logirr, loghr, nnt, rd, r, z, rp, zp, logvr, logcvr, prop, alpha, omega, icc $ grep -c '\\item \\bold' man/metaConvert-package.Rd # the 'Effect size measures available' list 22 $ grep -n '14 effect size' README.md README.Rmd README.md:25:This package automatically estimates 14 effect size measures from a README.Rmd:30:This package automatically estimates 14 effect size measures from a well-formatted dataset. ... $ grep -n '22 effect size' DESCRIPTION man/metaConvert-package.Rd DESCRIPTION:30:Description: Automatically estimate 22 effect size measures from ... man/metaConvert-package.Rd:9:The \pkg{metaConvert} package automatically estimates 22 effect size measures ... man/metaConvert-package.Rd:16:To visualize all the types of input data that can be used to estimate the 22 effect size measures ... $ grep -n 'README' .Rbuildignore ^README\.Rmd$ # README.md is not ignored -> it ships

**Impact.** No numeric consequence, but it is the single most visible sentence about the package (CRAN page, GitHub landing) and it understates the package's scope by 8 measures — including every measure added since 2.0.0 (prop, alpha, omega, icc, rd, hr, dw/gw/mdw). It also directly contradicts the DESCRIPTION rendered on the same CRAN page, which reads as a maintenance signal to reviewers.

**Fix.** Change '14' to '22' in README.Rmd:30, re-knit to README.md (or edit README.md:25 directly if the Rmd is not re-rendered on release). Update CLAUDE.md:9 for internal consistency. Verify no other '14 effect size' occurrences remain: `grep -rn '14 effect size\|14 different' README.md README.Rmd CLAUDE.md DESCRIPTION man/ vignettes/`.

**Verifier correction.** README.md:25 (and its source README.Rmd:30) still says the package "automatically estimates 14 effect size measures", while the authoritative `convert_df()` `measure` formal (R/main_convert_df.R:327-330), DESCRIPTION:30, R/metaConvert.R:3,10 and man/metaConvert-package.Rd:9,16,61 all say 22. The 14 was correct at v2.0.0 (whose `measure` formal had exactly 14 values) and was never carried forward. README.md ships: `.Rbuildignore` excludes only `^README\.Rmd$`, and the repo's own built source tree at metaConvert.Rcheck/00_pkg_src/metaConvert/ contains README.md but not README.Rmd — so CRAN renders the "14" sentence directly above the DESCRIPTION that says 22. Severity: doc (no numeric consequence). CORRECTION to the filed impact: the 8 measures missing from the stale count are dw, gw, mdw, loghr, prop, alpha, omega and icc — NOT `rd`, which was already among the 14 at 2.0.0. Fix: edit README.Rmd:30 to "22", re-knit to README.md (README.md carries the "generated from README.Rmd" banner, so do not patch it in isolation), and update CLAUDE.md:9 for internal consistency (CLAUDE.md is Rbuildignored and does not ship). Do not hand-edit metaConvert.Rcheck/00_pkg_src/metaConvert/README.md — it is a regenerated build artefact.

### The \details formula block of the pre/post SMD converters is unconditionally wrong under pool_sd = TRUE (a supported, documented setting)
`man/es_from_means_sd_pre_post.Rd:92` — subsystem `docs-vs-code` — refuted by **0 of 3** verifiers

**Claim.** man/es_from_means_sd_pre_post.Rd:92-124 presents "Four approaches can be used to compute the Cohen's d", gives a \deqn per arm for each (e.g. bonett: cohen_d_exp = (mean_exp - mean_pre_exp)/mean_pre_sd_exp), and closes with cohen_d = d_exp - d_nexp and cohen_d_se = sqrt(se_exp^2 + se_nexp^2). Every one of those eight \deqn expressions holds only for pool_sd = FALSE. Under pool_sd = TRUE the standardizer is a single SD pooled across arms (Morris's d_ppc2), so neither the per-arm d's nor the independent-variance addition applies, and the returned d differs by ~13%. The \details block never mentions pool_sd; the argument is described in words in \arguments only. man/es_from_mean_change_sd.Rd inherits the same block by reference ("To know more about the calculations, see es_from_means_sd_pre_post") and has the same gap.

**Evidence.** $ Rscript -e 'devtools::load_all("."); m1p<-10;s1p<-3;m1<-14;s1<-4;n1<-40; m2p<-11;s2p<-3.5;m2<-12;s2<-4.5;n2<-50; r<-0.6 for (meth in c("bonett","cooper","morris_dz","morris_dav")) for (ps in c(FALSE,TRUE)) { res <- es_from_means_sd_pre_post(mean_pre_exp=m1p,mean_exp=m1,mean_pre_sd_exp=s1p,mean_sd_exp=s1, mean_pre_nexp=m2p,mean_nexp=m2,mean_pre_sd_nexp=s2p,mean_sd_nexp=s2, r_pre_post_exp=r,r_pre_post_nexp=r,n_exp=n1,n_nexp=n2,pre_post_to_smd=meth,pool_sd=ps) cat(sprintf("%-11s pool_sd=%-5s d=%.8f se=%.8f\n", meth, ps, res$d, res$d_se))}' bonett pool_sd=FALSE d=1.04761905 se=0.28000187 bonett pool_sd=TRUE d=0.91246299 se=0.23788996 cooper pool_sd=FALSE d=0.85634889 se=0.22733802 cooper pool_sd=TRUE d=0.76601262 se=0.19970861 morris_dz pool_sd=FALSE d=0.95742716 se=0.25735451 morris_dz pool_sd=TRUE d=0.85642814 se=0.22328101 morris_dav pool_sd=FALSE d=0.88330138 se=0.22755049 morris_dav pool_sd=TRUE d=0.78545691 se=0.20170244 Hand-evaluating the printed \deqn block for bonett: d_exp - d_nexp = (14-10)/3 - (12-11)/3.5 = 1.04761905 <- matches pool_sd=FALSE exactly pooled-baseline-SD version: sp = sqrt((39*3^2 + 49*3.5^2)/88) = 3.287805 ((14-10)-(12-11))/sp = 0.91246299 <- matches pool_sd=TRUE exactly So the documented formulas reproduce the default branch to 15 digits and are off by 12.9% on the other branch. All four documented point-estimate formulas were verified term-by-term against the code on the default branch (diff = 0.000e+00 for bonett/cooper/morris_dz/morris_dav), as were es_from_paired_t's five \deqn expressions and es_from_mean_change_sd's documented delegation with mean_pre = mean_pre_sd = 0 (d and se identical to 7 dp).

**Impact.** pool_sd = TRUE is Morris's own recommended estimator (d_ppc2), so a user who takes it and then checks the arithmetic against the printed formula finds a 13% discrepancy with nothing in \details to explain it — and cannot verify the SE at all, since the printed sqrt(se_exp^2 + se_nexp^2) does not apply either. The returned numbers are correct; only the documented derivation is incomplete. Risk is a user 'correcting' toward the documented value, or reporting the wrong estimand label in a methods section.

**Fix.** Add one paragraph to the \details of R/es_from_means_sd_pre_post.R after the four-approach block: state that the formulas shown are the pool_sd = FALSE (Morris d_ppc1 / Becker 1988) branch, and that pool_sd = TRUE replaces the per-arm standardizer with the df-weighted pooled SD and the added-variance SE with the d_ppc2 variance, giving the corresponding \deqn. Add a cross-reference sentence in R/es_from_PAIRED_MC.R's block so es_from_mean_change_sd inherits it.

**Verifier correction.** The \details "Four approaches" block of man/es_from_means_sd_pre_post.Rd (roxygen at R/es_from_PAIRED_MEANS.R:32-70) documents only the `pool_sd = FALSE` branch and never says so. Two distinct consequences, which the original finding merged: - The per-arm point-estimate \deqn's plus `cohen_d = d_exp - d_nexp` are exact whenever the two arms' standardizing SDs are equal (verified: all four methods agree to 15 digits at SD_pre = SD_post = 3 in both arms, n = 40/40) and diverge only under heteroscedasticity — 12.9% in the filed example's deliberately unequal SDs, not a general figure. - `cohen_d_se = sqrt(se_exp^2 + se_nexp^2)` is inapplicable under `pool_sd = TRUE` unconditionally, including at exact homoscedasticity (0.25861 vs 0.21687, a 42% variance difference), because d_ppc1 sums two within-group standardizer-noise terms where the pooled form carries one between-group term. The pooled variance forms exist only in @noRd comments, so a user cannot verify the SE from any user-facing page. Scope is 8 exported man pages (es_from_means_{sd,se,ci}_pre_post, es_from_mean_change_{sd,se,ci,pval}, es_from_paired_t), all of which either hold or cross-reference the single block, plus convert_df.Rd's shared `pool_sd` \item. The fix must NOT state that `pool_sd = TRUE` uses "the d_ppc2 variance": the code uses metafor-LS-pattern variances (SMCRH-robust for bonett — which its own header says reduces to Viechtbauer's published vi, not Morris's eq. 25; escalc SMD/LS on change scores for morris_dz; 2(1−r)·Var(d_z) for morris_drm; Bonett 2008 eq. 19 fourth-moment g² coefficient with an n-based leading term for morris_dav). Nor is the pooled standardizer d_ppc2 on every branch: it is the pooled baseline SD (d_ppc2, eq. 8-9) only for bonett, the pooled change SD for morris_dz/cooper (cooper also switching per-arm r to the df-weighted r_avg), and the pooled quadratic-mean SD for morris_dav (Morris's d_ppc3, eq. 12-14). The shared \arguments \item carries this same over-broad "d_ppc2" label in all 9 pages and should be corrected in the same pass.


## REFUTED — do not act on these (7)

- **[minor]** Category G (cross-row direction conflict) and E5 (cross-method direction disagreement) are dead code for measure = "rp" and "zp": a partial-correlation pool split half significantly positive / half significantly negative raises no flag at all

Working tree, R/internal_flags.R: - line 3068 (`.flag_direction_conflict`, Category G): `signed_measures <- c("d","g","dw","gw","md","mdw","r","z","rp","zp","logor","logrr","logirr","loghr")` - line 3975 (E5 block): the identical vector, `rp`/`zp` included. At HEAD both vectors read `... "r", "z", "logor", ...` with no `rp`/`zp` — which is what the finding quotes. So the quoted code is HEAD's, and the exact line numbers cited (3068 / 3975) are the working tree's, i.e. the finding mixed the two. I ran the finding's own reproduction script verbatim against the working tree (pkgload::load_al
- **[major]** V3 (correct_inputs = TRUE) nulls the point estimate along with its CI, deleting studies whose non-CI route was intact

The claim's central argument -- that V7's justification "does not transfer" -- rests on quoting only half of that justification. The half it omits (which member of the triple is wrong cannot be inferred) applies verbatim to V2/V3: when a mean contradicts its own reported interval, the mean is as likely to be the mistranscribed value as either bound. The reviewer's fix would therefore admit an unverifiable point estimate into the pool, producing a silently wrong g with a plausible SE that no other che
- **[doc]** Two user-visible default changes on exported OR routes are absent from NEWS.md  
  _why:_ The two code changes are real, but the premise of the finding — "absent from NEWS.md" — is false. The committed NEWS.md at HEAD (1347 lines) documents BOTH changes in dedicated, headed sections: 1. Line 507, "## Results change: `or_to_cor` no longer depends on which function you call": names `es_from_or_se()`, `es_from_user_crude()`, `es_from_user_adj()` as the three that shipped `"pearson"`, states all three now default to `"bonett"` matching `convert_df()`, tells users to re-run, and gives the worked number (OR 2.5, 60/140, n=200: r = 0.3463 old, 0.3205 new) plus a code block showing how to restore the old behaviour. 2. Lines 3-35, "## An `or_to_cor` row that cannot use the method it asked
- **[minor]** Transposed 95% CI is not neutralised in the pre/post CI routes: the reconstructed SD goes negative and the SMD is silently dropped to NA, contrary to the documented .ci_width() contract  
  _why:_ The literal code observation reproduces (R/es_from_PAIRED_SINGLE_GROUP.R:354-355 computes the width inline rather than via `.ci_width()`, and a fully transposed pair returns d = NA), but every load-bearing part of the claim — the harm, the scope, and the value of the fix — is wrong. 1. **The claimed harm ("the loss is silent") is false.** The reviewer asserts "the flag layer emits no [INVALID] for a transposed *mean* CI in a `dw` run." It does. `.ci_triplets()` (R/internal_flags.R:379-382) registers `mean_exp`/`mean_ci_lo_exp`/`mean_ci_up_exp` and `mean_pre_exp`/`mean_pre_ci_lo_exp`/`mean_pre_ci_up_exp`, and V2 fires on both. I ran a two-row `dw` frame with the transposed row; under **both**
- **[minor]** baseline_risk = 0 is deliberately admitted by .baseline_risk_or_na() for the logRR identity, but on the same call it makes es_from_or_se() return rd = 0 with rd_se = 0 — an infinite inverse-variance weight on a direct call  
  _why:_ The number reproduces exactly, but every load-bearing part of the claim fails. 1. **rd = 0, rd_se = 0 is the correct answer, not a degenerate artefact.** The route treats `baseline_risk` as a *known constant* and delta-propagates only the OR's uncertainty (`rd_se = |drd_dor| * or * logor_se`, es_from_stand_OR.R:555-562). At BR = 0 the control risk is 0 and Grant's treatment risk is `or*0/(1-0+0) = 0`, so RD is *identically* 0 for every value of OR — a function of a known constant with no sampling variation. Zero is the arithmetically right delta-method value under the route's own stated model. It is also the exact continuous limit of the neighbourhood: BR = 1e-6 gives rd = -1.0e-6 with rd_se
- **[minor]** No `Rplots.pdf` guard in .Rbuildignore/.gitignore: a stray plot file at the package root ships and costs an --as-cran NOTE plus a WARNING  
  _why:_ The only true part of the claim is the trivial one: R's built-in build exclusions (tools:::get_exclude_patterns()) cover `^inst/doc/Rplots\.(ps|pdf)$` but not a top-level `Rplots.pdf`, so IF such a file existed at build time it would ship and draw the "Non-standard file/directory found at top level" NOTE. I reproduced that on a minimal dummy package. Everything that makes it a *finding about metaConvert* fails: (1) The file does not exist and nothing in the repo produces it. `grep -rnE "(plot|barplot|hist|boxplot|forest|funnel)\("` over R/, man/ (examples) and tests/ returns ZERO hits — the package never plots outside the vignette. The reviewer self-discloses that the Rplots.pdf they observe
- **[doc]** cran-comments.md describes a different 2.0.1 than the one being built, and carries no check-results statement for it  
  _why:_ Two of the three legs are demonstrably false and the third is mechanically inert; the load-bearing evidence was misread. (1) "carries no check-results statement for it" is wrong. `grep -n "^## " cran-comments.md` returns headings at lines 1 (`## version 2.0.1`), 77 (`## R CMD check results`), 91 (`## version 2.0.0`). Line 77 sits between the 2.0.1 block and the 2.0.0 block, i.e. it IS 2.0.1's, and its body is 2.0.1-specific: `--as-cran` Status OK on R 4.5.1/Windows, plus a `_R_CHECK_DEPENDS_ONLY_=true` run naming "14 tests skipped because they exercise the tetrachoric route, which needs mvtnorm" (a 2.0.1 addition) and a metaumbrella revdep check "against this build". The 2.0.0 section has no
